It makes reminder the slab grave of Bronze Age with the rectangular fence in the center of square like shaped surface cover which lost its initial shape. It is interesting that the direction of this grave was pointed toward the east as the square grave was. Stone steles in front of the cover were noted in cemetery of such places as, Burkhan Tolgoi, Tamiryn Ulaan Khoshuu, Nukhtiin Am, Duulga Uul. This is a stele that is often encountered in the nomad’s funeral ceremony. In most cemeteries, due a fact that the steles were made of wood, it has not been preserved till today. Xiongnu circular grave stone mound range 5-8 meters on average. The grave’s stone mound height is approximately 40-60 cm depending on the size. Depending on the height, from 1-4 stages of stones are piled. Grave pit depth is directly dependent on its cover size. The fact that in the grave
pit there is a combination of stone and earth layers have been noted in the most of excavated graves of the Xiongnu. In most cases, the pit walls are vertical steep but there were several occurrences of up to three step walls. Unique rituals and customs may sometimes occur such as the covering the gravel pit with mud and graveling the pit bottom. Sometimes the gravel pit was purified by putting on fire. There are specifically built grave constructions of wood and stone is within the circular graves. These are classified by the followings: 1. Stone fence: A) With board coffin inside, B) With log fence and board coffin inside, 2. Log fence: A) With board coffin inside, 3. Board coffin. It frequently occurs when long flat stone made fence was put vertically in the gravel pit alone or in combination of wooden construction. As to see from the materials of the circular grave excavated recently in Mongolia, stone made boxes alone or attached with wooden construction are found occasionally. Graves with single stone fence constitutes 13% of total graves. In most cases such graves had simple interior structure with less accompanied funeral objects and some of them are referred as child graves. Log coffin graves with stone fenced frame inside make up 14% of the graves. Such graves had complicated structures but mostly
Xiongnu tombs of Duulga Uul



F
88
they belonged to women enclosed with less offering animals. Board coffin graves with stone and log framed fences constitute 12%. As this type is considered as complex structured grave, it has the deepest in matter of funeral pit and richest by funeral artifacts. In general 40 percent of the graves belong to 3 types of pits with stone fences. These graves had the most complicated construction and their grave pits are considered the deepest, and stone mounds are the biggest. These graves are found in the center, the north and the west of Mongolia. Log fenced grave is a grave that is fenced on the basis of 2-4 levels of log piled up. Sometimes there is a log or board made lid on them. The woods in a grave grew musty, and sometimes it is difficult to recognize timber fence from board coffin as it only left a sign behind. According to the materials of graves excavated recently in Mongolia, such internal construction of a grave occurred in one particular grave. This is the Tomb No.42 of Burkhan Tolgoi. The fence width of the grave is 0.9 cm and woman above 20 was buried inside. Most of graves with
timber fence had duplicate board coffin inside. Such graves constitute 14% of the total graves. These graves are big in size, complicated structure, and had many artifacts. Almost 24% of the graves have board made coffins inside, which is the most common form for internal construction of the Xiongnu common people graves. It covers almost all over the country in terms of distribution. However, it is not included in the richest type for the artifacts. There are also graves that are put directly in the depth of ground hole. The graves without any interior constructions occur 13% of all studied tombs in Mongolia. These tombs were most poor in term of accompanied artifacts, and offering animals, which belongs mostly to women. The position of deceased is one of the major subjects of the study of funeral rites. This is the important sign of determining cultural relevance of particular grave and highly ordered and never occurred any accidental cases related to positioning of deceased in traditional cultures. Different positioning differentiates depending on the dead’s
Avyn khukh uul site Xiongnu tombs



F
89
age, sex, status, and cause of death. Generally, it is one of the most stable characters of funeral practices. It is a common determination among our researchers such as Ts.Dorjsuren that “the dead person was put in recumbent position with legs and arms stretched along the body”. However, there are some unique positions. For instance, when deceased man and woman were buried in the Tomb No.6 of Burkhan Tolgoi, a left leg of the person on right was crossed over the right leg, and the arms were stretched along the body. In the Tomb No.90 of Burkhan Tolgoi, a dead woman was lied down in her front with her head cut which was on the side of her legs. In the Tomb No.4 of Morin Tolgoi, the dead one was lied down recumbent with his head pointing toward the north with his knees bend and in left side. In the Tomb No.12 of Nukhtiin Am, the dead was lied down recumbent with his arms on the stomach and legs stretched. In most cases, although the deceased was buried alone in the grave of the Xiongnu, but it occurred several times that a number of the skeletons were found in one grave. However, only one of them
is the main funeral, the other separate bones must have come from outside by different circumstances. But it should be noticed that this is different case than specific practices called secondary burial or intrusive burials. There were more than one dead people bones in 12 graves out of 103 graves in Burkhan Tolgoi. Among these examples, only 3 graves were found with 2 dead adult bones along. There is an occurrence that 2 dead children bones were found together. For other 9 occurrences, adult bones were found with bones of children. These were 5 men, and 4 women. 2 new born baby bones were found with a dead woman. It is not possible to prove whether these occurrences were prepared in accordance with specific rituals and customs or these were combined accidently. It is doubtless that children death rate was high at that time. As some researchers assumed that if they practiced secondary burials, children bones could have been mixed accidently with when carrying the bones of deceased to the grave. However, the assuming could be less trustworthy by the fact that the bones of children belong to new



F
90
born children. Also it is possible that they could have been simply mixed when they were being robbed as A.V.Davydova supposed. Another thing that could be regarded with the positioning is stone that was used to put under the dead’s head and legs as pillow. It was found in the Tomb No.66 of Duulga Uul. Similar practices were found in the slab graves of Bronze Age. Although it is predominant that the Xiongnu people were buried alone in the grave, pair burials were occasionally performed. Such graving is consisted of 2 main types. 1/. Each dead person was buried and lied down in separate holes under stone attachment of grave; 2/. Two dead people were buried along in one gravel pit. It is rare that such graves were found, and it occupies special place in the Xiongnu funeral practices. The number of offering animals accompanied with pair funeral graves is high and usually rich artifacts were accompanied with it, and all the deceased’s head were pointed toward the east. Adult men and women were buried in such graves, however, the position of man was supposed to be on the left side, and the woman was to be on his right side. As the men’s graves were richer in such pair graves and the internal construction had complicated structure, women could have been accompanied with them. A pair grave is the significant evidence that can show the Xiongnu sacrifice offering customs. Probably children and women were buried by accompanying or offering as sacrifice with the dead master. The orientation of the deceased in the grave is deeply related to custom, belief, tradition, and environment of the people who left the particular culture behind. According the Xiongnu people grave funeral positioning, the northward dominates (80%), and the east and east south were considered significant (18%) for the Xiongnu practices but the southward makes up the least (2%). In terms of chronology, eastward oriented graves belong to
relatively early period, whereas northward oriented graves are referred to late period. As the Xiongnu practiced shamanism, they believed that afterlife or spirit-world is in the north where sunsets. Therefore, their funeral direction was pointed toward the north. Among the Xiongnu people graves, there were few separate graves of children, in terms of practices, children graves were different than the adult ones. However, there is not a proved material about the Xiongnu children death ratings, the children death ratings were high based on the materials on people who lived in similar situations, and information about death ratings of Mongolians who lived in late 19th century. It has a number of reasons. 49 graves of children were found in the cemetery of Ivolga, which is 23 percent of the total graves, and 17 children graves were found from 130 graves researched in Derestuy cemetery. Children grave were recorded twice in Mongolian archaeologists research until 1990s. 20 children bones (17%) of 118 were found in 103 graves of Burkhan Tolgoi cemetery. These graves were divided into 2 types. The graves were located under the same stone mound or in connection with it of some adults. So these were named satellite graves. Most of them were looted in the antiquities and the bones were mixed and some of them disappeared so that no evidence to study the cause of death. But they could have been buried by satellite burials according to the offering sacrifice. Somewhat they belonged to lower social class, or different nationality due to a typical grave structure, fewer accompanying animals, and less funeral objects. There are also some distinct children tombs with accompanying animals and funeral belongings in accordance with practices which is similar to adults’ tombs even though they belonged to children. Some of them has their own satellite graves, which show that the children who were buried in those graves were from upper class of society. (Ts.T.)
Xiongnu tombs of Baga Gazriin chuluu site



G
91
G
ANWEI River. During the 90 CE, the united military forces of South Xiongnu and Han Empires got out of the fortification Jilu of the Shuofang administrative circuit, arrived in the mountain Zhou-ye, left animal drawn transports with their baggage and load and divided their force into two groups. Left road soldiers arrived in the North of Heyong, right road soldiers bypassed the mountain Tianshan in the right while they were going along the river Xiongnu-he. Then they turned to the South, crossed over the river Ganwei and united with the left road soldiers. At the same night, as the united force encircled Chanyu palace, the Chanyu fought them, ran the blockage and escaped. (B.B.)
GAOCHE. This is a Chinese word meaning “high cart”. Turkic speaking nomadic tribes were named so in the 3-4th centuries CE. From the 4th century, it began to be named “Tele”. They named themselves “Oguz”. The Oguz was the big confederacy of close and far related tribes. A lot of Turkic and Mongolian speaking tribes were included in the confederation. Some scientists view them as descendants of Xiongnu. They were under the administration of Xianbei, Rouran, and Turks. During the period of domination of Rouran, Oguz leader Afuzhilo tried to gain independence by leading those Tele people. They reached the river Ertysh, conquired the Semirechiye region and founded the state of Gaoche which was destroyed by Rouran people in 516. (Ts.T.)
GAODI (256-195 BCE) His real name was Liu Bang. Despite his peasant origin he founded the Han dynasty, one of the most long lasting and influent state in the history of China. (206 BCE-220 CE). During the riots out broken when the emperor Shihuangdi of Qin dynasty died in 210 BCE, Liu Bang the leader of the rebels army, joined the another rebels army commanded by
Xian You. It is Xian You played leading role in the collapse of Qin dynasty, and after the victory he had granted the principality of Han in Western China to Liu Bang. However, soon after that, they became enemies. As a result of that Liu operated more realistic and smarter policy than his rival did, he won and founded the Han dynasty. At the same time when Liu Bang founded his state, Maodun Chanyu of Xiongnu also was founding his state. Xiongnu state was accepted as the state which was equal to the Han dynasty after these two leaders fought each other in the mountain Baideng beside the city Pinchen in 200 BCE when Maodun Chanyu won a victory. (Ts.T.)
GHER (Yurt). A gher covered with a felt is a main dwelling of nomads in the steppes of Central Asia. It can be considered that a round shaped gher that is consisted of lattice wall, roof poles and felt cover existed in the Xiongnu period. In the Shiji of Sima Qian is briefly mentioned about the Xiongnu dwelling, “the Xiongnu people, father and sons sleep together in one gher (hung lu)”, and “they make the Chinese messengers to do special ritual before entering into the Chanyu’s gher (hung lu)”. G.Sukhbaatar suggested that the above stated word “Hun” is transliterated in Mongolian as “round” and therefore the gher of the Xiongnu was round shaped like a later Mongolian gher. The gher of the Xiongnu people was with a wooden column and a felt cover. In a Chinese narrative named “Discources on salt and iron” dated around in 81 BCE, stated that the Xiongnu people made dwellings from interlaced willows and covered it with felt. Uchjulu Chanyu gave rejection when the Chinese wanted to take a part of the Xiongnu land, because the asked part contained woods of gher (hung lu) and chariot production. Another evidence of that the gher of the Xiongnu had similar shape to the modern ghers can be seen from the statement of Wusun ghers, article on the western boundaries in Qian Han Shu. The Wusun is a nomadic tribe who was once the composing part of Xiongnu empire. Once, a Chinese princess, who was sent to the Wusun from the Han around 110105 BCE, grievously sung that they live in a round shaped gher (hung lu) with a felt wall. People from other lands called it felt walled because it had pieces of felt covering the lattice wall. V.S.Tsakin presumed that the Xiongnu gher is probably identical to the ghers of the modern Mongolia. The tomb No.7 of Tsaram cemetery, Transbaikalia, has yielded a birch bark object with several line drawings, performed with a spiky tool, of which one of them is a gher image. Although the image is an outline, a high lattice wall, felt cover and round shape is visible. (Ch.Ye.)



G
92



G
93
GHER-CHARIOT. The Xiongnu people had a Gher-Chariot or a gher built on a cart. G.Sukhbaatar conducted a detailed study on a proof of this that appears in Hou Han Shu. The Chinese destroyed the “Feng Weng” and gher (Hung Lu) while invading the Xiongnu. Ying Xiao (140-206 CE) explained the “Feng Weng” as a cart of the Xiongnu. As noted in the Chinese dictionary “Sho Weng”, written in 100 CE, the “round dwelling on a cart” (Che Hong Lun) expressed by the word “Feng”. The word Che transliterated as “cart”, the “Hong” as “round” and the “Lun” as “hole” and “dwelling”. Furthermore, another Chinese commentator Fu Qian (125-195 CE) explained that the “Feng Weng” is measured at hundred and twenty steps, where people could sleep and use in fights. Moreover in the big Chinese encyclopedic dictionary the “Feng Weng” is explained as a cart with four wheels, covered with cattle skin that could contain 10 people. G.Sukhbaatar suggested that, the “Feng Weng” of the Xiongnu is definitely identical to those of the Mongolian gher carts from the 13th century. K.Shiritori, a Japanese scientist, considered that “Feng Weng” is identical to the Mongolian gher cart that appear in Changchun account “Travels to the west of Changchun zi”. (Ch.Ye.)
GISCARD Pierre-Henri. French scientist, archaeologist. PhD, professor of the Institute of Archaeology, MAS. Born in 1929 in the Central France. From the beginning of the 1990s he is tightly connected with the Mongolian archaeological science and he made a significant contribution in the Mongolian archaeological science development. Founded number of joint projects and leaded many joint expeditions in different parts of Mongolia and covering different historical periods. P.-H.Giscard got his high education at the University of Marseille. He defended a PhD in humanitarian science at the same university in 1957. Since 1959 he had started working as a lecturer at the Sorbonne University of Paris and in 1962 he became an assistant professor of the École Pratique des Hautes Études, which is one of the most prestigious higher educational institutions of France. He was brought into archaeological science
by the famous French explorer and archaeologist Th.Monod in 1970. Since this time he had organized several expeditions in the Northern Africa with his teacher covering Stone Ages study to the ethnographic period. Then in 1984, P.-H.Giscard and Th.Monod founded the Institute of Deserts of Natural History Museum. Since 1990 he has been working as the director of this institute. He firstly took contact with Mongolian archaeologist in 1992 during the international scientific tour “Nomads Silk Road” organized by UNESCO in Mongolia. In 1994, his first directed joint expedition commenced its studies at Egyin Gol valley in Khutag-Undur soum, Bulgan aimag, under the auspices of the UNESCO. At Egyin Gol valley Mongolian-French researchers had continuously worked until 1999, excavating in many ancient and medieval burial site and sacrificial sites. Especially the Xiongnu cemetery of Burkhan Tolgoi is the first and only biggest cemetery that has been excavated completely and it is still draws scientists’ attention. Since 1999 he initiated the project to study the Xiongnu elite cemetery of Gol Mod and then started to work under auspices of the president of Mongolia and France. In the same year he founded EURASIAT, Center for international sciences and cultural cooperation, and still works as a president of the center. Since 2002 P.-H.Giscard have taken charge of new and interesting theme, to study Bronze and Early Iron Age burial sites in the Northern and Western Mongolia and have been successfully leading the project. Hundreds of archaeological sites were recorded during survey conducted in the Northern Mongolian in 2002 and in Bayan-Ulgy and Uvs provinces in 2004 by Mongol–French joint expedition. The collected information is still become a valuable source to understand the ancient historical sites of these remote regions. Within the scope of the Mongolian-French joint EURASIAT project many interesting Bronze and Early Iron Age burials were excavated in Alag-Erdene soum of Khuvsgul aimag in 2003, in Tsengel and Sagsai soum territories (since 2005 to date) of Bayan-Ulgy aimag, and uncovered many and valuable archaeological findings. Also the team discovered new types of sacrificial and burial features which contributed in the Mongolian Archaeology. Especially, here should be noted a special sacrificial feature that was firstly found in Tsengel soum territory of Bayan-Ulgy aimag. Moreover the first Early Iron Age Pazyryk culture burial was excavated by the project field team in 2005. In the works of Professor P.-H.Giscard have reflected many issues of different historical periods



G
94



G
95



G
96
of Mongolia. His monograph “Funeral practice of the Xiongnu”, published in 2001 in Paris, and should be pointed out here. This monograph is written in high level of theory and methodology and considered as the biggest work on the Xiongnu common people funeral practice that published in western language. He wrote several articles on Bronze, Early Iron Age, Xiongnu, and Middle Ages by himself and with colleagues and published in the western scientific magazines. (Ts.T.)
GOLDEN DECORATION. Tear drop shaped decoration. These pair of decoration in have tear drop shaped turquoise inlays at the center, in the edge have small round drop shaped convex pattern around and have crooked ring at back, which suggest that these are used as pin decorations. Size: 0.6×0.9 cm, weight 1 gram. Close analogues have been unearthed from Tomb No.2 of Cheremukhovaya Pad’ in Buryatia, Russian Federation, and from Tomb No.23 of Noyon Uul. Buryat archaeologist P.B.Konovalov, considered that this was used to be a brooch. Another interesting fact is that similar decoration has been found in Tilliya Tepe tombs in Afghanistan, which located over 3000 km west of Gol Mod site. This decoration is originated from Bactria which had high developed jewelry production centers. Similar things can be seen on these items found in different places but since they are not completely identical, which aloows to make a suggestion that these have had a unique origin. Some tear drop shaped items found in Tomb No.20 of Gol Mod was similar to the patterns on the big decorative object that found in Tilliya Tepe. These are the evidence that the Xiongnu not only had a relation with the Han in the south but also communicated with the far western countries. (Ch.Ye.)
GOLD DIADEM. A gold diadem owned by a nobleman, was found from the grave of the Ar Tsaidam, Khyanganii Khoshuu, Ikh Zuu aimag, Inner Mongolia. This diadem comprises of two parts: Upper and Lower. On the upper part, there are two sheep and two wolves in fight are embossed on the round gold decoration with spiral type of pattern. Also, a falcon set with a turquoise on it or neck and head parts, is displayed like it is flying. And
the sets around the falcon eyes are likely to show it is alive. The falcon’s curved beak expresses it is a bird of prey. Its feathers are rather delicate and the claws are stubby big. Its height is 7.1 cm. On the lower part of the diadem, there are a semicircle gold decoration, tied on to the rim with 12 pieces of gold thread. On the circles, some animals including horse, sheep and tiger are artificially displayed together with the lace/knitting/woven pattern. The diadem is 16.5 cm in diameter, 60 cm in length and 1202 gm in weight. This diadem is considered to be a wonderful masterpiece of ancient nomadic art and an invaluable heritage as its artificial imagery and delicate made. (B.A.)
GOLDEN PENDANT (pin ornament). There were uncovered 6 golden pins in the gap between wooden vault and head part of the coffin of the Tomb No.20 excavated in 2002-2005 by Mongolian-French joint expedition at the Gol Mod site of Khairkhan soum, Arkhangai aimag. This floral shaped pin with four leaves of which edges decorated with small convex drops and there are turquoises incrustation installed
Golden diadem, grave of the Ar Tsaidam
Golden decoration of the Noyon Uul
Golden pendant of the Gol Mod site



G
97
in sockets of each leaf. At the center another turquoise incrustation is installed in a socket and decorated with the round convex drop pattern as well. Back of the pins is flat, and there is crooked ring at the center. Size: 1×1 cm, weight 3 grams. According to the skin remains on the back of some pins these were probably attached to some kind of object. (Ch.Ye.)
GOL MOD tombs. Xiongnu period elite and common people mixed cemetery in Mongolia. This cemetery is located in 35 km southeast of the Khairkhan soum center of Arkhangai aimag. There are over 420 burials both of elite and common people were identified in the cemetery. Discovered by Mongolian archaeologist Ts.Dorjsuren in 1956, in following years he had excavated 26 small satellite burials and one elite tomb but he could not
accomplish his excavation due to sliding of sand. The burials delivered many interesting items such as weaponry, labor tools, horse equipment, decorations in different kind of metals, earthenware ceramics and bronze utensils. Also the excavation results clarified some features of the burial structure and funeral ritual of Xiongnu. Later, he published results of the excavation in his book “The Northern Xiongnu”. MongolianFrench joint expedition has been carrying out investigations at Gol Mod since 2000. The Gol Mod site has larch forest at south and east and occupies 400 hectare area in the north of Buhun ridge. There is an open grassland valley of Khanui river in the west. Over 420 burials were included on the map and three
Golden decoration with turquoise inlay, Gol Mod site
Gol Mod site excavations in 2000



G
98
elite tombs and 15 satellite burials were excavated between 2001-2008. The first excavated elite tomb is Tomb No.1, which was left unfinished (finished in 2000-2001) by Ts.Dorjsuren in 1956-1957, second is the Tomb No.20 (excavated in 2002, 2004-2005), which was the biggest among the elite tombs, burial uncovered at a depth of 18.3 m, and the third is the Tomb No.79 (excavated in 2001-2002), which is smaller than other two tombs. Even thought that these tombs had been looted in ancient times, many interesting items to demonstrate the funeral ritual, economy, trade, and cultural relations with other
countries were uncovered. Bronze ornamented pieces of ceremonial chariots, golden and silver decorations of the coffins, felt rug, silk, bronze and iron utensils, and jade objects could be emphasized. Especially, some items related to the Middle Asia are new evidences to establish the cultural relation of the Xiongnu with other countries. (Ch.Ye.)
GOL MOD-II (Balgasan Tal) tombs. Xiongnu period elite and common people mixed cemetery in Mongolia. The Mongolian-American joint project “Khanuin Khundii” field research team discovered
Artefacts of the Gol Mod site
Artefacts of the Gol Mod site, Tomb 20



G
99
this cemetery in 2001 in the territory of Undur-Ulaan soum of Arkhangai aimag. 452 burials are identified at this site and 30 satellite burials of Tomb-1 and one satellite burial of Tomb-106 have been excavated so far. This cemetery is called Balgasyn Tal or Gol Mod by local herders, and in research literature called as Gol Mod-II site. It is surrounded by Elstiin Gol, a headstream begins from the Bugat ridge, in north; by Bugat river in southeast; by larch forest grown on loose sandy soil in west. The site lies 2.2 km from west to east and 1.3 km from north to south and occupies in total 3.5 square kilometer area. It is very interesting that the site has identical name with Gol Mod cemetery in Khairkhan soum and located in only 100 km west from the first. Probably it is the one of evidences that the region of Khangai ridge was the center of the Xiongnu empire. There were 107 big elite tombs, of which 98 are rectangular with entrance passage dromos and 9 are circular with entrance passage as well. Also there are 81 round holes without any surface stone covers. These are considered as burials too. The big burials with entrance passage have satellite burials on east and west sides but the number of satellites differ from 1 to 28. There are 264 satellite burials have been recorded. First excavations at Gol Mod-II site carried out in 2002. 30 satellite burials that located in line
east of the tomb 1 have been excavated completely so far. (Ch.Ye.)
GOLD BELT-DECORATION. Found from the 140th grave in Keshenzhuang, Shaanxi province in 1956. This decoration was casted in and embossed with bronze. Two bare-chested men in only long trousers, leaning on each other and wrestling with a grip from the hips and their tied up-two horses with a saddle and bridle, are displayed on the decoration. (B.A.)
GOLDEN DECORATION OF NOBLEMEN’S
BELT. This object was discovered in 1979 from the Tomb No.2 of Xigoupan, Zuungar banner, Ih Zuu aimag (modern Ordos city), China. Several plaques with different images were discovered from this graveyard. Among them there are two golden belt
Gol Mod site excavations in 2000
Gold belt-decoration grave of Keshenzhuang



G
100
buckles with the depiction of fighting tiger and boar. The images of animals were well crafted in relief. There is a rectangular loop for hanging from belt on the back side. The buckle is measured 13 cm long and 10 cm wide, and it weighs 291 gram and 330 gram. There is rope pattern on the edge of this plaque. (B.А.)
“GRAVE STUDY OF THE XIONGNU”. Article published in Nei Menggu kaogu yu wenwu in 2009. Author Shan Yueying. He gave detailed information about the Xiongnu grave’s feature, and its funeral practices by using the Xiongnu grave materials found in Mongolia, Transbaikalia and Northern China. Furthermore, as for the Xiongnu grave chronology, he made classifications of early history (Empire Period), and late history (North/South Xiongnu period) based on the Xiongnu grave structure and funeral practices, and in terms of distribution, he divided the following 3 regions; from Baikal lake to the middle of Huanghe river; Balkhash lake and “Doloon” river; upper stream of Huanghe or Ordos region. According to Shan Yueying, the Xiongnu Empire period related grave organization, obsequies are peculiar and the Xiongnu which migrated or moved to the west, and the Xiongnu funeral culture that followed and submitted to Han China was affected by the particular state or country and lost its culture peculiarity gradually. (Ch.S.)
GRAVES OF BALIKUN. Xiongnu period cemetery of common people in Mongolia. Discovered in the east part of Nanwang village (near Hami), Balikun Xiang, Xinjiang. In this cemetery during 1981-82, more than 100 graves were excavated and reported in a particular report volume. The tomb of Balikun were covered with a circular stone mound and had in the center depression without stones. As for the internal structure of the graves, they can be divided
into two different types: one has a rectangular hole as well as another one has a rectangular hole with a plate stone chamber. The archeologists have suggested that these graves date back to the period of 3600-3000 years ago and probably belongs to Xiongnu. (Ch.S.)
GRAVES OF KHULUSTAI. Xiongnu period tombs in China. Located on the east bank of the Khulustai River middle flow, in 15 km in the southeast of Khangain Gobi commune, Urag Dund banner, Bayannuur aimag, Inner Mongolia. In 1979, after the information from local herdsmen which found some pieces of bronze tools here, researchers from Inner Mongolian cultural heritage studies institute came to investigate them. They excavated here three Xiongnu graves and published a report. Tombs are all touched in ancient times, and actually they had square earth mounds and had many findings. In the Tomb No.2, the body was put lied in his back oriented toward the north. Near the head of the body, there were bones and heads of 27 horses. After cleaning and taking them all, the researchers found the decorations of bridle, bronze ax, bronze knife, bronze bell and some equipments of horse chariot, as well as a pottery near the head of the body. (Ch.S.)
GUA DOV Settlement. Xiongnu period settlement in Mongolia. Discovered in 1920s by the O.Jamiyan, the director of the Mongolian Institute of Fine Letters. He looked for the historic place named Gua Dov (Brown Hill) which was mentioned in the shaman’s worshiping call. He was gathered some information from a shaman called Gombojaw and found it after long exploration. Located in the place named Tsagaan Aral, west bank steppe of Kherlen river, in the territory of Bayanjargal sum, Central aimag. In 1952, archaeologist Kh.Perlee had initiated the excavation of this site. Gua Dov had a
Nomblemen’s golden belt decoration of Xigoupan
Roof tile of Gua Dov settlement



G
101
square rampart sized 367×360 m built of pressed earth, with actual height of 50-70 cm, width of 3.5 m. There are traces of doors which were both in the south and north of the citadel. There is a trace of a small house built inside the citadel. The hill named Gua Dov is located in the center of the rampart has 1 m high, 45 m wide and 56 m long. The remains of the building with many posts were discovered during the excavation by Kh.Perlee. The building had been built on specifically pressed earth socle. Findings uncludes bricks, roof tiles, and decorative square shaped bricks and lower part details of a wooden posts. Among them, there were some artifacts of daily life purposes as cracked pieces of Chinese pot and a metal button. (Ts.T.)
GUCE River. In 90 BCE , 140000 soldiers of the Han attacked Xiongnu by 3 roads, and Chanyu crossed over the river Guce with his soldiers and had been waiting for the enemy on the south bank of the river. Later, in 58 BCE, during civil war in Xiongnu, Huhanye Chanyu had come to the north of the Guce river on the road advancing to the west in order to attack Wuyanjudi Chanyu (60-58 BCE). (B.B.)
GUIFANG tribe. This tribe was mentioned in historical sources from the 11th century BCE between the periods of time of Yin-Shang (16-11th centuries BCE) and Zhou period. Mongolian scientist Togtohu following Wan Guowei concept “Guifang people were the ancestors of Xiongnu” and explained the meaning of hieroglyph Guifang as follows: “The first hieroglyph Gui is a figure of a person with a sword/knife in his hand; the second hieroglyph fang is a figure of a person with a large board frame in his neck. Thus, the word Guifang means “a scared slave with a sword”. After his explanation, Guifang
means “warmonger tribe or defeated slave-tribe”. In addition, if the Guifang people were ancestors of Xiongnu and the Xiongnu language was part of Altaic para-language, there are words “khuyag” or “khuyagt sain er” (meaning “armor”, “armored warrior”) similar in pronunciation with gui in contemporary Mongolian language. He also explains that Guifang is the land of armors or the land of armored people. In contrary to it, the viewpoints of some researchers considering the meaning of the hieroglyphs as “devil’s land” according to the Chinese dictionary entry where the Gui as “soul left after someone died, or devil”. Cultural relic and findings related to the Xiajiadian cultural lower period (1500-1000 BCE) have been spread in the south and north of the river Liaohe river. There is high probability of their connection to the Guifang, because they have been mostly found around Ulaankhad and Ordos of Inner Mongolia. Thus, researchers including Wan Gowei proposed that Guifang were the ancestors of Xiongnu by supporting the idea of that Ordos was the main territory of Xiongnu people. Researcher J. Ganbold views that if Guifang had been truly connected to Xiajiadian culture they must have been people who had settled way of life in part and used domestic animals such as pigs, sheep and cattle for a living. That they stopped being mentioned in historical records since the beginning of the 9th century BCE, indicates that they must have been dissolved into Chinese states and other nomadic tribes. (S.U.)
GUILUHOYI (Beginning of the 4th century CE). Son of Mugulyu, the legendary ancestor of Rouran. He was also called Charugui. He is considered as the founder of the Rouran state around 330 CE. He also founded the powerful confederation of tribes which united a lot of tribes. However, during a period of his reign, Rouran people paid horses, livestock, fox skin and sable skin to the Tabgach Wey dynasty for tribute. The fact that his descendants governed Rouran state from generation to generation, truly indicates that he founded state and became an emperor. (Ts.T.)
GUJIRMEG tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Buriatia. Located on the right bank of the Djida river. In 1900 the Russian anthropologist Yu.D. Tal’ko-Gryntsevich discovered it and identified 30 burials. In 1949 A.P.Okladnikov and P.Ph. Tugutov performed a brief survey, and in 1961-65, P.B.Konovalov and R.P.Tugutov visited this site and collected some artefacts: bronze arrowheads, knives, stone and glass beads, pottery and fragments of golden foil. (Ts.E.)
Plan of the Gua Dov settlement



G
102
GULI WANG. The name of one of the 4-horn wang (prince) of Xiongnu. There were 2 positions named Left Guli wang and West Guli wang. The position of Left Guli wang was one of the most authoritative position among other 4 horn wang to be succeeded by the oldest son of Chanyu. It happens that some scientists name this post as Luli wang instead of Guli wang. (No.B.)
GUMILEV, Leo Nikolayevich (1912-1992). Soviet-Russian scientist, historian and ethnographer. He worked in the field exploration expeditions in Sayan, Pamir and Krym regions in 1930-1934. In 1946, he graduated from the Faculty of History of the University of Leningrad and was admitted to the doctor’s program in the Leningrad branch of the Oriental Study Institute of the Academy of Sciences of the Soviet Union. In 1948, he defended his doctor’s dissertation in history and began to work as a research fellow in the Museum of Ethnography of Peoples of the Soviet Union. In 1961, he defended his doctor of science dissertation entitled “Ancient Turks”. In 1974, he defended his doctor of science degree in the field of geography. His main works include “History of Xiongnu people”. AST, Lyuks, 2004; “Xiongnu in China: Wars of China against steppe nations through 3 centuries”. – Moscow, 1974; “Gunni”. – Soviet historical encyclopedia. – T.4. 889-891. (M.Ts.)
GUNCHENG CHANYU (161-126 BCE). Xiongnu Chanyu, the grandson of Maodun and the son of Laoshang Chanyu. At the beginning of his 35 years reign as emperor, the Xiongnu Empire was at its peak. As soon as he came to the throne, he made a request for trading with the Han several times. As he did not succeed in it, he sent his troops from 158 BCE to attack Chinese border regions, as a result of that he won the war several times, in 152 years of BCE, they established the peace and executed the “Peace and Marriage treaty” once again
as during his father and grandfather reign period. Under this treaty, they took a large amount of tributes from Han each year. In 133 BCE, warmonger emperor Wudi of Han declared the war which lasted for several decennies. After the incident of Mayi fortress, it occurred that there was no longer any ground for two countries to have mutual understanding. Therefore, Xiongnu was involved in was inevitably. Guncheng Chanyu had been resisting successfully superior military forces of Han until he died in 126 BCE. (Ts.T.)
GURVAN MODOT UUL tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. Located in 45 km to the west from the center of Battsengel soum, Arkhangai aimag. Tombs are on the south slope of the small rocky hill named Gurvan Modot, which is on the west bank of a small stream flowing through the Shivert valley, the left part of the Tarkhint Uul, located in the North Tamir river basin. Along with Xiongnu tombs, some kereksur, deer stones, slab graves related to the bronze ages, ritual stone fences related to the Turkic periods have been identified in this area. The Bronze and Iron age study group of the Mongol-Soviet historical-cultural relics research expedition excavated 5 Xiongnu tombs in 1978 and 1 tomb in 1981. Artifacts consists of potteries, metal knife, a bronze belt buckle and a well designed metal buckle. D.Nawaan, the explorer of these tombs made an interesting conclusion by claiming that the vase made of clay dug up from tomb No. 13 is the finding related to the nomadic tribes of the pre-Xiongnu period. (G.E.)
Pottery of the Gurvan Modot Uul



H
103
H
AN dynasty (206 BCE-220 CE). The second empire in the history of China. During this dynasty the Chinese culture flourished and developed higher than previous Qin dynasty period. The first historian of China, Sima Qian wrote a great work “Records on the Grand Historian” or Shiji in this period. At the same time, “Fu”, a classical form of poem got popular and “Yue Fu”, a collection of music began to be written from the songs ranged from ceremonial to ordinary. A lacquer industry, founded in the period of Shang, reached its peak of development while the silk was exported to Europe as a main merchandise of foreign trade. Also paper was invented, the time began to be measured with water and sun clocks, the calendar began to be issued. All the culture, come out in the period of Han, is said to be Chinese culture and Chinese person is called to be Han person in north Chinese or Mandarin dialect. The main neighbor was Xiongnu Empire and both of them were established almost
in the same time. Firstly, both of them were equal in power but gradually, Xiongnu was defeated and collapsed. (Ts.T.)
HANDCRAFTING AND HOME MANUFACTURING. Despite its brilliant development Xiongnu manufacturing could not reach as an independent sector of economy in comparison to manufacturing economy of a settled country. However, it doesn’t mean that all nomads did essential things for their daily life at home, e.g. were self-sufficient. They made dairy products, accessories and decorations of domestic animals such as a horse. In addition, they made felt and clothes from livestock wool and skin on their own at home. However, intricately designed decorations, mobile houses-ger, chariots and carts and gold and silver accessories were made by qualified artisans, carpenters and ironsmiths at the order of people or in order to be sold. There are strong grounds to a put forward a hypothesis concerning that specially qualified hand crafters-artisans made some objects, widely used and distributed in Xiongnu, such as bronze kettle, intricately designed gray vases and pots made of clay and horse’s bits and bridles in large numbers. It was not possible for everyone to make all these accessories and decorations, tools, for making them
Textile piece of the Noyon Uul



H
104
obviously required high skills and heavy trainings. Mongolian historian G.Sukhbaatar was the first to suggest that “These qualified artisans needed to be distinguished from common shepherds, and their performance must be named - home manufacturing”, which is considered to be a more applicable and optimal term. Mongolian archaeologists Ts.Dorjsuren and Z.Batsaikhan studied the methods, knowledge and skills necessary for making those things revealed among archaeological findings in detail. Buryiat archaeologist P.B.Konovalov briefly mentioned about the methods used for making those items when he studied artifacts unearthed from the Xiongnu graves of Transbaikalia. The conclusions about that Xiongnu settlements and villages had been hand crafting centers and necessary items for nomads had been manufactured there have some solid foundations. Although there are very few written sources and information to be used for the Xiongnu home manufacturing study, there quite abundant archaeological material. Different types of accessories and decorations of domestic animals including horses, arms, accessories, ironware and pottery vase, clothes and details of a belt made of different materials are found in Xiongnu tombs and settlements. Construction materials, agricultural instruments, tools, everyday life household items
are often found. It is quite possible to conclude that those aforementioned 2 types of monuments fully demonstrate the primary artifacts that indicate that Xiongnu household culture has many types of items. The artifacts discovered by archaeological excavations were made of iron, bone; wood, stone, animal skin, silk and woolen fabric. There must have been artisans, silversmiths, carpenter, artisans who deal with felt, processed skin, tailor, embroiderer, as well as someone who makes appliqué. (Ts.T.)
HEAD DRESS OF THE PRINCESS. This spectacular finding was unearthed from the tomb No.4 of Xigoupan cemetery in the territory of Bor Tokhoi cooperative, Zuungar banner, Ikh Zuu aimag (present day Ordos) in 1979. The head dress made of gold, shell, bead, and precious stones and found from
Textile piece of the Noyon Uul



H
105
the grave of a woman. Unfortunately, at the time of discovery the excavators did nor realized the form and purpose of the separate pieces of the head dress and not well reported. (B.A.)
HEDOHAN (end of 4th c. – 391 CE). Leader of Rouran. He was leading Rouran tribes of South Gobi and was defeated by Tabgach army and killed in 391. From this period Rourans moved to North West and allied with High Carts-Tele. Some researchers believe his son is Shelun. (Ts.Т.)
HELIAN BOBO (381-425 CE). Given name at birth is Lui Bobo. He is the founder of South
Xiongnu state Xia and the founder of the capital city, Tongwangcheng. He was known to be a skillful and firm leader. His father Lui Weichen was one of South Xiongnu influential leaders of nowadays Ordos and north part of Shanxi. After the dead of his father, relatives and brothers in hands of Tabgach tribe, Lui Bobo survived with support of few and overcome hardship during his early years. His later life was rather influenced by his father-in-law, leaders of Sianbei, Mo Yigan. Around 407 Lui Bobo lived in the court of Later Qin state, one of the powerful state of its time. During those years, his talent was spotted by the emperor Yao Xing and was given a title of Duke
of Wuyuan. And he was appointed to be a military leader against Tabgach. When Lui Bobo realized that emperor Yao Xing was going to surrender to Tabgach Wei state, he called for military uprising, assassinates his father-in-law and other nobles, and declared of founding his state. He named the newly founded state Xia and called himself the King of Heaven. Since then Lui Bobo started war in three directions: against Tabgach Touba-Wei, Later Liang and South Liang. His main military power was Xiongnu elite cavalry with still keeps its ancient military tradition. Until 413 Lui Bobo had traditional nomadic mobile residence and did not have capital of his state. However, due to the need to have strong fortress to protect from Tabgach attacks, he founded grand city with strong fortress called Tongwangcheng, i.e. City to gather all, in the south of today’s Ordos. During his time, his city was known to have the best fortress and had been used for many centuries. Also he replaced his surname Lui with Helian. Lui is a surname created when Xiongnu Chanyu reached peace agreement with Han Empire by wedding their offsprings for symbolizing that Lui family of Han is the connection between two states. Bobo believed there was not such connection and would rather want to see connection with higher power and created surname “Divine” (Helian). In 417 he conquered Chan’yan, a capital of Later Qin state. The same year won Qin state in the war for Chan’yan and proclaimed himself as an emperor. During his last years he became a ruthless leader and died in 425. (Ts.Т.)
HEQIN. Lit. Peace and Marriage treaty. Specific type of peace agreement was one of the approaches of Xiongnu foreign relations with its neighboring states. With strengthening of Xiongnu empire, Chinese Han dynasty was seeking ways to enter into peace agreement, knowing that there was no way to defeat Xiongnu with military force. Thus in 198



H
106
BCE, Han initiated first time the peace treaty “Heqin” with Xiongnu acknowledging it to be the strongest empire in Central Asia. G.Sukhbaatar assume that a part of the letter written by Xiao Wendi of Han to Laoshang of Xiongnu in 162 BCE, which says “Archers in the north of Great wall should follow the Chanyu order, the land of those who wear clerical attire should be under my order... Han and Xiongnu are strong states neighboring peacefully with each other” is the first part of the peace agreement of 198 BCE. There are several issues that two parts of the agreement reached consensus on, namely, Princess of Han was to be given to Xiongnu Chanyu to be wedded and become a queen; Han state is to send presents, consisting of silk, textile, wine, liqueur and food; parties are to accept that “Xiongnu is strong enough to neighbor Han state” and on the other hand, Xiongnu was to fulfill its duty of not attacking Han state”. This agreement was valid for more than 200 years, with some breaks but was constantly enforced by two sides during this time period. (Na.B.)
“HISTORICAL MEMORIES ON THE ORIGINE OF HUNS AND TURKS”. (Joseph de Guignes. Memoire historique sur l’origine des Huns, et des Turcs). Author Joseph de Guignes. 1748 One of the first books in European languages on the history of nomadic peoples, including Xiongnu. In this book, the author wrote the narrative succession of Chinese emperors, and some information noted in Chinese sources on Xiongnu. He emphasized records such as Xiongnu was firstly recorded with a name “A cruel and fierce slave” in Chinese sources in 318 BCE, the construction of the Great Wall began in 221-210 BCE, Xiongnu was divided into two parts: Southern Xiongnu and Northern Xiongnu in 52 and 48 BCE, Southern Xiongnu joined China and Northern Xiongnu left here in 89 CE, some of them migrated to Europe. Also he says about complicated relationship between Xiongnu and Han, their regular fights and history of Xiongnu began in 209 BCE when the Empire was founded and so on. On the basis of Chinese sources he made comprehensive listing of Xiongnu Chanyu, beginning with Tumen, the first Chanyu followed by the others, including the year of their coronation and period of their reigns. He stated about Turks that they were inhabited near the Altai Mountain ranges and named “Tou-Kiu” by the Chinese. He assumed that Xiongnu were the ancestors of Turks. (Ch.E.)
“HISTORY OF EARLY AND MEDIEVAL AGE CITIES AND SETTLEMENTS IN REPUBLIC OF MONGOLIA”. Author Kh.Perlee, Ulaanbaatar, 1966. The author analyzed all accessible materials
concerning the ruins and cities related to the Early and Medieval Age in Mongolian territory and compared with the historical sources. It was not only the first monograph for this subject in Mongolia, but also stayed for many decades the only book on it. The author emphasizes the role of archaeological sources on the study of ancient settled life in Mongolia, which is traditionally considered the classic region of animal husbandry and nomadism. Kh.Perlee reviewed the importance of settled life, agriculture and manufacture in nomadic societies and states, and made differentiation and characters between Mongolian nomadic urbanism and settled countries urbanization. Moreover he made an essential conclusion that the urbanism, agriculture and handicraft were harmonically connected in ancient Mongolian society and it was impossible to exist without one of these components. (Ch.A.)
HOLOTOVA-SZINEK, Juliana. Born in 1976 and graduated from Charles University in Prague, Czech with a major of a Sinologist. She got a Master’s degree with a thesis “About Issues on the Relationship between Han and Xiongnu in 2nd c. BCE” at the University for Oriental Studies, Paris in 2003 and Doctor’s degree with a thesis “The relationship between Xiongnu and Han (on the basis of historical sources and archeological findings)” at the University Sorbonne-IV, Paris, in 2008. Since 2003, she has been working in a Mongol-France joint expedition on the study of Gol Mod site, Khairkhan soum, Arkhangai aimag. She has worked on some scientific booklets as a co-author and written about 10 scientific articles in French, Mongolian, English and Czech. (Ch.Ye.)
HONEYCHURCH, William. American archaeologist and anthropologist. Was born in California, in the USA, on April 6, 1966. He defended Bachelor’s degree at the University of California in 1989, Master’s degree at the Michigan University at Ann Arbor and Doctor’s degree in Anthropology at the same university in 2004, with a thesis “The Emperors and Warriors in Inner Asia: A Regional and Spatial Analysis on The Political System and Relationship of the Nomads”. W.Honeychurch organized and lead Mongol-American joint project “The North Mongolia” in the Egyin Gol River basin, Khutag



H
107
Undur soum, Bulgan aimag, in 1997-2000. During this projects his team implemented full scale archaeological surveys first time in Mongolia. During 2003-2008, he organized and directed the next survey-excavation project in Baga Gazriin Chuluu, Delgertsogt and Adaatsag soums, Dundgovi aimag. The project team discovered over 1500 monuments and excavated around 100 monuments dated to the periods from the Stone Age to the Middle Age. He wrote two monographs in Mongolian language, on the basis of the archeological research results, “The Archeological Research on Ancient Nomads’ State Structure” (Ulaanbaatar, 2004) and “The Archeological Research in Dundgobi Aimag: The Baga Gazriin Chuluu” (Ulaanbaatar, 2010). So he became one of the first foreign scholars in archeology, who wrote his works in Mongolian language. W.Honeychurch is also author and co-author of numerous scientific works on Mongolian and Nomads’ archeological research, such as “From the Steppe Road to The Silk Road: The Exchange between the Inner Asian Nomads and Ancient Regions” (2010), “Political groups: The Social Layer of Mongolian Ancient Nomads” (2010), “Mongolian Archeo-Geography” (2010), “Linking the Change of Stone Construction with the Change of the South Asian Politics” (2009), “The North and Central Asia: The Cultures in Steppe, Desert and Forest Zones” (2008), “The Collapse of Ancient Xiongnu and Its Social Activities” (2008), “The State on The Horse: The Allies of the South Asia and Flourish of the Empires” (2006) and “The Exploration and Settlements of The North Mongolia: The Structure of the Organization of Nomadic Regions” (2006) and so on. His research works are to reveal the politics and socio-economy of the nomads inhabited in the land of Mongolia, based on some archeological facts. (Ch.A.)
HORSE HARNESS. We have had inadequate information or fact concerning with the horse harness of the Xiongnu, noted in written sources but more archeological findings from their graves commonly, those can tell much about it. In fact, nomadic animal husbandry developed after the adoption of horse, and the enhancement of the horse harness inevitably depends on the progress of the animal husbandry. The history of the horse harness has a period which is described as a period of the Xiongnu. Bridle, crupper, breaststrap and bit had their own design or style, while saddle and stirrup were contrary. See the
scientific articles about the Xiongnu’s horse harness in each of above mentioned. (Ts.T.)
HOU HAN SHU. The History of Later or Eastern Han dynasty. One of twenty four official Chinese historical records. An official historical narrative of Eastern Han which existed in 26-220 CE. Compiled by Fan Ye, a historian of Liu Song dynasty, which was established after the collapse of Eastern Jing in 5th century CE, using a number of earlier histories and documents including Shiji and Han Shu (Book of Han). It contains a full chapter which covers of Xiongnu. However, as content, it is almost same to two earlier records above mentioned in many subjects. It displays some big changes occurred in Xiongnu society and especially the change of ruling tribe. (Ts.T.)
HU. Chinese generic name of the nomads. Translated into foreign languages with a meaning of “barbaian” or “savage”. Also a specified name of Xiongnu. But since Xiongnu Empire, also other nomads were commonly named like this. As Xiongnu was named “Hu”, the Xiongnu eastern neighbors were called “Eastern Hu” or “Donghu”. From the points of some researchers’ views, “Hu” is a Chinese pronunciation of the name of a tribe “hor” in ancient Mongolian. (Ts.T.)
HUCUQUAN CHANYU (2-3rd c. CE). The last Chanyu of the Southern Xiongnu. In 188 CE, the Han court broke the law of the East Wing privilege and raised West Tuqi Wang Yufuluo as Chanyu. Consequently, the Southern Xiongnu raised, forced him away and raised Left wing Xiubi Guduhou as Chanyu. In a short while, he died and his younger brother Hucuquan was reigned as Chanyu. We have had no more information concerning with Hucuquan Chanyu in 189-215. In 215, Cao Cao deceived him into coming to the palace and took his right of authority. Then he appointed West Tuqi wang Kuyubi as a puppet Chanyu. In 216, Cao Cao abolished southern Xiongnu as the State and divided them into five tribes. There is only fact about his later life that he granted Cao Pi a large gift, with his gratitude of defeating Han Dynasty in 220. In this way, Xiongnu and Han Dynasties arose and collapsed at almost same times in history. (Ts.T.)
HUHANYE CHANYU (58-31 BCE). Xiongnu Chanyu. After the death of Wuyanjiudi Chanyu in 60 BCE, a internal disorder risen up in Xiongnu



H
108
and five Chanyus reigned at the same time. In history, this period was named as “The troubles of five Chanyu”. Then it ended with a quarrel between brothers Huhanye and Zhizhi for the throne. In 54 BCE, Zhizhi captured the steppe under his control and Huhanye forced to shift Han Dynasty. In the hostile way towards each other, Xiongnu was separated into two parts and the brothers did not agree with each other. So Xiongnu Empire was getting weaker. There was a group who thought that one of the brothers would rule the Empire and dominate others again. On contrary, most of the lords and officials thought that Xiongnu could not be re-established in this situation that Han Dynasty had already got more powerful. So Xiongnu should join Han Dynasty for a while for the reinforcement. Drawing on these proposals, Huhanye Chanyu decided to receive the second proposal to submit Han Dynasty and reinforce his nation. In 53 BCE, Huhanye Chanyu sent his son to Han Dynasty as a hostage. In the following year, Huhanye Chanyu left for Chang’an, a capital city of Han and personally called upon Xuandi Emperor of Western Han. Xuandi Emperor met him friendly and formally. He told him to accept his proposal to became a vassal of Han Dynasty, and granted him a new golden seal with a writing “Xiongnu Chanyu Seal” tied with a yellow silk string. Due to the meaning of the writing on the seal, the Chanyu received the seal. Because Xiongnu rule and the power of Chanyu were still in the seal as content and a word “Seal” in the writing was not turned into “Sign” which was always used by the local governors of Han Dynasty. Even though Xiongnu was weakened caused by the State disintegration and joined Han Dynasty, its power, state rule and aspiration were still as before in people’s mind. So Han Dynasty was aware not to make or declare them a vassal of Han directly. The Emperor of Han sent some officials to make a greeting to Xiongnu Chanyu respectfully in distance of his palace, when he came to Han Dynasty at first. And when he visited the palace, he was offered a higher seat than ministers, dignitaries, lords and military commanders, and the state seal, hat, belt, outer or under clothes, sword with a precious set, knife, bow and arrow, cart, 15 horses, 20 jing (a jing is 596.8 gram) of gold, 200.000 copper coins, 8.000 pieces of silk goods, and 6.000 jing of cotton as gifts. Huhanye Chanyu went back from the palace of Han Emperor after his visit lasted a month, he was guarded by 20.000 Chinese
cavalry. According to the Emperor’s decree and Huhanye Chanyu’s request, he was settled down in a place named Shouxiancheng in the north of Ordos, that is to say, near the fortification line built by Han, in the south of Xiongnu land. Han Emperor sent a large amount of millet and rice to the people who accompanied Huhanye Chanyu and decided to supply them every year. At that time most of Xiongnu were under Zhizhi’s rule, but he moved from the Khangai Mountain and Orkhon Valley into the Semirechiye region, to avoid the sudden attach from Huhanye Chanyu who was supported by Han. Some Chinese envoys who visited the Zhizhi Chanyu palace died from an unknown reason in 48 BCE, and the Chinese side thought that Zhizhi killed the messengers, and bore a grudge and dealt with Zhizhi Chanyu with an antagonistic view. After this event, Zhizhi Chanyu was not able to move back to their homeland. On the other hand, Huhanye Chanyu was no longer worried about any attack of Zhizhi Chanyu. As a result, some Xiongnu officials and ministers submitted the Chanyu to move back to the homeland. Then they came back to the Eastern Mongolia again in 47 BCE. Huhanye Chanyu was settled down in the Bogd Khan Mountain or the Bayan-Ulaan Mountain or between them because they were all not close to the domain of Zhizhi Chanyu and the Eastern Tuqi wang domain was Huhanye Chanyu’s birthplace. Although formally Xiongnu was protected by China, the homeland in Mongolia was ruled by Huhanye. But Zhizhi Chanyu controlled the western countries as Semirechiye, Dingling, Wusun and Kangjiu and so on. Some tribes like Wuhuan which was dominated before, still gave duties and taxes to Huhanye Chanyu with fur and cloth. In 37 BCE, Zhizhi Chanyu was beaten and killed by Han Dynasty in his newly built city, in the Talas River, the Semirechiye. It gave Huhanye Chanyu a chance to be the only influential leader in the homeland. In 33 BCE, he invited one again to Chang’an city, called upon the Emperor of Han Dynasty and received a duty to protect the north border of the Han from other nomads. In this time, he proposed to ask a princess of Han Dynasty and to improve the relationship. The Emperor received his proposal and gave him one of his concubines named Wang Zhaojun as a yanji (queen in Xiongnu). She gave a birth of son named Yitujiyashi to Huhanye who would become later the West Zhijo wang of Xiongnu.



H
109
In 31 BCE, Huhanye Chanyu died and his son was raised a Chanyu with a title Fujulei-Jodi. He married Wang Zhaojun according to the levirate tradition and had two daughters. Apparently, Wang Zhaojun died before Fujulei-Jodi and now there is a big earth tomb that is known as one for Wang Zhaojun, near Huhhot, Inner Mongolia was built. Actually, the Palace of Huhanye Chanyu was in the east of Mongolia especially in the zone of the Bayan-Ulaan, the Bogd Khan Mountain and Chanyu and his wives and relatives were buried in the Noyon Uul, near Kharaa river. During the time of Huhanye Chanyu, Xiongnu Empire was recognized as a vassal of Han Dynasty, but Xiongnu still kept its independency indeed. After some researchers’ view, at that time, Xiongnu Empire was re-established and dominated all the land as before. (Ts.T.)
HULIU Khan (410-414). Emperor of Rouran. G.Sukhbaatar reconstructed his name as Khokhluud. A younger brother of emperor Jarun. Reigned as an emperor in 410 and granted an honor “Aguu gol”. He dominated Heshuegu tribe in the north and Bilichen tribe in the east. In 414, his cousin Buluzhen conspired with others and captured him. Then sent him to the North Yan capital and made him kill. (Ts.T.)
HULUGU CHANYU (reigned 96-85 BCE). A great Chanyu of Xiongnu. A son of Chedihou Chanyu. The 9th great Chanyu of Xiongnu. During his reign, Wudi emperor of Han Dynasty intended to mount an army in order to take his revenge for being defeated by Xiongnu in 101 and 99 BCE, although he was weakened and got older. The emperor Wudi and the general Li Guanli decided to multiply so-called “Heavanly horses” brought from Fergana for the armored cavalry and to mount an attack on Xiongnu. In 90 BCE, Li Guanli attacked with the cavalry of 140.000 soldiers, but failed and surrendered to the Xiongnu himself. As a result of this army defeat, Han Dynasty was not able to continue its dominance. Xiongnu Empire got more powerful and became the leading force in Central and Eastern Asia. For the battle of Wudi Emperor against Xiongnu and the aggression of Western Han, Wudi Emperor could not get a profits from the horses brought from Dawan and solve the problems profitably although they had strong bow and much more soldiers than Xiongnu. Since the army was defeated, Han Dynasty had no force to protect the frontier and weakened soon. Nevertheless, Xiongnu
did not want to take this advantage and to destroy Han Dynasty completely, even they did not attack the frontier regions and pillage there. According to L.N.Gumilev, before his death, Hulugu Chanyu could make known his intelligence and far sight which all the well educated statesmen were not granted. Instead of making unprofitable attack which can make more acute the revenge and pillages, he wanted to send a letter to Wudi Emperor in 89 BCE to re-establish the frontier trade and the “The Marriage Treaty/Treaty of Perpetual Peace”. So he wanted the Emperor of Han Dynasty to send his daughter and 10 dan (a dan is 103.5 liter) of best alcohol, 50000 hu (a hu is 51.8 liter) of rice, 10000 rolls of silk per year. But it is unclear that Wudi emperor sent a reply or not. Wudi, the Emperor of Western Han who waged a war, brought China down and made Xiongnu weaken, died in 87 BCE. Hulugu Chanyu was an intelligent, talented and brave emperor who managed to save his country from more powerful enemy. (Ts.T.)
HUNA. The name of Xiongnu who invaded India. It is related to the Hionit tribes who settled down in Middle Asia. At the time when the tribes of Xiongnu origin settled in Middle Asia, were disintegrated and splited to Iran, India and Europe. Huna tribe dominated some parts of the Northern India and established a state in Bharatvarsha. They apparently started their attack from Oksus or near the Amudarya River. They named themselves “Hono”. In 455, Skandagupta emperor of Gupta Empire beat the attack of Huna but by the end of 5th century, the Gupta Empire had been collapsed due to the dominance of Huna in the Northern India. In around 470-475, the Huna captured Kandahar of the Northern India and controlled Kandahar and West Penjabi region entirely during Toramana emperor. In 528, Huna was beaten off by the army of Malva of Central India and pushed to the north. In 565, it was collapsed by the allied force of Sassanid Persian and Kok Turks. (Ts.T.)
HUNTING. The hunting played an important role in nomadic life. Although it was defined to be an auxiliary economy, it was mainly used to provide people with some essential products like meat, leather, horn and bone. Particularly, their meat consumption depended on the hunting. So the hunting of Xiongnu is inevitably considered to be not an ancillary economy.



H
110
There was no any conflict between pastoral and hunting economies, but a supplementation to each other. Also horse, commonly used for the nomadic lifestyle was a basic factor of the hunting. On the other hand, according to the research, the hunting was a kind of training in military studies as well as combat. So the hunting can be conceived to be a regular activity in regard to the considerably militarized society of Xiongnu and the battle or war which was a normal state of life for more time in history of Xiongnu. The facts from the sources also say about it. As an example, we can find in historical records the hunting made by Maodun Chanyu together with 100.000 soldiers. Like other nomads, Xiongnu people began to ride horse and made hunting from their childhood in order to become warrior heroes. A source makes known that the children hunted bird or mouse with a self-bow while looking after sheep. And the teenagers hunted fox or rabbit for their food. And the adults became warriors because they had been trained and had had strength to shoot the bow through this way. Traditionally, they made a life in not only looking after sheep but also hunting and training themselves for combat, in peacetime. This is a definite and perfect example of nomadic lifestyle in brief. Han Shu mentions about their special territory for hunting, with a few words that “This Mountain Yinshan is rich in herbs, forests and wild animals. Here, Maodun Chanyu prepares bow and archery and started to attack. This is a place like a resource of wild animals”. Also about hunting’s made by Wuwei Chanyu, Huliu Chanyu have been noted in some historical sources. The products from hunting not only supplied domestic consumption but also were exported on account of a merchantable quality. There is a fact that the Xiongnu traded with the Chinese with furs of animals including sable, fox and badger. We have been finding out some facts on hunting from some archeological findings. So we can conclude that the hunting played an ancillary role for their life from the calculation of bone of
wild animals such as deer, antelope, fox, steppe polecat and rabbit, is merely 7.5 per cent of animal bone, dag out from the Ivolga settlement. Moreover, there are some bones of animals like wild sheep, wild pig and sable as well as birds like wood-grouse, discovered by archeological excavation. Also some bones of four wild asses, one antelope, one gazelle and one bird have been discovered from the Xiongnu graves in Ilmovaya Pad’, Transbaikalia. The main tool or weapon for hunting was apparently the bow. Several types of bone arrow heads, found from the Xiongnu graves are hypothesized to be used for hunting because such kind of bone arrow heads does not hurt the fur. (Ts.T.)
HUNYU TRIBE. The tribe lived in the North of China during the legendary period of Chinese history. In accordance with a historical record that “Huandi forced away Hunyu to the north” in “The Bibliography of Five Emperors”, in “Record of the Grand Historian” compiled by Sima Qian, the earliest historical source, Hunyu tribe lived 4.000 years ago. There are no any other facts related to them in other source materials. (S.U.)
HUO QUBING (140-117 BCE). General of Western Han. He began military career from his childhood. His early career facilitated by his familial relationship with famous general Wei Qing. After appointment of the commander of the light cavalry, he attacked Xiongnu and mounted several successful battles. He was famous for capturing the statue of a Golden man placed in the Palace of Xiutu Wang of Xiongnu. In 120 BCE or when Han Dynasty launched his largest attack on Xiongnu, he commanded the army together with Wei Qing. He besieged the Xiongnu Left Tuqi wang and captured or killed more than 70000 soldiers. After this victory, he was granted an estate with ten thousand households. It talks in the historical records that although he was a brave general, he never took care of his soldiers. He died of a plague at the age of 24. (Ts.T.)



I
111
I
CHISE CHANYU (126-114 BCE). Xiongnu Great Chanyu. Grand son of Maodun Chanyu and the son of Gunchen Chanyu. Ichise Chanyu reigned during the reign of the Han Wudi, when he broke the Peace and Marriage Treay, and destabilized peaceful harmony between two nations. Ichise Chanyu reigned during one of the most aggressive periods in the Chinese history, but honorably resisted Chinese aggressions and keep Xiongnu independence. To come to the throne, the East Luli wang Ichise, a younger brother of Gunchen Chanyu had to stage a coup against the next in line to the throne, the East Juku wang Yuibi. Yuibi faced Ichise in a battle, was defeated, and fled and submitted to China, where Wudi gave him a princely title She-an-hou, but in few months after that Yuibi died. A few years before Ichise Chanyu’s enthronement, in 133 BCE, the Chinese captured Ordos, which the Xiongnu held for the previous 80 years from the time of the Tumen Chanyu, after a decade-long occupation by the Zhao state. The loss was a heavy blow for the Xiongnu, and the war flared up. Just as Ichise Chanuy ascended the throne, in the summer of 126 BCE, Xiongnu with several tens of thousands cavalry raided Dai province, killed regional governor Gong Ji, and captured thousands of people; in the autumn the Xiongnu also raided Yameng, and also captured up to 1.000 people. The next year, 125 BCE, Xiongnu again raided Chinese provinces. The West Juku wang, incensed that Chinese Court took away Ordos and built Shuofang, a few times attacked the borders of China; and when entered the Ordos, plundered Shuofang, and killed and captured a multitude of officials and people. In 124 and 123 the Chinese took initiative to attack Xiongnu Xiongnu lands to the north, penetrating hundreds of li. During these campaigns, thousands of Xiongnu people were killed and captured, but the Han troops suffered also heavy losses. Han commander of the second detachment Zhao Xin surrendered when he was surrounded. Zhao Xin was a minor Xiongnu prince, who switched to the Han. Once again among his tribesmen, he married the sister of the Ichjise Chanyu and became his chief adviser on matters related to China. On the advice of Zhao Xin, Chanyu moved his headquar
ters to the north of the Gobi Desert, to avoid direct Chinese assaults, and to lure Chinese troops deep into the steppes to destroy them. The struggle for the Ordos thus concluded with a complete victory for China. After the loss of Ordos, Gansu corridor region, as the main commercial artery of the period was the lost to Han overwhelming forces. Eventhough these heavy loses Xiongnu keep their main forces and avoid complete defeat by righteous strategy of Ichise Chanyu. (Ts.T.)
IL’MOVAYA PAD’ tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery with elite and common people tombs in Buriatia. Located in the territory of Republic of Buriatia, Russian Federation, near the Kiakhta town, on the state border between Mongolia and Russia. The cemetery has over 300 tombs and represents one of the biggest funeral site in Transbaikalia. Near this cemetery two more important Xiongnu funeral monuments are located: Sudji cemetery in the east side, Cheremukhovaya Pad’ cemetery in the north side. Ilmovaya Pad’ cemetery was discovered by Yu.D.Tal’ko-Gryntsevich in 1896, who had conducted the excavations in this site in following years until the 1900. He had excavated 32 common people tombs and begun the excavation of a square terraced elite tomb, which he had no chance to accomplish. After his descriptions the square terraced tomb had 15×17 m mound, 1.25 m high, 2.75 m deep depression with 4 m diameter in the center and stone path entrance in the south side. He excavated the tomb in a surface of 1.5×3 m, and in 8 m depth the excavation was stopped due to earthfall. In 1928-1929, G.P.Sosnovskii continued the excavation of his predecessor and studied 2 elite tombs numbered 40 and 128. Tomb No. 40 had rectangular stone mound measured 12×11 m in the surface, 0,6 m deep depression at the center. In 6 m depth unearthed a log chamber of 3.35 m long, 1.3 m large and 0.42 m high. Inside the chamber found a timber coffin. The coffin was coated by variegated silk, which fixed by gilded bronze quadifolie headed nails. Some objects as gilded bronze horse openwork decorations, white jade plaque with carved patterns, small bird sculpture made of stone, pieces of iron, and fragments of large ceramic pot, bone chopsticks and spoon, worked antler were found in this grave. G.P.Sosnovskii suggested that these tombs belong to 1st century CE, on the basis of close analogues of Noin Uul tombs and East Han tombs. In 1970-1975, Buriat archaeologist P.B.Konovalov from Buriat Social Sciences Institute excavated another one elite tombs numbered



I
112
54. The results of the excavation was published in 2008 in Russian and English. (Ch.Ye.)
INTELLECTUAL CULTURE. Culture of Xiongnu is the subject of wide range understanding and study. Intellectual culture comprises their language, writing, decorative art, religion and poetry and musical art. According to Chinese historical records, it is possible that Xiongnu had own scripts, and used it for state affairs. After establishing Xiongnu State, one of the biggest achievements for culture was the writing, however they considered no writing before establishing the state. According to Chinese sources, Maodun Chanyu exchanged letters with Western Northern and Chinese officer Zhonghan Yue “trained the close servants of Chanyu to count and register cattle and tribute”. In the “Discourses on salt and iron”, the Chinese narrative written in the 1st century BCE, “in Xiongnu many officers carve on the bone, board of wood and use all lower and upper officers”. It demonstrates that Xiongnu were used different writing that Chinese script. Xiongnu had shamanic and Tengri or Eternal Sky belief and in some part the Buddhism spread apparently. There are many facts of Tengri belief, for example, they convened once a year and arranged the celebration to sacrifice the earth.
One of important part of celebration is the singing the song. There is a long song myth of Xiongnu recorded in the historical source, which it reveals their linkage to the Mongols. Musical instruments as bamboo jews harp and reed pipe gave us the information of Xiongnu musical culture. Moreover, there were many traditional customs as January, May, September, preferring the dog and snake days, funeral customs for aristocrats and common people, funeral meal and sacrificing ceremonies. Xiongnu political and diplomatic tradition and organization of the army reveal their high developed political culture. (No.B.)
ISHJAMTS Nyambuu (1928-2000). Mongolian historian. Born in 1928, in Khuvsgul soum of Dornogobi aimag. In 1936-1943 studied at Sainshand middle school, in 1944-1946 at the preparatory course of the Moskow State University, in 19471953 at the History Faculty of the same university. In 1950’s he was one of the first national professional historians, with Russian, Chinese and English languages. In 1949



I
113
1950 worked at the Lecturs Committee of the Institute of Science, in 1953-1961 at the Department of History of the Institute of Science, in 1961-1968 at the Institute of History of the Mongolian Academy of Sciences, in 1968-1972 at the Institute of Oriental Studies of the Academy of Sciences of USSR, in 1972-1991 worked at the Insitute of History, then at the Institute of Oriental Studies and Institute of Foreign Studies as the senior research fellow, scientific secretary and the director. From 1991 until mid 2000 he served as the Director of the American Study Center of Mongolia. In 1962 he defenced his Ph.D in Beijing University, and in 1972 his Sc.D degree in Moscow State University. In 1991 he was elected to the Academy member of the Mongolian Academy of Sciences and in 1996 honered as the Emeritus Scientific Fellow of Mongolia. Scientific interests of Academician N.Ishjamts ranges widely from Mongolian history to the American and Chinese study. His works on the study of Xiongnu origin, social and economic characteristics and their relationship with Mongols significantly contributed on Xiongnu study and keeping their actuality until now. (No.B.)
“IVOLGA cemetery (Иволгинский могильник)”. A monographe on Xiongnu archaeology in Russian. Author A.V.Davydova. Published in Sant-Peterburg in 1996. The Ivolga cemetery is located in the vicinity of Ulan-Ude city, 440 m to the North-East of the Ivolga settlement. The author completely excavated 1950’s this cemetery consisted of 216 ground tombs with no stone mounds. This monograph presenting the results of this research in extensive form. Author gives the general description, planning and structure of the cemetery and the detailed analyses of artifacts. According to the author the cemetery belongs to the occupants of Ivolga settlement and presenting the funeral rite of sedentary population of Xiongnu period. (Na.B.)
IVOLGA settlement. Xiongnu period permanent habitation site in Buriatia. Located in the bank of Ivolga stream, in 16 km from Ulan-Ude, the capital of Buriat Republic of the Russian Federation. Discovered in 1920’s by local archaeologists leaded by V.V.Popov. The settlement excavated by A.V.Davydova. Total surface of the settlement is equal to 7.5 hectare, which walled by four ramparts with three successive ditches between them. These ditches were formed by the construction of ramparts and have also defensive purposes. The depth of ditches is actually 2-2.5 m and the height is 0.5 m, total
