width of defensive stripe is measured as 35-38 m. Eastern part of the rampart if eroded by Selenge River but it is possible to measure it as 348×216. Principal entrance to the settlement was placed in the middle south rampart. The interior of the fort was densely built up, in only 20 percent of the excavated area were found the remains of 54 half buried dwellings. These dwellings presenting as a rectangular semidugout dwellings to a relatively small width of 2.8 to 6.85 m long, on 0.55-1.1 m depth, with an entrance on the south side. Mud walls of dwellings had pillar framework. Supporting pillars supporting gable roof, composed of beams and boards of coated with clay, covered with poles, bark, straw, ash, and on top of all this - turf. In the northeast corner of each dwelling had closed furnace built of stone slabs. From the oven, along the western and northern walls, passed a chimney constructed from flat stone slabs and fragments of large pottery, acting as a heating duct, similar to Kan, very common heating system in the Far East. Different from semi-dugout dwellings, a large building unearthed in the central part of the settlement, where supposedly lived his ruler. It was built on the hard-packed ground in the center of the settlement, which measured by 13×11.5 m, there has been large, thick adobe walls measured by 1.11.4 m, a large oven, carefully compacted ground. As a result of research carried out on the site, revealed the remains of the craftsmen of different specialties. Existed on this site the workshops of ceramic and metal production, blacksmithing, and various crafts, including high developed bone crafting. Besides the crafting the residents of the settlement were engaged in agriculture and animal husbandry. Only on the explored part of the settlements opened more than 600 holes of various sizes and depths, covered with lids and sheds intended for the storage of food, like grains, meat, fish, etc. In the settlement they raised primarily the millet, but were also used to culture barley and wheat. Agricultural implements as coulters, sickles, hoes, found during excavations, indicate a high level of agriculture. Residents of the settlement were kept dogs of various breeds and bred cattle, sheep, goats and pigs. Used in food also meat of wild animals caught in hunting very commonly. In 440 meters from the settlement, more close to the confluence with the Selenga and Ivolga River, found burial site of the settlement population, which has been excavated totally in 1956 by A.V.Davydova, with 216 burial ground including 244 people. (Ts.T.)



I
114
“IVOLGA settlement (Иволгинское городище)”. A monographe on Xiongnu studies in Russian. Author A.V.Davydova. Published in Sankt-Peterburg in 1995 as a volume on series of monographs on Xiongnu archaeology monuments of Transbaikalia. The book covered the complete excavation reports and extensive analyses of Ivolga settlement excavated by the author. According to the author the demographic composition of Ivolga settlement was quite complex, consisted of native Xiongnu, immigrants from China and other captive sedentary population. The general conclusion of the book is the Ivolga settlement had complex economy, combining nomadic pastural economy with the sedentary agricultural and handicraft activities. (Na.B.)
“IVOLGA SETTLEMENT AND CEMETERY – THE STUDY OF SOME ISSUES OF XIONGNU ARCHAEOLOGY”. A monograph on Xiongnu archaeology study in Chinese. Author Pan Ling. Changchun, 2008. This monograph is based on the materials of the Ivolga settlement and cemetery the most complete and important Xiongnu monument in Transbaikalia. The author made a comparative study of the Transbaikalia and Mongolia Xiongnu materials with the Chinese materials from West Han to early East Han periods. She noticed the influences of Han period material culture in Ivolga settlement structure and organization and construction materials, Ivolga cemetery funeral goods. Moreover she made the extensive typological and chronological analyses of openwork belt plaques discovering from Early Iron age and Xiongnu period monuments. (Ch.S.)



J
115
J
EW’S HARP. The Xiongnu rich musical culture consisted of different musical instruments and types of singing and dancing. According to historical records Xiongnu were being played instrument, throat singing, musical and dancing. Some Chinese source indicates that they had a drum, lute; whistle a six stringed fiddle (Kunhou), blower, and transverse flutes. However these musical instruments have not left until today. The only Xiongnu musical instrument recovered so far is a Jew’s harp found at the burial site of Morin Tolgoi of Altanbulag soum in Tuv aimag. It is made from carved bone and is 12.5 cm long, 1.4 cm wide and 2 mm thick. It has a tongue that is 9.7 cm long, 5 mm wide in the middle, and 2 mm wide at the end. There is a small hole on the bottom of it, and, on two side is carved to curve the edge. Similar kinds of Jews harps, made from metal, have been found in many locations
from Japan in Asia to England in Europe. The modern accordion is basically a set of mouth harps with bellows for moving the air. Thus the “khel khuur”-jew’s harp found at Morin Tolgoi is the one of the most ancient musical instruments in the world and ancestor of aerophones. (D.Ts.)
JILUOSHAN MOUNTAIN BATTLE. Also called Ikh Bayan battle. Major battle between Xiongnu Empire and East Han dynasty. In summer of 89 CE, in the territory of modern Mongolia, East Han and their ally South Xiongnu unified army military force launched a campaign against North Xiongnu. Chanyu lead the North Xiongnu army himself and Dou Xian, Deng Hong lead the army of Han, Tuntuhe Chanyu lead South Xiongnu army. According to Chinese records, Xiongnu army consisted of 213.000 men, allied force consisted
of 8.000 chinese infantary, 30.000 South Xiongnu cavaliers and 8.000 Qian auxiliary forces. In 70 CE due to deterioration of North Xiongnu economic and social conditions, many tribes joined South Xiongnu and in same time Xianbei tribes started to attack them from east. In 87 CE Yilu Chanyu of North Xiongnu was defeated by Xianbei and was killed. After this, 58 tribes with around 200.000 persons moved to South Xiongnu. That year there was severe drought and increased number of grasshoppers destroyed pasture causing famine, according to historical records. With these hard conditions, North Xiongnu entered into war in Jiluoshan Mountain. In 89 CE allied forces entered the depth of the territory of North Xiongnu through three routes. Chinese army entered through Gobi Altai mountain pass and defeated North Xiongnu Chanyu and who run away to the west. Chinese leading general Duo Xian commanded the main force towards Khangai Mountain and reached Yanjian Mountain. Here historian Ban Gu inscribed a stele about war and victory and erected it. The war ended with complete overtaking of North Xiongnu. After the war North Xiongnu Chanyu asked for peace agreement, but Tuntuhe Chanyu
of South Xiongnu disagreed and attacked North Xiongnu in the following 90 CE to destroy completely North Xiongnu. During this attack North Chanyu palace was invaded and state seal was taken, wives and children were captured. In 91 CE general Dou Xian attacked North Xiongnu again and captured Chanyu mother, killed 5.000 soldiers and pushed North Xiongnu army over Altai mountain range. Since then there is no record of Chanyu of North Xiongnu. In 93 CE remaining 100.000 men from North Xiongnu submitted to the leadership of Xianbei and took the peoples name Xianbei, that was the story of end of North Xiongnu. (Ts.Т.)
JIN MIDI (134-86 BCE). Xiongnu origine prominent official of Han Dynasty. He was born in family of Xiongnu Xiutu wang and was his heir apparent. During attack by Huo Qubing in
“Khel khuur” -Jew’s harp. Morin tolgoi tomb



J
116
121 BCE, Xiutu wang responsible for western border of Xiongnu with another powerful aristocrat Hunye wang were defeated by the Han army and son of Hunye wang, number of officials and “Golden man” the idol that was in the palace of Xiutu wang were captured. This angered Chanyu who thought those wangs betrayed him and ordered to execute them. Two wang decided to run away from capital punishment to surrender Han, but Xiutu wang changed his mind. Then Hunye killed Xiutu wang and submitted to Han Empire with their people. Midi, son of Xiutu, his mother and sisters were taken to the palace of Han Emperor as prisoners. Midi started to work in the palace as a stable boy. Wudi Emperor noticed Midi as being strong in nature, loyal, diligent and hardworking personality and promoted him to be a supervisor of the stable. Later Wudi who heard about “Golden man” idol found from the palace of Midi’s father gave him a surname Jin, meaning Golden. When Midi’s mother died, Wudi got her portrait painted and hang the painting “Xiutu queen” in Royal Palace. This shows how influential Midi became in Han court. His two sons became Wudi’s internal guards. In 88 BCE, Jin Midi saved life of Wudi from from assassination, which further improved his reputation. In 87 BCE, when it became clear that Wudi dying from illness, Midi was chosen to be the regent of young prince. Jin Midi died in 86 BCE. Seven generations of his descendants served West Han Dynasty. (Ts.Т.)
JIN SHU. One of 24 official historical records of China. History of Jin (265-420 CE). The record contains special part on South Xiongnu history. Tang Prime Minister Fan Xianling compiled
this record based on ancient records and official papers in 648. Tang Taizong Li Shimin wrote some of the biographies in the 1, 3, 54, 80th book of the record. Apart from Jin history, this record contains history of foreign 5 states and 16 states. There is one sentence in Jiehu language recorded in this book, which was the language of one of the Xiongnu tribes. (Ts.Т.)
JUQU. Surname of one of the tribes of South Xiongnu. This surname was taken by king Mengxun of Liang state, South Xiongnu when he found his state, who thought he was a descendant of Juqu wang of Xiongnu during Maodun Chanyu reign. Records about Juqu wang from Xiongnu period are rare. It says in Shiji record that 24 great kings appointed their own Juqu wang. (Ts.Т.)
JUQU MENGXUN (368-433 BCE). King of North Liang state, of South Xiongnu. A founder of Juqu clan of South Xiongnu. He is one of the initiators of rebellion against Late Liang state in 397. He was named King of North Liang from 401 CE. In his youth he was known to be a skillful leader, but later became a cruel ruler. (Ts.Т.)
JUYANG Lake. Modern Gashuun and Sogoo lake in Inner Mongolia, in China was called also Juyang in ancient times. During 8th c. CE there was one big lake in that lowland. Chinese geagraphers think there were two big lakes of 85-62 km long and 40-30 km wide during Xiongnu period. A large city, later know as Khar Khot, was founded on the shore of a river that feed into East Juyang Lake. (B.B.)



K
117
K
ANG IN-UK. Korean archaeologist, specialist on the Siberia and NorthEast Asian Bronze and Early Iron age archaeology. Born in 1970 and graduated from Seoul University Archaeology-Ancient Art History department. In 2000 completed Ph.D dissertation on the theme “Chinese bronze daggers from 1st millennium BCE (problems of typology and chronology)” at the Institute of Archaeology and Ethnography of the Siberian Branch of Russian Academy of Sciences. Associate professor at the Pukyong National University. Main research interests of Kang In-uk is the Bronze age archaeology of the North-East Asian regions, but covering also ancient nomads, including Xiongnu period archaeology research. His works related to the Xiongnu study includes, “Western Migration of North Xiongnu and the Xiongnu relics in Xinjiang” (Central Asian Studies, Vol. 13, 2008), “Xiongnu and East Asian relationship based on the archaeological materials from 4th c. BCE to 1st c. CE” (Central Asian Studies, Vol. 15, 2010). (G.E.)
KEBINEN (172-235 CE). One of the Xianbei leaders emerged after Cao Cao defeated Wuhuan. Kebinen in the Chinese famous historical novel entitled “The Story of Three Dynasties” was described as an ally of Cao Cao. In this period of time, he was granted the title Chanyu of Xianbei. He was an honest, talented leader and a man of principle. Most Xianbei people followed him. Under Kebinen’s command there were 100.000 cavalrymen. After he was killed in 235, a confederacy of Xianbei was split. (Ts.T.)
KESHENJUAN tomb (Tomb No.140). Xiongnu period cemetery of common people in Mongolia. This tomb is located in the territory of the Keshen village of Chan’an city, Shaanxi province, China. The excavation was conducted by the Institute of Archaeology of the Academy of Science, The People’s Republic of China during 1955-1957. The rectangle shaped tomb pit was put in the direction of the north east 10o. The tomb pit was 1.9 m long and 0.6 m wide. The deceased person was laid on his back. It is unknown whether there was a coffin or not. Burial goods include 2 bronze buckles of a belt with the figure of 2 wrestling men on each of them, 2 bronze circular decorations, 1 gold circle and an iron knife which were put beside the deceased person’s were unearthed. A dwelling house of the Warring states period was also revealed from below the tomb. Based on it, this tomb has been considered to be the Xiongnu tomb related to the period of times from the latter period of Warring states (476-221 BCE) to the middle period of West Han dynasty (202 BCE-9 CE). (Ch.S.)
KHANAN KHAD tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. Located in the west of the Khiidiin Davaa mountain pass, on the north bank of the Egyin Gol River, Khutag-Undur soum, Bulgan aimag. In front of the monument, two roads pass to the east and west. Along the roads, there are graves, put a side by side and look like loose stones. There are eight Xiongnu graves identified. This site was discovered by a Mongol-America joint expedition in 2000 and excavated one grave placed on this side. (G.E.)
KHERMEN TAL fortress. Ruins of three fortresses are located in Khermen Tal valley, in 20 km to the north from Ugiinuur soum, Arkhangai aimag. Since 1950, Mongolian archeologists have had recorded these ruins. In 2000, Z.Batsaikhan,
Plan of the Khermen Tal fortress



K
118
took measurements of these ruins and carried out test excavation. The ruins are lined from west to east and there are ruins of three fortresses. The western fortress wall is 1.5 m tall, measured by 400×452 m; there are entrances to the north, west and east, the width of the entrance is 10 m. Inside the fortress, there are four remains of construction: a palace ruin is in the middle, which measured by 35×25 m and there are four other remains of smaller constructions to its south west and south. The central fortress ruin is located 150 m to the east from westernmost ruin and it is measured by 430×290 м, it may have entrance in the west and south side. The width of the entrance is 10 m. The remains of construction inside the fortress are taller than the ones found in the west fortress ruin, which its central structure measures by 25×35 m. There is also one building ruin to the south and 3 remains of construction to the south west also there is another remain of construction by the east wall. The much smaller remain of the easternmost fortress ruin is almost invisible and it is located 270 m from the central fortress. Most probably this fortress had only one entrance to the west and there is a central building ruin measured by 25×20 m. There are three other smaller remains of construction to the south of the central one. Even though no artifacts are found to date the ruins the fortresses, in 2004 students from MNUE found small shards of pottery from Xiongnu period. Not far from here, 4-6 km away there are two hills, named Burkhan Tolgoi and Emeelt tolgoi with burial sites for ordinary people of Xiongnu period. Archaeologists consider also the state of preservation of the fortress ruin to date the site to Xiongnu period. (Ts.Т.)
KHIRGIST KHOOLOI tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. This monument located in the north of the road between Tsogt-Biger in distance of 3 km in the north of Tsogt soum center, Gobi-Altai aimag. It is named Khirgist Khooloi by the locals due to its landscape, comparatively flat valley between low mountains in front of the Altai mountain ranges. The burial place is situated at the flat slope in front of the low hill, the west end of the valley and comprises three sections which are isolated by little hills. This cemetery has 97 graves in total: 31 in the east, 51 in the central and 5 in the west sections. The graves in the east
and central sections are not too far from each other but the west section is located very far from both of them. A shallow ravine formed by flood, makes the isolation from the south of the east section to the west through the graves. The site was discovered by an expedition for the study of Xiongnu monuments in 1986 and excavated only five graves of the central section in the same year. According to the research report, each grave has a circular external construction with no stone at the center, and the smaller ones are 5 m and the bigger ones are 12 m in diameters. As some grave stones of the external construction were used for the building, there are only marks of the stones remained on the surface soil. (G.E.)
Plan of the Khirgist khooloi site
Some artefacts of the Khirgist khooloi site



K
119
KHOLTOST NUGA tomb. Xiongnu period isolated tomb in Mongolia. Located in distance of
20 km in the southeast of Khantai bag center, Khutag Undur soum, Bulgan aimag. A low land in the east of the scarp, close to the north bank of the Egyin Gol River is named Kholtost Nuga and it is a small burial place having over 10 slab graves, dated to the Bronze Age. In the middle of them, there is a Xiongnu grave and it was excavated by a Mongol-France joint expedition in 1999. (G.E.)
KHUDGYN TOLGOI tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. Located in distance of 23 km in the northwest of the Battsengel soum center, Arkhangai aimag. The graves are placed in 7 km in the north of Khoid Tamir River, on the terracing hill, the Khondiin valley, where small streams run. A couple of hills in the east isolate the valley. It is comparatively flat and open in the south, surrounding by high mountains in the north and northeast. There are identified 360 Xiongnu
graves, placed in a row from north to south and in groups. The graves in the southwest and north are considerably big and their stone constructions look like huge from the far. But ones of the small graves are made of a few small stones, stuck out from the soil and placed in circle. The external stone constructions of the graves are commonly in circle but ones of the small graves are rectangular. The diameter of the external stone construction of the big grave is 20-25 m while of the smaller one is 3-5 m and of the middle ones is 10-15 m. In the east edge of the Xiongnu graves, there is a big kereksur with central stone mound and surrounded with square enclosure. The expedition of researching the monuments of Bronze and Iron Ages discovered this site in 1981 and excavated 6 of them in the same year. Later or in 1987, the expedition of researching Xiongnu monuments excavated 2 and in 2001 the expedition of a Mongol-Korean joint project “Mon-Sol” excavated 4 tombs. (G.E.)
Plan of the Khudgyn tolgoi site



K
120
KHUDYAKOV, Yuli Sergeevich. Russian archaeologist, researcher of Central, Middle Asian and Siberian ancient nomadic cultures, leading specialist of ancient weaponry of nomads. Leading research fellow of the Institute of Archeology and Ethnography, Siberian Branch, Russian Academy of Sciences, Head of the Department of Archeology and Ethnography, University of Novosibirsk. Doctor of History, professor. Scholar and pedagogue, one of the representatives of Siberian modern archeology and is recognized as an expert of nomadic weaponry studies in the world. Corresponding member of the Academy of Natural Sciences of Russian Federation, a vice president of the Russian Union of Orientalists, a prize-winner of Academicians A.Altimishbaev and A.Tabaldiev, an honored foreign member of Kyrgyzstan’s National Academy of Sciences, an honored scholar of the Sciences, an honored professor of National University of Kyrgyzstan, an honored professor of the Institute of Oriental Language and Culture, and a Danaker Orden-winner of Kyrgyzstan. In addition to them, he is a member of the International Institute for Nomadic Civilization of UNESCO in Ulaanbaatar and an Honored Doctor of the Institute of Archeology, Mongolian Academy of Sciences. Yulii Sergeevich Hudyakov was born in Medvejka village, Krapivii district, Kemerovo province, Russian Federation, on December 8, 1947. After he finished primary and secondary schools and did a military service, he entered the faculty of Humanity, the University of Novosibirsk. Being a student, he joined some archeological expeditions, exposed his talents and started his scientific career under the supervision of well known archeologists A.P.Okladnikov and A.P.Derevyanko. As soon as graduating the University, he continued to study for doctor’s degree and defended Ph.D degree in History with a thesis “The Weapons Of The Kyrgyz In The Yenisei In 6-12th c. CE” in 1977 and Sc.D degree in History with a thesis “The Art of War of South Siberian and Central Asian Nomads (2nd c. BCE-10th c. CE)” and was granted the title Professor in 1991. Yu.S.Hudyakov has conducted field
archeological researches in the regions of Southern and Western Siberia, Mongolia, Tajikistan and Kyrgyzstan for 40 years. When he studied for doctor’s degree, he already directed his first archeological expedition in 1975. He wrote around 30 books including monographs, collective works and course books and more than 1000 scientific articles and notes between 1971-2006. 100 and more are dedicated on Xiongnu-Sarmatian period study. His works have been published in Russian, English, German, Mongolian, Kirgiz, Chinese, Turkmen and Turkish languages in Russia, Hungary, Poland, Mongolia, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, China, Korea, Turkmenistan, Turkey and Ukraine. He systematically studied the materials related to the weapons of the nomads from
Xiongnu to Turkic and Uighur periods in his book
“Вооружение средневековых кочевников Южной Сибири и Центральной Азии” (Novosibirsk,
1986). He also drew a methodology to classify the weapons in this book. His next book is devoted to systematical research on the types of weapons used by Kurykan, Bayirku and Shiwei in the PreMiddle Age and of Khidan and Mongols in the
Middle Age, as well as art of war, in the book
entitled “Вооружение центральное-азиатских кочевников в эпоху раннего и развитого
средневековья” (Novosibirsk, 1991). The book
“Военное дело древних кочевников Южной Сибири и Центральной Азии” (Novosibirsk,
1995) reveals the weapons of the nomads in the Pre-Iron Age and particularly the Bronze Age. And he studied and wrote about the iron guard meansarmor and helmet owned by the nomads in the
periods from Later Bronze Age to the Middle Age
in the book “Защитное вооружение номадов
Центральной Азии” (Novosibirsk, 2003). These main works are considered to be important contributions of Professor Yu.S.Khudyakov to the studies on Central Asian Nomadic weapons and art of war. He is a scholar who connected his life with Mongolia, and particularly Mongolian studies for tens of years and made a valuable contribution to the development of Mongolian Archeology. He started to work in Mongolia in the end of 1970 in the framework of the Mongol-Soviet joint expedition for history and culture. Since then, he has come to Mongolia several times, worked in cooperation with Mongolian researchers and got a high reputation among them. His 10 books and 150 articles telling are about Mongolia. Number of scientific articles on the archeological materials of the Burkhan Tolgoi burial site dated the Xiongnu



K
121
period, especially their weapons and equipments of horse are significant for the study of Xiongnu archeology. (Ts.T.)
KHUKH KHAD tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. Khukh Khad is a particular big rock in the middle of some rocky hills with no trees in the east part of the Baga Gazriin Chuluu, Dundgobi aimag. Here identified 8 Xiongnu circular graves in 2004 by Mongol-American joint expedition. Also in south of it an isolated Xiongnu burial was discovered in nearby hollow between the hills. Same year the expedition conducted an excavation on that isolated tomb and one of the main cemetery. (G.E.)
KHUNKHERIIN AM tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. Located on the north bank of Egyin Gol river, where is a narrow valley with a small stream called Belseg, Khutag-Undur soum, Bulgan aimag. The sub-valley facing to eastward along Belseg to the west is named Khunkheriin Am. This Xiongnu cemetery with 6 burials is on the first elevated terrace in Khunkheriin Am. These burials are presented by similar sized circular rings of flat stones on the surface. This site was discovered in 1999 by Mongolian-American joint archaeological team and 1 burial was excavated the same year. (G.E.)
KHURALDAI (Assembly). According to the historical records about Xiongnu, “During Tsagaan sar, all leaders gathered in a smaller assembly in the palace of Chanyu and carried out offering ceremony. Great assembly took place in Longcheng in May to carry out offering ceremony to ancestors, sky, earth, mountain spirits. In the fall when horse gets fattened, great assembly took place in Dailing to carry out census of population and livestock“. According to the records “Offering ceremony took place on dog day to worship sky and mountains three times a year, during Tsagaan sar, in May and September. So many nomadic tribes gathered to discuss state matters, and have horse and camel racing to celebrate this convention”. There
were regular gatherings every year. It is clear from historical recordings that Tsagaan sar annual gathering was named “Baga huraldai” (Smaller Assembly) and the annual gathering that took place in May and September were named “Ikh huraldai” (Great Assembly). Apart from 10 horned chefs, state noblemen, such as 14 chefs of ten thousand households took part in “Baga huraldai”, but not those of lower noblemen and ordinary people. Apart from state noblemen, ordinary citizens were all allowed to take part in “Ikh huraldai”. During this event, state matters were discussed, offering ceremonies and festivities were carried out along with population and livestock census. (No.B.)
KHUREET DOV Settlement. Xiongnu period settlement in Mongolia. Located in Baruun Baidlag, Mungunmorit soum, Tuv aimag. There is no archaeological excavation conducted yet. Size and structure of Khureet Dov is not defined. It has been defined as Xiongnu period site based on the chance finding of a roof tile found in Khureet Dov and brought by local herdsmen named Bazarvaani in 1950. (Ts.E.)
KHUSTYN BULAG METAL SMELTING KILN. Xiongnu period metal processing site in Mongolia. The small river that located approximately 20 km from Mungunmorit soum, Tuv province, and flows into Kherlen River is called Zuun Baidlag. There are hundreds of historical and archaeological sites from the Stone Ages to the Middle Ages in the river valley. There Khustyn bulag site



K
122
is a small spring south of a small mountain, located in the midst of the river channel, with few birches. The spring is called Khustyn Bulag by locals. In the vicinity of this place Mongolian-Japanese joint “Gurvan Gol” project conducted survey in 1990 and later in 1999 Mongolian-Korean joint project “Mon-Sol” carried out an excavation on a ceramic producing kiln. Since 2011 Mongolian-Japanese joint team of “History of Mongolian metallurgy” project have been carrying out excavations on the site with iron slags that located on the small hill north of Khustyn Bulag. As a result the team uncovered a kiln that had been used for smelting metal. Currently there have been found several significant items that are connected to the history of metallurgy and these items are three remains of furnace and ceramic ends of pumps, and wall pieces of furnace. The furnaces for smelting ore are square and corners are round and have stone made walls that covered with clay. Average size of the furnaces is 70×50 cm. 40 cm deep pit also with clay cover was found under the base of the furnaces. These pits were dedicated to concentrate the slags during ore smelting. After completion of ore smelting process furnaces were deliberately broken into pieces in order get the smelted iron. Thus it is impossible to determine the height of the furnaces. Though the ends of pumps and furnace walls were found in broken condition
these proves that pumps were used in furnaces. The furnaces are from the 1st century BCE and 1st century CE, according to the C14 laboratory analysis. Hence it is obvious that these are related to history of The Xiongnu. While placing the furnace in the pit the edges and wall part of the pit were put up high and covered with clay which then baked in order make it hard. This demonstrates the process of iron bloomery from ore in that place. This type of furnace is differentiated from the furnaces, which raised with raw bricks, of the Chinese Han dynasty, but has similarities with iron smelting furnaces of Europe, Black Sea and South Siberia. (Ch.A.)
KNIFE. The Xiongnu graves have kept a few iron swords and knives, short distance weapons, because the iron was a precious metal at that time. So the Xiongnu rarely put these kinds of weapons along the distinguished warriors only on a special occasion in the graves. The Xiongnu used a knife having a straight back and a short handle with a knife blade and a ring at the top of the ring. Knives with a short handle and a straight back are found from the Xiongnu monuments excavated in the neighboring countries. The knife was used for not only household needs but also battle especially at close-handed fight. (N.E.)
KONOVALOV, Prokopii Batyurovich (1937-). Russian archaeologist, Xiongnu scholar. Doctor of Science in History, Honored Scientific worker of Buriyat Republic, member of Oriental Studies Association of Russian Federation, member of International Mongolian Studies Association. He graduated from the State University of Irkutsk, Russian Federation by specialization “History” in 1959. In 1960-1962, he worked in the Department of History and Ethnography of the Complex Institute of Science and Studies of Buriyatia of the Siberian branch of the Academy of Science, Soviet Union. In 1975, he received his doctor of education degree in history by defending dissertation entitled “Burial sites of Xiongnu”. In 2000, he received his doctor of Science degree in history by
Khustyn bulag site
Khustyn bulag site



K
123
defending his dissertation “The Cultural and ethnic origins of Central Asian people (ancient and middle period)”. Since 1965, he has been working in the Institute of Social Sciences of Buriyatia (actaully named as The Institute of Mongol, Buddhism and Tibetian studies of the Siberian branch of the Academy of Sciences, Russian Federation). In 1981-1991, he worked as researcher of the Early iron ages and middle ages field research group of the Mongolian-Soviet joint expedition of history and culture. He has excavated more than 50 graves of Xiongnu period at Ilmovaya Pad’, Derestuy, Eduy; Cheremukhovaya Pad’ in Trabsbaikalia. Main scientific works of P.B.Konovalov on Xiongnu thematic include: “Xiongnu in the Transbaikalia”. -Ulan-Ude, 1976.-220p.; “The Royal tomb at Suji (Ilimovaya Pad’ Zabaikaliye)”. -Ulan-Ude: Publishing house of the Center of Science of Buried. SO RAN, 2008.-106p. (M.Ts.)
KOZLOV, Petr Kuz’imich (1863-1935). Russian explorer and scientist. Member of the Academy of Science of Ukraine, an honored member of the Geographical Society of Russia. He participated as field assistant in the field expeditions of N.M.Prjewaliski, M.W.Pevtsov and W.I.Robovskii. In 1899-1901, he led the Mongolian-Tibetan exploration expedition. He combined the results of their studies and wrote the book entitled “Mongolia and Kam”. He discovered the ancient Khar Khot city of Tangut, during the field expedition of Mongolia-Sichuan in 1907-1909. He also excavated and studied the Royal tomb at Noyon Uul of Mongolia in 19231926. This excavation and study became the world class scientific discovery. Among the scholarly works of P.K.Kozlov followings books concerning Xiongnu thematic: “Diaries of the MongolianTibetan expedition”, 1923-1924”, “Scientific legacy”- T.30. - Spb, “Science” 2003” are the most important. (M.Ts.)
KRADIN, Nikolai Nikolayevich (1962-). Russian anthropologist and historian. Was born in the village Onokhoi of the Zaigrayev district of Buriyatia in April 17, 1962. Chief research fellow of the Institute of History, Archaeology and Ethnography of Far Eastern people of the Far Eastern branch of the Academy of Sciences
of the Russian Federation, chef of the Department of the Social Anthropology of the Far Eastern State Technological University. He had been doing research in the theoretical and practical fields of Eurasian nomads, political anthropology, social archaeology, theory of social evolution, earth-system concept. N.N.Kradin, in political anthropology, defined “the super complex (complicated) chiefdom” as the principal socialpolitical form suitable to nomads, but not to settled or peasant people. He has published more than 300 scholarly works, including 8 monographs and 18 collective works. Namely, they include “Nomads society”, Vladivostok, 1992; “Xiongnu Empire”, Vladivostok, 1996; “Alternative ways to civilization”, M., 2000 (with D.M. Bodarenko, A.B Korotaev, B.A. Lynshei and others); “Political anthropology”, textbook, M., 2004; “The empire Chinggis Khan”, M., 2006 (with T.D.Shrynnikova); “Nomads of Eurasia”, Almaty, 2007. He supported the theory of that nomads were attracted to the neighboring settled civilizations and furthermore have tendency to be assimilated by them, following O.Lattimor, A.M.Khazanov and Th.Barfield. Moreover, he tried to demonstrate that the Xiongnu society had the above mentioned regularity and it was connected to the specific system of rile of nomads in his work “Xiongnu Empire”. The reasons for the foundation of the Xiongnu Empire were, as he defined, nomad’s intention to acquire Chinese agricultural and handcrafting products, to take control over transcontinental trade roads, to resist southern political and cultural influences. That was why herdsmen had to unite into a big “Tribal Empire”. He also emphasized the military and political talents of Maodun Chanyu for the founding of Xiongnu Empire. The social structure of Xiongnu was complex and hierarchical. At the top of the social pyramid were Chanyu and his relatives (of Luanti clan). Under them were other noble clans, with their leaders; aristocrats; beneath them came economically non dependent herdsmen-nomads. At the bottom of this pyramid might have been poor nomads, half dependent settled citizens, prisoners of war, peasants, hand craftsmen and slaves. The stability of the Empire ruled by Chanyu was maintained by its military operations, distribution



L
124
of “tribute”, income from trade and attacking its neighboring countries. Thus, he concluded that, the personal charisma of the ruler played an important role in governance. He proved that Xiongnu did not have a State, in his classical forms, based on several criteria such as general social economical indices, taxation system, formation of administrative officials and legal system. “The empire of nomads” was based on the confederacy of tribes which didn’t have any regulations concerning taxation (no tributary system) and the exploitation of dependent herdsmen is neglected. The power of the ruler was based on his ability to lead military forces and distribute the spoils of war and tribute taken from neighboring settled countries and income from trading, but it was not based on his legitimacy accepted by his population. It is not possible to consider this kind of political system as a State, he concluded. He defined the Xiongnu empire as a partial confederacy of tribes. As he assumed in the “Empire of nomads” with non-state style of the function, the governance was done by the super complex leadership. (S.U.)
KUITOU. One of the influent rulers of Xianbei. When Heliyan Khan was died, his son Qianman was too small to become Khan, Kuitou came to the throne temporarily. It is recorded in history that when Qianman grew up, Kuitou refused to give the throne to him who caused riots in Xianbei and the state was collapsed. (Ts.T.)
KUNMO title. This is the title of the ruler of the Wusun state. Wusun people conferred the title “Kunmi” (Gunmi, Kunmo) to their ruler. It happens to explain this word as this means “kunbek” (great ruler). Wusun people submitted to Xiongnu during the Maodun Chanyu period. What Maodun Chanyu wrote in his letter to the emperor of the Han dynasty indicates that Wusun state submitted to Xiongnu in the earlier period of time. This state’s name was emphasized in this letter as follows, “... At the order of the Sky, thanks to efforts of my great and brave warriors and the strength of my good horses, I have invaded and took control over 26 neighboring small and
big tribes including Loulan, Wusun and Huse. Nowadays, they have been united under the fame of the Xiongnu state...”. According to the legend noted by Zhan Qian, a courier sent by the Emperor Wudi of Han to the west, Wusun people used to protect the west border of ancient Xiongnu, when Wusun was defeated by Yuezhi, Xiongnu Chanyu adopted the small boy, a successor, and raised him at himself and granted him the title Kunmo, to have the small boy continue his father’s work. Later, Han emoire tried to attract Kunmo to its side by sending a princess to him, but he was attached with Xiongnu consistently. It indicates that Wusun was an important ally of Xiongnu in the beginning of Wudi reign of West Han. (G.E.; S.U.)
KYRGYZ. Ancient Turkic speaking people who resided in the Altai-Sayan mountainous region. It was first mentioned in historical sources in 201 BCE. The name was written in Chinese in several different ways such as Geguni, Jiangun etc. They might have resided in the North-West region of Mongolia. During Xiongnu expansion, they were forced to migrate to the North and lived on the territory of present Tuva and Khakasia. During the Rouran, Turkic and Uighur domination, they were in the subordinate position. In 758, it was invaded by Uighur. It broke out uprising very often, and in 840 they attacked and conquered the Uighur state suddenly, destroyed it and founded its own state. During this period of time, the king Abo ruled Kirgiz. Since this period of time, Kirgiz had been a dominant power for 80 years in Central Asia. Later, it was split into many khanate`s. When Khitans broke through Central Mongolia, Kirgiz had already been wiped out. In 1207, it submitted to Great Mongol Empire. Kirgiz people were engaged in half nomadic husbandry due to the fact that Minusinsk lowland, the main territory of Kirgiz had a lot of precipitation and much snowfall. They mostly raised cattle, horses and sheep and ran agriculture. Moreover, they were famous for their metallurgical manufacture and handcrafting. They used the Runic script adopted from Turkic people. They were ancestors of present Khakas, Altai and Kirgiz people. (Ts.T.)



L
125
L
AN Clan. Xiongnu noble tribe or clan name. In the chapter about Southern Xiongnu of the “Hou Han Shu”, talking about the events held in 40-50 CE, described that there was a permanent marriage treaty system between ruling Kuanti clan with other 4 noble clans as Huyani, Xiubu, Qiulin and Lan. Most researchers support the idea of G.Sukhbaatar about that main clans of Xiongnu such as Huyan, Lan, attested also between Xianbei, which was the remains of the tribal dual organization. (S.U.)
LAND USE. In the framework of the Theory of Formation, land is considered as an important industrial tool. As for the nomads, the land becomes an production tool together with livestock. The principle for using pasture causes it, as the theory explains. Until the 2nd half of 1950s, Soviet researchers raised a debate about the fact that the economic basis of feudalism in Mongolia was only land, or it was only livestock, or it was both of them together. Sh.Natsagdorj put forward his idea of the fact that a correct way of explaining the basis of feudalism in Mongolia is not emphasizing only land or livestock, but taking both of them in their dialectic relationships. This principle of Marxist methodology of land owning relations description existed for a long time in our historical documentary and Xiongnu and Xianbei society was compared with the primitive kingdoms of Western Europe of the 3-5th centuries CE. Land was under state property in the Xiongnu Empire. Maodun Chanyu was not possessive about his best horse and loved queen when Donghu people asked for them. However, when they asked for a piece of land between the two countires border which was not inhabited, he executed the officials who pretend that they agreed to give the land by saying that the “Land is the basis of a country”. Moreover, he invaded the Donghu pretending the demand of them as an aggression. This historical event is the evidence of the fact that land was in the possession of the state. Our researchers believe that under the Treaty established in 198 BCE, the Great Wall became the border between Xiongnu and Han. The Donghu coveted the piece of land between Xiongnu
and Donghu. In addition, there was the official named “Outo wang” who was responsible for the border guard. All these are sufficient evidences of that land was state property in Xiongnu. The Xiongnu people developed the production method of nomadic husbandry in which land owning efficiency was earned indirectly. For example: Tumet aristocracies from South Mongolia in late Middle age, let their subjects put their livestock out to pasture in their possessed land and regulated their movement. Nomadic economy, in which strength and fatness of livestock depended on the pasture and water, created traditional culture to love the land, treat the nature and environment appropriately and it became the vital basis for state policy. For instance, clear evidence of this is that there is information about the fact that Xiongnu had its own special map of its territory. However, representatives of Non-Marxist theory considered that the basis of nomadic economy was not connected with land relationships so on. Thus, although they did not deny the interrelation between land and livestock, they considered that land did not have any decisive influence on the nomadic economy. It is viewed that, as for nomads, land’s value increases when they communicate and have dealings with settlers. (S.U.)
LAOSHANG CHANYU (174-161 BCE). Xiongnu second Great Chanyu. This is the title granted to Xiongnu Chanyu whose real name was Jizhu. His official title was “Xiongnu Chanyu, the supreme leader, blessed by the Sun and the Moon, and conferred by the Sky and the Earth”. He was the son of Maodun Chanyu and his successor. During his reign Xiongnu became a dominant power in East Asia and expanded Xiongnu territory to the west and south west. Yuezhi were driven out of the Altai Mountain. He took control of the Northern part of Xinjiang and Gansu. Xiongnu took control of the Northern branch of Silk Road. (Ts.T.)
LIAOXI–XIANBEI. Tribal conferderation of Xianbei. It means Xianbei of the West part of the Liao River. After the collapse of the Xianbei Confederation, it was split into West Xianbei and East Xianbei. East Xianbei consisted of Murun and Duan tribes, West Xianbei consisted of Yuwen, Chifa, Tufa, Touba-Tabgach and Kumosi tribes. Khitan people also were included in Kumosi tribe. History of Khitan after this period was divided into 3 stages. 1st, Khitans of Eight Tribes (386-618), second, period of Dahe tribe (618-730), 3rd, period of the Confederacy of Yaonian tribe (730-907). (Ts.T.)



L
126
LI GUANG (died in 119 BCE). Military general of the West Han dynasty. His nickname was “Flying general”. His light cavalry had made a sudden rapid attack to the center of Xiongnu. Thus, he was named “Flying general”. First he emerged by his efforts into putting down the internal riots. However, he also got bad reputations for a series of failures he had in the following events and was named “unfortunate”. Several failures and unlucky events such as getting lost and being surrounded by enemies during the fight were written in his biography of a military general. That his soldiers were bad disciplined, he was bad at planning and adopting strategically military policy were the reasons for it. During the Yanmen battle, his army were defeated in 129 BCE, he fled barely, lost his title and became an ordinary citizen. In 120 BCE, he was also surrounded and defeated by Xiongnu. He committed suicide due to the face that he was going to be charged with the fact that he was blamed for that he was late for appointment during the fight with Xiongnu in the west of Gobi and let Ichixie Chanyu to flee. (Ts.T.)
LI LIN (died in 74 BCE). Famous general and statesman of West Han and Xiongnu Empire. He is the grandson of Li Guan, “Flying general”. Han Wudi emperor promoted him to high ranking military position considering his good origin and his talent for hunting and shooting even though he was young. Li Lin served for the border defense and was sent to the inland (towards the interior) as a secret agent. Although he did not succeed much he was promoted to the position of cavalry general. After several years, he began to participate in the campaigns accompanying great generals such as Li Guanli. In 99 BCE, general Li Guanli led 30.000 army to attack Xiongnu from the west. Li Lin was responsible for the backside and supply. However, he was not content with his responsibility. He asked for 5.000 cavalry to fulfill his plan to make a sudden attack to the palace of Chanyu and destroy him. When he introduced his plan to Han Wudi emperor, he granted permission. The army led by Li Lin advanced to the North for one month, and near Altai mountains have been surrounded and crashed by Xiongnu army. Only 400 cavalry could go home from his army. Li Lin surrendered to Xiongnu. After he arrived in Xiongnu, he was granted the title Yuxiao wang. It was the high rank equal to the chief counselor of Chanyu. However, mother queen of Chanyu did not like Li Lin. Thus,
he was sent to the North and was not called back until she died. In 90 BCE Han army attacked Xiongnu by 3 routes. When the general Shang Qiuchen arrived in the Altai mountain with his 30.000 cavalry, Li Lin opposed him with 30.000 cavalry of Xiongnu. He was defeated and withdrew back. He also failed to turn out Han envoy Su Wu to Xiongnu. He lived in Xiongnu for more than 20 years and died of illness in 74 BCE. There is a hypothesis that the remains of the Chinese style building found around the capital city Abakan of the Khakas Republic, Russian Federation might be Li Lin‘s palace. However, this issue has still been controversial. (Ts.T.)
LIN GAN. Chinese historian, Xiongnu scholar. Was born in Guandong province in 1916. Professor of the Department of Mongolian history of the Inner Mongolian University, Chairman of Mongolian studies society of the People’s Republic of China, Chairman of Middle Asian Culture studies society of the People’s Republic of China, Counselor of historical studies of the Nation, Honored doctor of the Institute of Social Science of Inner Mongolia. He wrote 12 monographs, 50 academic articles. His monographs based on the ancient records and archaeological studies such as “General history of Xiongnu” (Beijing, 1986), “History of Turkic people” (Huhhot, 1988), “History of Donghu” (Huhhot, 1989) they have been considered as important works on nomadic studies published in China. In addition to them, he also published works such as “Narrative of Xiongnu history” (Beijing, 1982), “Collection of historical sources of Xiongnu” Volume 1, 2 (Beijing, 1988), “Collection of scientific articles of Xiongnu history. 1919-1979”,(Beijing, 1983). (B.A.)
LIU BAO (3rd c. CE). Influent chef of South Xiongnu. Son of Yufuluo Chanyu and father of Liu Yuan, the founder of Xiongnu Han–Zhao State. When Huchuxian Chanyu came to the throne, he appointed him as the Left Tuqi wang. He exercised direct authority over 5 tribes of South Xiongnu. In the 21st year of Huchuxian, Cao Cao captured him, took him hostage and promoted him to the position of Chanyu. There are some evidences that he participated very actively in the political life of Northern China. It is unknown when he died. Though he was conferred the title Chanyu, he is not considered as the real Chanyu of South Xiongnu. When he changed his clan



L
127
name to Chinese style one syllable clan name, he considered the fact that ancient Chanyu had relations with Emperors with the clan name Liu of Han Dynasty by marriage and adopted the clan name Liu. (Ts.T.)
LIU YUAN (?-310 CE). The founder of the Han-Zhao state of Southern Xiongnu. He was originally from Luandi clan of Maodun Chanyu. His grandfather was Yufuluo Chanyu, his father was Liu Bao. When Cao Cao destroyed Southern Xiongnu and divided Xiongnu into 5 tribes, Liu Yuan was appointed to the position of the chef of the left tribe. During this period of time, 5 tribes Xiongnu settled in the North of present Shanxi province. Liu Yuan acquired classical Chinese education when he grew up in hostage in the Emperor’s palace of Jing Dynasty. After his father Liu Bao died he returned to his country and got entitled to become the chef of the left tribe. After he distinguished as an honest governor and was surrounded by respect of many skillful Xiongnu and Han people. In the end of 260, he was appointed as the governor of 5 Xiongnu tribes. However, around 300, he lost all his titles and ranks at the order of Jing court. The uprising was broken out under the leadership of chefs of Northern tribes of Southern Xiongnu around 304. Xiongnu people conferred the title “Great Chanyu” to Liu Yuan. Within a short period of time, he gathered 50.000 cavalry, grew powerful and declared that he founded the independent Han State by being separated from Jing Dynasty. It was promoted by him that he restored the Han State due to the fact that he had relationships with the old Han Dynasty in his mother’s lineage. After Liu Yuan became the emperor of State, he spent most of his time on founding the structure of governance of ancient Han Dynasty style. That he didn’t focus much on the expansion of the territory of the country and increasing the population had a negative influence on the further development of the state Han-Zhao of Xiongnu. In 308, Liu Yuan declared him as the emperor. In 310, he died of illness. Liu Yuan is a historic hero who could free southern Xiongnu who were under the exploitation of China for many years and founded the independent state. (Ts.T.)
LIWEI (219-277). Touba first historic leader. Later he was conferred the title Shen Yuan-di. He functioned very actively in the Central part of Inner Mongolia during the 3 Dysnasty period. He
was an ally of Wei state. (Ts.T.)
LONGCHENG. The Xiongnu Chanyu residence name recorded in historic sources. It means “Dragon city”. On Tsagaan sar (White Month), all important chefs gathered for a small assembly and made offerings. In May (the middle of summer) they gathered at Longcheng for a great assembly and made offerings to ancestors, the Sky, the Earth, sacred mountains. (B.B.)
LOULAN battle. Historcial battle between Xiongnu allies and Han in 108 BCE. Battle between the city-state which was in Xiongnu subordination and Han state for acquiring the Lob Nur lake basin in present day Xinjiang. There was no direct military involvement of Xiongnu. Military leader of Han dynasty was Zhao Ponu. Chinese military force consisted of 700 cavalry. Han Wudi emperor was aimed at cutting off the west part of Xiongnu and cutting agricultural and hand crafting supply. He considered this as a part of the large scale combat to be done against Xiongnu. In order to reach this goal, 2 city-states Loulan and Jushi which were in the subordination of Xiongnu were to be invaded. Under the command of Zhao Pony, light cavalry of Han state arrested Loulan wang and conquered Jushi aggressively. As a result of this battle, Han state took control of the east part of Tarim basin. It formed the circumstance of Chinese influence expansion into the Middle Asia. The last wang of Loulan city-state was killed by Chinese in 77 BCE. Soon after that Chinese people changed the name Loulan to Shanshang. (Ts.T.)
LOUFAN tribe. Xiongnu tribe name. The tribe is mentioned in different sources very often. It was written in historical sources to be located in the north of Qin, in the north of Zhao (in the west of present Shanxi province) and in the west of Zhao. Information to be recorded in the historical source was that Wuling wang of Zhao met Loufan tribe wang in the west of Zhao in 297 BCE. Researchers agree that all these pieces of information indicate that Loufan people resided in the Ordos region in the 4-3rd centuries BCE. Loufan nomads who were in the subordination of Yan State resided on the present day Datong county territory. It was in the west from the border of Yan State. There was a lot of blending and assimilation among them because of “Hu” Xiongnu attack until the 4th century BCE. The excavation made in the ruins of the Lower capital



L
128
city of Yan State made it possible for researchers to conclude that possibly Ordos region was settled by barbarian tribes with handcrafting animal art style similar to Altai Pazyryk style. Some researchers propose that these artifacts would be are related to Loufan people. The fact that Yan State rulers were very worried about Xiongnu invasion to Loufan indicates that Loufan was an alliance with Yan or was its vassals. Later Loufan was mentioned in historical records as Xiongnu tribe. (S.U.)
LUANDI clan. The name of Xiongnu ruling clan. It was written in “Hou Han Shu” that “Power of Chanyu could pass to someone from Xiuluanti clan. Huyan, Xiubu, Qiulin and Lan clans were noblemen clans. Huyan comes from left, Lan and Xiubu clans came from right wings.” In contrary to it, it was written in “Qian Han Shu” that “Power of Chanyu could pass to someone from Luandi clan. His state conferred him the title “Chengli gudu Chanyu”. Chengli means “The Heaven”, gudu means “Son” for Xiongnu.” Thus, the word Chanyu has broad meaning as the Sky. There are 2 versions of the explanation of this word. (S.U.)
LULI WANG. Name of one of 4 horned wangs of Xiongnu. The term is also named Guli wan. There were Luli wang of the left and Luli wang of the right. They were originally from Luandi clan of Chanyu. More than 10.000 soldiers were under his command. He also exercised direct authority over the right and left parts of the empire. (No.B.)
LÜ ZHI (Lü Taihou, 241-180 BCE). Commonly known as Empress Lü and Empress Dowager Lü or formally Empress Gao of Han, was the empress consort of Emperor Gaozu, the founder and first ruler of the Han Dynasty. After Emperor Gaozu’s death, she was honoured as Empress Dowager and later as Grand Empress Dowager respectively during the short reigns of Emperor Hui and his successors Liu Gong and Liu Hong. Lü Zhi dominated the political scene for 15 years until her death in 180 BC. During her reign period Peace and Marriage Treaty between Xiongnu and Han still in power and commercial relationship between two nations developed. (Ch. Ye.)



M
129
M
AODUN CHANYU (234-174 BCE). The founder and the first emperor of Xiongnu Empire during the period of 209-174 BCE. He is well known for establishing structure of military and social administration of nomads. Origin of his name is arguable. There are two main ideas about that and one explains his name in relation with Mongolian word “modun” (wood) and another one explains in relation with MongolTurkic word “bagatur” (hero). His childhood lifetime is unknown and only it is clear that he was the eldest son of Xiongnu Tumen Chanyu. His young manhood was described clearly in Shiji so far. According to the source he was taken in to the hostage in neighboring rival Yuezhi state due to the order of his father who was affected of others abetment however he was Tumen’s eldest son who supposed to inherit his father according to the steppe law. He became popular among the people and his reputation has increased after he stole the best horse from Yuezhi and escaped from hostage safely. Even though his father did not like, he appointed his son as chief of ten thousand warriors according to the condition. Since that time Maodun Chanyu become more popular for his bravery and smartness. In 209 BCE he killed his father as well as many rival lords and acceded to the throne. During this time Donghu tribes from east side of Xiongnu tried to watch the young Chanyu and sent messenger and claimed many things to find reason for assaulting. Maodun Chanyu said that “we are neighbors so how we could scant horses and queens from them” and implemented their claims many times. He did it tactically to deny Donghu assault. The third claim of Donghu was to get empty land between borders of two countries which was overrunning issue. So Maodun Chanyu convened great assembly and discussed about Donghu’s claim. Some of the lords suggested giving this land to the neighbor and it made Maodun Chanyu furious and he said the word “Land is the root of the country” which became later a wise word. There’s a history that when Maodun Chanyu was ready for the war tying up their forces and they attacked Donghu suddenly and destroyed their confederation. Xiongnu started to rule in 209 BCE destroying Donghu confederation and winning in the
war of northern Dinling and south-western Yuezhi. This year is considered as the historical year of establishment of Xiongnu Empire. Historical feat of Maodun Chanyu is that first time Mongolian, Turkic, Tungusic, Iranian and Tibetan-Qian nomads reunited and became more powerful to stand against Chinese intervention. In 200 BCE war against Han dynasty was ended up with victory and established peace treaty with them. Establishing first empire in nomadic history Xiongnu faced many bigger wars and brought 26 countries under their administration. After the victory of Baideng in 200 BCE Han dynasty accepted Xiongnu power and defined borders of two great powers by the Great Wall and decided to pay tribute named as “present” every year. The agreement is called “Peace and marriage treaty”. This was the historical merit of nomads which created first model of co-habitation with China which last hundred years. “Peace and marriage treaty” of 198 BCE is a historical evidence of approving nomads are not part of China, they’re independent and has their own culture. When emperors of Xiongnu and China exchanged official letters Maodun Chanyu used to start his letter as “Great Chanyu of Xiongnu with the mandate of Heaven respectfully greeting to Huangdi”. When emperor of Han sent official letter to Xiongnu Chanyu he used to start “Huangdi respectfully greeting to Great Chanyu of Xiongnu”. This shows that diplomatic relation between Xiongnu Empire and West Han dynasty was equal and respectful. Modun divided its country to three main parts and each part is divided to ten thousands, thousands, hundreds and tens. Horizontal system of transferring state power from brother to brother, uncle to cousin was followed by Maodun’s family continuously. Administration organization of Maodun which is a perfect system existed stable for hundreds of years. Later Chinggis khan used this system for establishing his empire without any big change and it shows the great historical tradition and at the same time it shows that Xiongnu and Mongols are originally related. Maodun Chanyu was died in 174 BCE after ruling Xiongnu for 35 years and extended, strengthened and brought its country to the same level as powerful countries. His empire lasted for more 300 years so far. (Ts.T.)
MAO KING. Legendary person related to Xianbei and Xiongnu history. Some scholars as G.Sukhbaatar suggested that Mao king, appeared in the myth of Touba-Tabgach could be identified as Maodun Chanyu of Xiongnu. According to the Chinese historical source Wei Shu, Mao or Perfect



M
130
Huangdi reigned as a king of 36 states and 99 bigger tribes by his kinship for his heroic and sensible emprise. Consequently, by some scholars view, ToubaTabgach ancestors linkage to Maodun Chanyu shows the connection between Xiongnu and Xianbei. (Ts.T.)
MANGASYN KHUREE Fortress. Xiongny period settlement in Mongolia. This square shaped wall is situated in the north-west side of Gurvan Zeerd cave of Galbyn Gobi, 10 km far from state border, in Khanbogd soum of Umnugobi aimag. The ruin located at the mounting middle of the ancient large rivers 2 branches. West branch of ancient swash located in west north to south east broke the ground wall of south west of the ruin so there is only arched wall left in the north of the ruin. This wall has 2-5 meters of width, 50 centimeters of height and 595 meters of diameter. Squire wall is located 200 meters inwards to the circle wall. The wall has doors in north and south side and width of the doors is 1819 meters and width of the wall is 6-18 meters and height is 1.62-1.7 meters. Length of the north wall is 130 meters, west wall is 140 meters, south wall is 130 meters and east wall is 132 meters. There are some stone structures in the north-west and the south side of the wall. Some tools as bronze arrowhead, iron axe, broken ceramic vases are gathered near the wall. Scholars consider that Mangasyn Khuree site could be dated to same period as nearby located Bayanbulag ruin around 1st century BCE to 1st century CE. These two sites located at the north of 42th latitude, otherwise in the “North of Gobi” or Borzongyn Gobi and Galbyn Gobi, 200 km distant from each other. It might been a frontier fortification based by natural border between Xiongnu and Han dynasty. (Ch.A.; J.G.)
MATERIAL CULTURE. Dwelling, household items, clothing, jewelry made from gold, silver, bronze, art, animal gear, weaponry, utensils, pottery can be studied in relation to material culture of Xiongnu.These objects defining material culture have their own specific making features distinguishing them from artifacts found from burials of other
nomadic tribal states. Particularly, pottery with wave pattern is totally different from the pottery used by nomads of different period. (Nо.B.)
Mangasyn khuree fortress
Xiongnu typical ceramic pot



M
131
MAYI CITY INCIDENT. Frontier city incident between Xiongnu and Han. It was a 60 years peace breaking event happened near the Mayi fortified city in Shouzhou city, north of present Shanxi province in summer of 133 BCE. However damages was not much for both Xiongnu and Han dynasties, but it was the first disturbance since 200 BCE after Baideng battle. In other side, it was beginning event of next 40 years of fighting. Xiongnu army leaded by Guncheng Chanyu himself, Han army leaded by Wang Hui, Han Anguo, Li Guang and Gongsong He. Xiongnu army force consisted by 100.000 cavaliers, and Han army consisted by 300.000 soldiers. Pre-condition of fighting based by the enlargement of Han army forces, by another side, an young king Wudi, who was an aggressive one started to reign Han dynasty. By the plan of Wudi, a salesman named Nie Wengyi reached to Guncheng Chanyu and became trustiness one, and proposed to plunder Mayi city to Chanyu. It was a trap to defeat the Xiongnu, and Chanyu passed over the Great Wall, moved to China by Wudi`s arrangement. But 300.000 Han soldiers were concealed in the vicinity of Mayi city in waiting Xiongnu to approach. When Xiongnu army reached 100 li (40 km) close in Mayi city, they overlooked numerous cattle without herders, and Guncheng Chanyu becoming suspicious by this condition. Then he examined the seized Chinese low ranking officer of yuxi rank, and knew the trap. Xiongnu army back to the country immediately and Chanyu had said “I have saved by the heavens grandeur, which sent me that officer”. Wudi understood that he needs to develop his cavaliers in order to be efficient first of all and to promote young determinant generals as Wei Qing and Huo Qubing. (Ts.T.)
MA LIQING. Chinese archaeologist, Xiongnu scholar. Was born in Huhhot, Inner Mongolia, in 1969. Working at the Department of History, Peoples University of PRC. Her major is history and archaeology of ancient nomads of Central Asia including Xiongnu study. Wrote a monograph “Pre-Xiongnu and Xiongnu: Study of History and Cultural Archaeology of Xiongnu” (Huhhot, 2005), and scientific articles related to Xiongnu study as “To Explore Xiongnu Monuments by Archaeology and Anthropological Science” (Scientific Journal of Mingzu University, Beijing, 2007, No4), “Some Issues of Xiongnu Ruins” (Journal of Social Science of Inner Mongolia, 2002, No4), “To Examine Xiongnu Scripts by Archaeological Study” (Kaogu yu Wenwu, 2004, No2), “The Rank of Xiongnu Study in Ordos” (Archaeological Study of Western Regions,
2006, No1), “To Comprehend Xiongnu borders by Xiongnu Archaeological Distribution” (Scientific Journal of National University of Inner Mongolia, 2005, No1), “First Sources of Xiongnu Name and its Concept” (Scientific Journal of Mingzu University, Beijing, 2005, No6), “North Xiongnu Moving to the West by Archaeological Survey” (Journal of Social Science of Inner Mongolia, 2004, No1). (B.A.)
MEANS OF GUARD-ARMOR. In the Russian translation of Chinese historical records “Materials for History of Xiongnu” made by V.S.Taksin has a word of Chao Cao (?-154 BCE) to Wendi Emperor that “... because crossbow shooting is pretty strong, Xiongnu leather armor and wood shield were easily broken”. According to the source, the Xiongnu soldiers had their own means of guard like leather armor and wood shield and so on. Arm or elbow guards made of metal and bronze, were objectively found from the tombs Nos. 20 and 25 of the Noyon Uul and remains of plate armor from the tomb No.32 of the Gol Mod and the Ivolga settlement respectively. Two bronze casting objects were discovered from the tomb No.25 of the Noyon Uul: one has 26×8 cm, another one has 23×7 cm. The long narrow surface is little round at one end and two internal ends have strong rings. This guard might be used for shooting, battling with a sword during the handgrip and protecting the forearm, shoulder and muscle from the lateral hit. The guard is fastened the arm with a ring and made thick due to the force of hit. The plate iron armor found from the Ivolga settlement is considered to be the first one of the Xiongnu monuments in Central Asia. Its plate is generally rectangular but round at the lower end with holes to tie the plates. Yu.S.Khudyakov proved that this was the chest-guard armor and Central Asian nomadic plate armor was originated from the Xiongnu plate armor. Probably the plates of the upper part of the armor found from the tomb No.20 of the Gol Mod have two holes on each those are like leaf in shape and are tied with a leather strap. Its strong and thick feature indicates this might be an armor of the cavalry. The light armor was suitable for the dynamic maneuver of the Xiongnu cavalry. Apart from them, there is another important mean of guard is the helmet or a head guard. But no helmet has been found by any archeological excavation now. The Xiongnu means of guard are very simple but suitable for their dynamic movement and got more sophisticated and passed down to the Middle Age. (N.E.)



M
132
MENG TIAN (?-210 BCE). Prominent general of Qin Dynasty, close entourage of Shi Huangdi. His father was a general too. He attacked to Xiongnu and occupied some part of Ordos and south side of Yingshan mountain area in 215 BCE. In order to keep his newly occupied land he created by the order of Shi Huangdi the Great Wall and put up the new road from the Xian’an city the capital of Qin dynasty to the frontier wall, shifted inner land farmers to the new borderland and was tried to decide their food difficulties. Also he is considered as an inventor of writing brush. After passing away Shi Huangdi he suicide himself by the court intrigue. (Ts.T.)
METALLURGY. As we know metallurgy had been developed in CentralAsia before the Xiongnu. Square burials of Central Mongolia, Chandmani Culture of Western Mongolia and Pazyryk burials keep the evidences of highly developed metallurgy. We can see Xiongnu metallurgy level by archaeological data,
and they were made hammer, axe, chisel, copper, shovel shout and knife, besides weapons as hanger knife, arrowhead, sword, and horse equipments, belt adornments by iron. In Ivolga settlement an iron casting kiln was found in a large scale production building. Unfortunately this building was not completely
excavated and it is difficult to understand the whole complex. The horn was presented in surface by 30×30 m measured rectangular earthen mound with rounded corners and with the height of 1.6-1.65 m, looked like a burial mound. Under randomly lying stone slabs were found the remains of forge horn to smelt iron. Horn consisted of two parts: a mine where ore is melted, and the floor of the underground channel through which air blower and regulated furnace hearth and where take off ready metal. Shaft was a dug in the earth square hollow with rounded corners and with walls, covered with clay, was strongly slaged. Shaft length - 37.5 cm, width - 37.5 cm, depth - 35 cm. Lower base of the shaft northern wall was connected with an oval cross section underground canal of 20 cm high, and a width equal to the side of the shaft. Bottom of the shaft gradually increased to the oval in terms of the external opening of the course. Total length of semiunderground channel was 90 cm. At the top at the southwest corner of the shaft remains of clay deified spherical dome ceiling of the shaft. The process of smelting iron is as follows: strongly crushed iron ore and charcoal laid alternating layers through the hole in the dome, the ceiling of the shaft, which is
Iron cheek piece, bronze decorations of bridle, Duurlig nars, Tomb 2
Iron bit, Gol mod, Tomb 44
Iron buckle of bridle, Burkhan tolgoi site



M
133
then closed up the special cork clay for the entire production cycle of iron smelting. The particles of the dome with a cork were found near the horn. As a result of melting, the recovered particles of iron sank to the bottom, slip and forming loup (crude iron) soaked with some more slag. The wreckage of loup were discovered in large quantities. Russian archaeologist A.V.Davidova assumed that the metallurgy ores exploited in local mines, more precisely from iron ore rich localities in Tarvagatai valley of Selenge river, Korotkovo and Petrovsk-Zabaikalsk in Dzida river valley. Accordingly, it is impossible to bring the ores from the distant mines and for the development of metallurgy the local mines were essential. One of important fact of pig iron casting is the pig iron plowshares discovered from Ivolga site. These plowshares length is 11 cm, width is 8 cm and in cross section it has trapezoid shape, backside is wider than the working face of it. The working face of the flow has rounded edge. There made triangle holes to attach to wooden harrow. (Ts.T.)
MILLER, Bryan K. American archaeologist and anthropologist. Born on June 23, 1975. He defended Master’s degree in Chasten Institute of Archeology, University of California, Los-Angeles and Doctor’s degree entitled “Power Politics in the Xiongnu Empire” in the Institute of East Asian Languages and Civilizations at the University of Pennsylvania. B.Miller was worked at tomb No.1 of Gol Mod-2 site, one of the biggest Xiongnu elite funeral complex found in Arkhangai province in 20012006. Made a Xiongnu funeral monuments survey in Khovd province in 2006. Since 2007, he has started the joint project with Mongolian National Museum for the study of Xiongnu tombs in Khovd province. By this project, he excavated 1 elite tomb in Takhiltyn Khotgor site in Mankhan soum, and discovered common funerals in Shombuuzyn Belchir site in Munkh-Khairkhan soum. He has published more than 10 scientific articles about the result of the study and the excavation in Mongolia. He organized the 1st International conference for Xiongnu Archaeology in Ulaanbaatar in 2008 with Dr. Ursula Brosseder. Moreover, B.Miller and U.Brosseder are the co-editors of the conference proceedings on Xiongnu study, which collected more than 35 articles in several languages. This work is published in 2011 for 2220th anniversary of Xiongnu State. (Na.B.)
MILITARY WEAPONRY. According to the historical records and artifacts related to weaponry from archeological excavations, there were many
types of weapons used by Xiongnu soldiers. Xiongnu army consisted of swift cavalry with horn composite bow and arrow as main weapon. Composite bows were used with arrows made from bone, bronze, and iron arrow heads. Bone arrowheads were used to attack light cavalry without armor, iron arrowheads with 2-4 blade were used to hurt or kill horsemen with iron and leather armor. Also Xiongnu used different arrowheads with special purposes, such as whistling arrowheads to make signals or frighten men and animals of opposition side using bulbous bone arrowhead. Xiongnu warriors used long and short swords with double blade and straight, one sided blade to strike, pierce opposition in close combat. Also iron spear with socket was used for close combat. As protection gear, they used wooden shield, iron and leather armor, arm and elbow protection,
Iron arrowheads of Burkhan tolgoi tombs
Iron arrowhead, Burkhan tolgoi, Tomb 72



M
134
belt and leather boots. These gears were simple, but sturdy, well made and adequate for swift movement of cavalry. By comparing Xiongnu weaponry with Bronze and Early Iron Age weaponry, several significant progress steps noticeable in weapon development. For example: type, shape and size of arrowhead for long range changed dramatically, namely due to the introduction of whistling arrowhead, arrow head made of combination of bone with iron and combination of iron and bronze and entirely from one material, changing the size and shape, was not only made progress in making of arrowhead, but it improved the making of bows. Plate armor, iron protection for hand and elbow, belt, horse forehead protection are the most significant achievements made in the protection gear of cavalry of nomad tribes. Improvements in the fortress building also were noticed. (N.E.)
MILITARY WEAPONRY DEVELOPMENT. End of 2nd millennium BCE and beginning of 1st millennium BCE Central Asian nomads played important role in the history of Eurasia. In special geographical location and extreme climate, people living in Gobi and steppe region were follow nomadic way of life. Nature dictated people who consumed meat and dairy product form domestic animals to look for better pasture for their livestock in vast to overcome climatic and other hardship. Apart from natural forces, there were social factors as well. With intensified development of animal husbandry, weaponry affaire of nomads also developed. The increase of livestock created need for tribal states to merge. United
tribal states could overcome heavy winter, and protect themselves from robbery by joining forces human and other resources. Researcher Yu.S.Khudyakov noted that attacks to rob others’ livestock and other wealth were common type of armored conflicts among nomads. In most cases, the side with better use of weapons defeated the opponent, which was the main factor to develop weaponry. Archeological artifacts reflect the importance of weaponry and tools for nomads. Single and multiple deer stone dedicated to war heroes, kereksur with complicated structure, petroglyphs with military and battle images, numerous artifacts of various weapons discovered during archeological excavation are inherited to this generation. According to the archeological artifacts studies on the discovered early weapons, before the establishment of Xiongnu Empire, i.e. end of 1st millennium BCE, weaponry of nomads were fairly well developed, nomadic tribal confederations with cavalry with complete weaponry were created. Introduction of metal use among nomads, developed production of weapons and significant progress is made in material and intellectual culture. This created cultural connection among Mongolia, Transbaikalia, South Siberia and Middle Asian nomadic tribes. With establishment of Xiongnu Empire, Maodun Chanyu defined internal and external diverse policy. During this time, the legitimate need to create diplomatic service in order to send special envoy, exchange of letters, establishment of treaties, making negotiation, all of this followed by military and weaponry reform. From historical records and archeological materials, it is clear that Xiongnu developed and improved traditional weaponry, military arrangement, and combat tactics. Progress in military arrangement, weaponry and combat tactics made during Xiongnu period differ from previous generation, due to the fact that nomadic tribal state were not fighting with each other, but rather had to fight against other settled civilizations. Nomadic lifestyle, stamina of nomads, archery and horse riding skills developed from early childhood and through everyday training, long range
Bone arrowheads, Burkhan tolgoi
Iron sword, Baruun khairkhan
Bone bulb of a wistlin arrow, Ulaan del tomb



M
135
composite bow and arrow, weaponry, protection gear, skillful cavalry, elaborate tactics, maneuvers, talented leaders, strict command were all quality of high skillfulness of Xiongnu army and succeeded to unite Eurasian nomads under one flag with strong army equal to Chinese empire. We agree the writing by well-known researcher L.N.Gumilev, saying “Chinese historians d i s t o r t e d history by recording that X i o n g n u were barbarians and robbers from steppe. In reality, it was not the case. Once border trade i s s u e s were solved, a t t a c k s from nomads s t o p p e d and economic relationship improved, if X i o n g n u were barbarian robbers as claimed in the records, the robbery attacks would had been still there. H o w e v e r , this situation was not pleasant to C h i n e s e government”. Han empire had a policy to spoil good relationship between people of two countries and dismantle unified nomad state, which later became the reason for Xiongnu extinction. Xiongnu Empire was unified, powerful Eurasian
empire and the progress made in the development of social, political, defense, culture sphere were continuously further applied by many states that ruled the territory of Mongolia. (N.E.)
MING YUANDI (409-423). Xianbei origine Touba emperor. He was the second emperor of Touba Wei dynasty. Own name was Touba Xi. Son of Touba Gui. However he could not extend the territory as his father and brothers, but he could pacified his state and created the practice to decide state major affairs with aristocrats and counselors. He is called in the history as a wise emperor. His son Touba Tao succeeded him. (Ts.T.)
MINYAEV, Sergey Stepanovich. Russian archaeologist, Xiongnu scholar. Senior research fellow at the Institute for the Material Culture in Sankt-Petersburg, Russia. Was born in 1948, and graduated Department of History, State University of Leningrad in 1972. Received Ph.D by the thesis of “Bronze Tools of Xiongnu, Typology, Production and Distribution” in 1982.
