Since 1981, he has been working as a head of the Transbaikalian archaeological expedition. This team made an archaeological surveys at Duren 1, 2, Lower Ivolga, and Dyrestuy sites, and in the last decade the team have been excavating elite and common burials of Xiongnu at Tsaram near Kyakhta. S.S.Minyaev published about 70 scientific works for archaeology and culture of Xiongnu
and his major works are as follows: “К проблеме
происхождения Сюнну”, Информационный бюллетень МАИКЦА (вып.9, Москва, 1985, сс.70-80), “Раскопки Хуннских поселений в Забайкалье” Археологические открытия 1974 г. (Москва, 1975), “К выделению Гуннских памятников Сибири”, Материалы конференции “Методические аспекты археологических исследовании в Западной Сибири” (Томск, 1981), “К проблеме ранних и поздних памятников Сюнну” (Древние культуры Северной Азии и их охранные раскопки Новосибирск, 1988), “Дырестуйский могильник” (Археологические памятники Сюнну, вып. 3, СПб., 2003), “Ханьская колесница из могильника Царам” (Археологические Вести, вып.14, М, 2007,
стр.130-137). (M.Ts.)
ib d i
Bone arrowhead
Iron arrowheads, Burkhan tolgoi tombs



M
136
“MONGOLIA: FIRST EMPIRE OF THE STEPPE” (Mongolie: le premier empire des steppes. Actes sud/ Mission archeologique francaise en Mongolie, Paris, 2003, pp. 236). Catalogue. Dedicated for one of Mongolian first archaeologist Ts.Dorjsuren (1923-1994). Published in 2003, during the anniversary of Mongolia-France joint archaeological expedition. Middle age French envoys travel notes, French Photographic expedition of 1909 in Mongolia, Mongol-French joint archaeological expedition at Egyin Gol site in Mongolia are included in foreword of the catalogue. The major chapters of the catalogue consisted by the articles about the study of Gol Mod site, which Mongolian and French archaeologists have been working since 2000. The articles consisted by the topics as environmental survey, result of the study and palaeoanthropology, iron tools, and artifacts of the beginning of the Bronze and Iron ages. (Ch.Ye.)
“MONGOLIA: XIONGNU OF ARKHANGAI AIMAG”. (Mongolie: les Xiongnu de l`Arkhangai), Ulaanbaatar, 2007 (in French). Brochure dedicated to the 15th anniversary of Mongol-French joint archaeological expedition. The brief introduction of the expedition, and the result of the Gol Mod tomb No.20 excavation results included in this brochure. Also, concise results of the study of archaeological findings near the Gol Mod site related from Neolithic to Turkic period, archaeological survey around the Gol Mod site, study of Xiongnu funeral structure, analyze of paleoanthropological and archaeozoological findings, Xiongnu aristocrats, restoration of artifacts, and laboratory analyze etc about 10 scientific articles are included. Archaeological monuments of various periods discovered in Gol Mod confirm that Gol Mod site is the native land of Xiongnu and afterward nomads, besides settled from very beginning of human being. The newly discovered artifacts from the tomb No.20 of Gol Mod site confirm that Xiongnu were deeply related with southern and western far regions. (Ch.Ye.)
MORIN TOLGOI tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. Morin tolgoi site located on the heap of mountain, in 1 km distant from northern bank of Tuul river, 130 km far from Ulaanbaatar to the south-west, in the territory of Altanbulag soum, Tuv aimag. Xiongnu tombs are on the high slope of the
Bone fragment with carved images (scetch), Morin tolgoi site
Bone fragment with carved images



M
137
mountain, back of small heap, invisible to perceive from the west side. Around this site beyond the Xiongnu burials, there are about 130 slab burials with 2 unbroken deer stones and 1 broken deer stone. Slab burials are at open steppe in the front of high mountain heap by 3 lines from the north to the south, but Xiongnu burials are 300 meter distant from these slab burials placed from the south-west to the north-east. There are in total 26 burials which is noted first time by Russian archaeologist G.I.Borovka in 1926. Morin Tolgoi burials excavated many times. The first excavation carried out by Bronze and Iron Age Study team of Mongolian-Soviet Joint Expedition of History and Culture in 1975, and later by Irkutsk University professor V.V.Svinin, who excavated himself, published the archaeological data by the name of “Burials of Rashaan bulag”. After him 2 burials excavated again by Early Bronze and Iron Age Study team of Mongolian-Soviet Joint Expedition of History and Culture in 1983, 4 burials excavated by the Xiongnu Study team, Institute of History, Mongolian Academy of Sciences in 1989, 1 burial excavated by the Mongol-Korean joint “Mon-Sol” project field team in 2000, and 2 burials excavated by the fieldwork team of National History Museum of Mongolia in 2001. (G.E.)
MUGULYU (beginning of 3rd century CE). Founder of the Rouran state. A slave who arrested during the reign of Liwei wang of Tabgach called as Mugulyu. During the Mudi wang reign in Tabgach, he retard for the military mobilization and then escaped from the capital punishment. After that he concealed in Gobi desert and depended on for support of Chongtouling tribe with his a hundred people. His son Guilihou succeeded him. Some scholars consider that Mugulyu is the distant spell of the name of Mongol. (Ts.T.)
MURUN CHUNG or JUNG (319-360 CE). Xianbei origine Murun tribe leader. King of Yan Dynasty of Murun. During his reign his land was extended from the Huanghe river to Korean peninsula. However, he had a Yan king rank when he succeeding his father in 348 CE, but later he proclaimed himself as a king of Yan in 352 CE. He
attacked to the Fuyu state in Manchuria, captured the leader and occupied the capital in 346 CE. He destroyed the Zhao state of Xiongnu and occupied whole north-east China in 350-351 CE. Moreover, he destroyed Duan state in Shandong province in 355 CE. Then he won in the war with Jin dynasty and occupied some lands around Huanghe river in 359 CE. His son Murun Wei succeeded him. (Ts.T.)
MURUN HUANG (297-348). King of Later Yan, originated from Xianbei Murun tribe. His father is Murun Hui, mother was the daughter of Duan Zhe nobleman of Duan tribe, of Xianbei origine. Murun Huang was participated in numerous battles since the young and was destroyed Yuwen tribe in 320 and 325. When he receiving fathers` crown he was the wang of Liaodong. After became the king, he suspected his brothers and chased and killed some of them. He declared himself as a king of Yan in 337. In 338, he attacked in alliance with Xiongnu Zhao state to Duan state, but during the end of war the relation between former allies Yan and Zhao was deteriorated, and finished with Yan victory. In 339 Zhao and Duan states were submitted to Yan. Then started to attack to Yuwen and Koguryo dynasties in 342 and 344. In 341, he moved the capital to the Longcheng and passed away in 348 because of illness. Later, his son created the Empire and granted the title Wenming Emperor of the Yan for his father. (Ts.T.)
MURUN HUI CHANYU (269-333 CE). Leader of Murun tribe of Xianbei origine. Had a rank Xian wang of Liaodong. Initially he was a leader of Xianbei against Jin Dynasty and but in 289 CE he surrendered the former adversary. In order to hold Duan tribes` aggression he bonded by marriage and his sons were born from his Duan spouse. During this period the situation in Jin dynasty was deteriorated by the internal or external attacks, many of peasants immigrated to the land controlled by Murun Hui. He used this circumstances enlarge his supremacy and created his own independent state beside the Jin dynasty. Simultaneously, he attacked to neighboring Xianbei tribes and could increase his military power. Passed away in 333 and his son Murun Huang succeeded him. (Ts.T.)

AIMAA TOLGOI tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. It situates in northeast side of Khunt lake, which is located on the east bank of Khunui river, Erdenemandal soum, Arkhangai aimag. Two parts of Xiongnu tombs are on the front of this hill. First part of the tombs are locating on the higher side of the hill and consisted by smaller tombs. These tombs are in average 3-5.5 meter in diameter, and another part of tombs are bigger than first part and in average 10-13 meter in diameter. One of the peculiarities of this cemetery is some tombs have smaller stone construction like a satellite tomb (for example tombs Nos. 3, 4 and 7) but it is impossible to say if they were burials or not, before the excavation.
The site discovered by Mongol-Hungarian joint archaeological expedition in 1963, and registered all 35 tombs. Also excavated 2 tombs in 1963, 2 tombs in 1964, 5 tombs in 1975 by MongolHungarian joint archaeological expedition, and 11 tombs by the team of Xiongnu study in 1987. (G.E.)
NAVAAN, Dorjpalam (1925-2008). One of the first archaeologists of Mongolia. Was born in Erdenedalai wangiin banner, Khan Khentii aimag by old administration unit in 1925 (present day MunkhKhaan soum of Sukhbaatar aimag). Entered the elementary school in 1937, 1st secondary school in 1946, and completed the full course of Department of History, Moscow University in 1951. He had been worked in Central Museum and Institute of History,



N
140
Mongolian Academy of Sciences, and was a lecturer in the Department of Archaeology and Anthropology, National University of Mongolia during his late career. D.Navaan had been studied early history and archaeology of Mongolia, and specialized on Bronze Age period archaeology. Received Ph.D in 1971, by the thesis of “Bronze age of the Eastern Mongolia”. D.Navaan had been made and excavation at the Naimaa Tolgoi, Duulga Uul and other Xiongnu sites since 1960s, and since 1988, he directed the “Khovd expedition” of the Institute of History, Mongolian Academy of Sciences and excavated the Xiongnu elite tombs at Takhiltyn Khotgor site in Mankhan sum of Khovd aimag. His work “Cultural Heritage of the Xiongnu” (UB, 1999) published by the result of this excavation. (G.E.)
NIJNYI MANGIRTUY settlement. Xiongnu period settlement in Republic of Buriyatia, Russian Federation. Located in the west bank Khilko river and in the influx of the Mangirtuyka stream. It represents a settlement with the remains of the fortifications, and housing structures, similar to Ivolga settlement. Xiongnu type ceramics found in large number. Discovered and studied by L.V.Lbova and E.A.Khamzina in 1987. (Ts.T.)
“NORTHERN DI AND XIONGNU”. The book on early Xiongnu history in Chinese. Author Ma Changshou. Published in Beijing, in 1962. Generally based by written sources about Xiongnu and wrote about history of Northern Di and Tefu, Xianbei and their descendants. Moreover, social structure, ethnogenesis, language and custom of Xiongnu and related nomadic nations are included in this book. (Ch.S.)
NORTHERN LIANG (397-439). Founded by Juqu tribe of Xiongnu. Existed in the territory of North Gansu. Leaders of this tribe were officials of Later Liang Dynasty and ruled by Duan E, who appointed by Later Liang. In 397, Xiongnu leaded by Juqu Mengxun begun the rebellion against Later Liang and persuaded Duan E to lead them. But in 401, they removed the puppet leader Duan E, reigned Mengxun as a leader and declared establishment of their own state. Although Northern Liang state was independent during Juqu Mengxun reign period, but considered as vassal state of Later Qin, Jin and Northern Wei. In 401-412, Northern Liang attacked their neighboring Later Liang continuously and occupied their capital city Gujang finally, and his land extended to the east largely, thus Mengxun declared himself as Hexi wang. Simultaneously, he attacked to the Southern Liang, and after defeating Southern Liang he started to attack to Western Qin.
In 421, he occupied Western Liang territory. Since 423, Rouran attacked to Northern Liang often and since 426 Northern Wei strengthened much and Northern Liang became his vassal. During Northern Liang state the Buddhist caves in Gansu built, including famous Tiandishan and Wenshushan caves. Most of scholars consider that history of Northern Liang state finished after occupying Gujang capital city in 439 by Touba army, but remained Juqu Xiongnu moved to Gaochang (present day Turfan) by the rule of Wuhui and Anzhou and were existed independently until 460. (Ts.T.)
NORTHERN XIONGNU. Xiongnu separated by Northern and Southern in 48 CE because of internal revolt, Bi Chanyu played leading role to this separation. He declared himself as a Chanyu by support of his 50.000 warriors of 8 tribes and attacked to legal Chanyu. In this way a large part of Xiongnu tribes subdued to Han. In 85 and 89, when Northern Xiongnu had defeated by old vassal Xianbei, Han and Southern Xiongnu attacked from the south, Xianbei from the east and Dingling from the north and resulted the lost of the Xiongnu authority under nomadic tribes. Thus last Chanyu of Northern Chanyu moved to the west with his dependents. Some of them remained in Middle Asia under the name of Yueban, most of them reached to Volga and Ural river areas and established the nomadic empire of Hun in eastern Europe in 370s. Also, White Xiongnu or Huna of Middle Asia connects with some ties to Northern Xiongnu. (Ts.T.)
“NORTHERN XIONGNU”. First Xiongnu studies book in Mongolian. Author Ts.Dorjsuren. Published in Ulaanbaatar in 1961. Written by the data of the P.K.Kozlov expedition excavation of Noyon Uul in 1924-1925, authors own study of Xiongnu funeral monuments in 1954-1960s in Mongolia, and previous archaeological materials from Transbaikalia and Chinese historical records. Author divided the Xiongnu funeral monuments into 2 types by structure as elite and common people tomb. Small or common people burials divided into several types as “with wooden chamber”, “with wooden coffin”, “with stone enclosure” and “without additional structure”, as well as explained funeral goods, interior and exterior structure of tombs in basis of their social status. Determined the chronology of the funeral monuments after the relative chronology of artifacts, such as lacquer bowl with inscriptions. Specially dealt the issue of Xiongnu settled life and archaeological discoveries of Xiongnu settlements. Moreover made some suggestions concerning the



N
141
origin of Xiongnu culture in connection with slab grave culture widespread in Mongolia during the Bronze and Early Iron age. This monograph is the first work on the study of Xiongnu, covering every aspects of Xiongnu study, including economy, society and cultural issues. (Na.B.)
NOYON UUL tombs. Xiongnu period elite cemetery in Mongolia. These tombs located on Noyon Uul mountain near Kharaa river, Batsumber soum of Tuv aimag. All about 230 tombs at Sujigt, Zuramt, Khujirt sites of Noyon Uul, and 12 elite, 20 satellite burials are excavated. Noyon Uul tombs discovered unexpectedly in 1912 by A.Ballod, Russian technician of “Mongol Or” mining society, then the P.K.Kozlov expedition for the study of Mongolia and Tibet, excavated 6 elite and 4 small, in total 10 burials in 1924-1925. P.K.Kozlov excavated these tombs neglecting scientific methods, using gold miners “well” method and did not paid any attention to the study of tomb structure. But numerous artifacts of high scientific importance discovered by this excavation and attracted attention of scholars at that time and highly influenced further development of Mongolian archaeology, specially the Xiongnu archaeological study. In September 1924, Soviet professional archaeologists S.A.Teplukhov and G.I.Borovka were sent by Soviet Academy of Sciences in order to determine the scientific importance of Noyon Uul tombs, and to assist P.K.Kozlov excavation in 1925. They excavated with respects of all scientific excavation requirement the Tomb No.24 the following year, which delivered
highly valued information on the structure and form of the elite tomb. In 1926, G.I.Borovka came back to Noyon Uul and excavated a smaller tomb with timber chamber at Sujigtiin Am site and discovered more modest findings including: clay pot, lacquered pot, bronze plaque with horse image, bridle and bridle bit. Same time he transferred some artifacts from the preceding years excavations to the Institute of Letter of Mongolia according to the agreement signed in 1925. In 1926-27, A.D.Simukov, then the researcher of the Institute of Letter of Mongolia was excavated 2 burials at the Sujigt and Zuramt valleys of Noyon Uul and discovered remarkable findings, such as the part of felt carpet, golden plate Noyon Uul excavation



N
142



N
143



N
144
with ornament, plaque gold horse equipment, pieces of silk, and lacquered cup with Chinese inscription. That inscription on the bottom of the lacquered cup demonstrated relative chronology of the tomb around the beginning of Common Era. This cup and the other one from the tomb No.6 are related to the 1st century BCE, and made in Sichuan province of China. Number of articles and books are published on the Noyon Uul tombs research, including S.I.Rudenko fundamental research, still keeping his importance until today. Ts.Dorjsuren, one of first archaeologist of Mongolia excavated at Noyon Uul in 1 elite tomb and 15 small (satellite?) tombs in 1954-1957. Also, Ts.Dorjsuren and Hungarian archaeologist I.Erdely are excavated 2 tombs at Khujirtyn Am and published the result. Since 2006, the joint archaeological expedition of the Institute of Archaeology, Mongolian Academy of Sciences and Institute of Archaeology and Ethnography, Siberian branch of Russian Academy of Sciences have been conducting excavations at Noyon Uul site. Actually they were excavated 4 elite tombs and some other smaller tombs. In 2006 they excavated tomb No.20 at Sujigtyn Am completely. The funeral chamber appeared from the deep of 18.3m, and numerous artifacts as bronze and silver adornments of carriage, piece of carpet, lacquered cup with Chinese inscription and bronze utensils are discovered. These artifacts are basically similar with the artifacts of Noyon Uul site, which are excavated in 1920s. One of the most extragant findings from this tomb was the silver disc adornment with European mythological motive depictions, which demonstrating the trade and cultural relationship with far western regions during Xiongnu period. The same team excavated in 2009 another big elite tomb No.31. (Ch.Ye.)
NUKHTIIN AM tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. Located at the north bank of Ider river, 10 km distant from Galt soum of Khubsgul aimag. Tombs of this site divided into 2 groups and there are about 100 Xiongnu burials with circular stone mounds and 2 deer stones from Bronze age. Discovered in 1975 by Mongol-Soviet joint expedition of History and Culture. They made a site map, and excavated 3 tombs in same year. (G.E.)
Plan of the Nukhtiin am site
Noyon Uul excavation



O
145
O
NITOLT tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. Situated on the south slope of a mountain located in the north of a narrow ravine named Ikher at the beginning of Onitolit gorge oriented to the northeast, in 7 km from Uyanga soum of Uvurkhangai aimag. The northern mountain of this site called Khadan Khoshuuny ridge and it is a part of Khangai mountain range. There are identified in total 17 Xiongnu tombs, aligned from the north to the south direction. Southernmost tombs are lying close to each other forming a group, and northern group of tombs are dispersed from each other. The biggest tomb of this site has is 13 m diameter mound and smallest one is 6-7 m diameter mound. The Xiongnu Study expedition of the Institute of History of the Mongolian Academy of Sciences discovered this site and excavated 1 tomb in 1989. (G.E.)
ORGOITON tombs. Xiongnu period elite and common people mixed cemetery in Buriyatia. It situates in the east bank of Selenge river, in 5 km from
Zarubino village of Dzida district of the Republic of Buriyatia, Russian Federation. Discovered by Y.D.Talko-Gryntsevich in 1897, who identified here 4 elite tombs with dromos, but relatively small sized, and 14 small circular tombs of common people. Moreover he excavated probably 1 tomb at this site. Russian archaeologist N.N.Nikolayev and S.S.Minyaev, Institute of Material Culture of SanktPeterburgh excavated 1 small tomb in 2009. This tomb was looted in the antiquities and a broken piece of bronze mirror has found. (Ch.Ye.)
Orgoiton tomb excavations
Plan of the Onitolit site



O
146
OUTO. Just after that Maodun take Xiongnu throne, he had received several envoys from neighboring Donghu state. Donghu claimed some prerequisite from Maodun Chanyu, including famous horse named “1000 li horse”, Chanyu young wife and finally small parcel of land in border region between Xiongnu and Donghu. Even though Maodun Chanyu fulfilled first two claim of Donghu, he became furious after receiving last demand, and said his legendary word “Land is the basis of the State” and attacked to Donghu unexpectedly. According to Chinese source “Along with the border land they lived by forming Outo”. Mongolian scholar A.Ochir consider that “outo” is the later days “uthia”, and the another Mongolian historian G.Sukhbaatar consider that the Middle age “utku” which recorded in the Rashid al-Din`s “Jami al-Tawarikh”, is the Xiongnu period “outo”. As recorded in a source Xiongnu were moved to the north, after their outo wang taken off by Hans, fearing that he could guide the secret routes through Gobi. (B.B.)
ORAL LAW. Xiongnu oral law was not written, mainly inherited from ancient times orally and had based mostly on tradition to prohibit and tabooing certain actions. According to historical references, Xiongnu had rather strict law with heavy punishments, such as one that uses weapon is be to executed, property of a thief is to be confiscated, those who committed light crime should be thwacked. The length of imprisonment was not more than 10 days and the number of prisoners in whole country did not exceed few in permanence. These characteristic features are typical in nomadic societies, due to absence of bureaucratic tradition and permanent mobility. Probably suspected person was isolated only during the investigation and in fact there was no jailing punishment. Only three major punishment were practiced commonly in Xiongnu justice system, as some scholars suppose: capital punishment for those who take out weapon, property confiscation for robbery and thwacking for light crimes. (Nо.B.)
“OVERVIEW OF THE XIONGNU STUDY IN PRC”. Numerous thousand years neighboring with China, history of the nomadic peoples has been noted and written in Chinese ancient records. Particularly, not underestimating amount of historical records relating to Xiongnu period is records remain in Chinese. After the establishment of the Xiongnu Empire
by the Maodun Chanyu, in 209 BCE, this powerful empire, which was equal adversary to Chinese Qin and Han dynasties established broad relations in matter of economy, politics and culture. During the Chinese Qin, Han, Jin and Wei, number of official chronicles and other types of historical records talking about Xiongnu. After the establishment of the Peoples Republic of China, scientific research on Xiongnu study has took new life, conducted various archaeological, historical study and their results published on various scientific articles and books. For instance: Lin Gan “History of Xiongnu” (1979), “Comprehensive history of Xiongnu” (1986), Togtohu “History of Felt shredding people – Xiongnu nation” (1993), Ma Liqing “Pre-Xiongnu and Xiongnu” (2005), Wu Mu “Historical research of the Xiongnu” (2009), “Brief history of China’s northern nations” (1979), “Scientific articles on Xiongnu history” (1983) thematic literatures and books are published. Herein, briefings from above literatures main concept, research approach and study results. One. Origin of Xiongnu. Chinese historical records are mainly agreed that northern nomadic people are ancestors of the Xiongnu people. However, there are several dissimilar schemes as well. Such as: 1. Xiongnu is descendants of the Guifang (鬼方), Xunyu (獯鬻),
Xianyun (猃狁) tribal states which existed before Chinese Qin and Han dynasty. Particularly, most Chinese scholars are agreeing with Sima Qian’s statement in his text “Xiongnu Chapter”, states that pre-historic time Xiongnu were called Xunyu, in the Zhou period they were called Xianyun and during Qin period called Xiongnu. Additonaly: Chinese scholar Wang Guowei wrote on his text called “Inspection on Guifang (
鬼方), Kun-yi (昆夷), Xianyun (猃狁) says that “the tribal names Guifang (
鬼方), Xunyu (獯鬻), Xianyun (鮮
虞), Rong (戎), Di (狄), Hu (胡) and later it became the Xiongnu”. Some scholars, namely Ma Changshou (马
长寿), Lin Gan (林幹) agreed with above mentioned theory. Lin Gan argues on his book “Comprehensive history of Xiongnu”, “Before the raise of the Xiongnu, several tribes such as Guigang, Xunyu, Xianyun, Rong, Di were existed in the north side of the



O
147
Gobi, and later they all united under the Xiongnu around 4th century BCE, and famed after a century. 2. Some Chinese leading historians believe that Xiongnu people originated from and have close genetic relation with Turkic people. Mongol people are believed the descendants of Donghu, the eastern neighbors of Xiongnu. Chinese scholar Lin Gan has conducted comparative analysis between Mongolian Noyon Uul burial cranial findings and bronze belt buckles with human depictions found in Shaanxi province and made conclusions that main feature of Xiongnu people with their high raised big nasal bones and long hairs was close to Turkic peoples. Also he stated that genetically the Mongols and Xiongnu people are dissimilar even they do not belong to same ancestor, and they do have different languages. According to his study Chinese ancient text books confirm that Mongol and Tatar tribes belong Donghu, they derived from Xibei tribe. Inner Mongolian historian Ikh-Myangadai Renchin, on his research article “Origin of Mongols and Chinese northern frontier peoples”, he criticizing concept of Mongolian origin from Xiongnu and state that Mongols are descendants of Donghu. 3. Few scholars judge that Xiongnu people are autochthones which were settled over the Mongolian plateau since ancient times. For instance, Chinese historian Cao Yongnian on his work published in 1963, “Xiongnu in the Warring States history”, mentioned that Xiongnu was native tribe that has been existed along the mountain ranges situated north of the Gobi desert. Such hypothesis is becoming widely accepted following the deepening research works on Xiongnu archaeology in China since 1980’s. For instance, well known archaeologist Wu En (Unen Yosutu), on his work “Some issues on Xiongnu archaeological research”, he used recent archaeological materials and noted that Xiongnu was native nomadic tribe along the Mongolian highland plateau and it has been
including other more ancient tribes such as Guifang, Xunyu, Xianyun, Rong, Di under its composition. Historian Togtohu studied Chinese ancient texts profoundly and conducted deep analysis of resent archaeological finding and prove that Mongols are descendants of Xiongnu. He published his research result into two volumes “History of Felt shredding people–Xiongnu nation”. Furthermore, Inner Mongolian scholar Daichuudai Mansang did etymological study and made allusion that “Hu-nu” (胡奴) is the native Mongolian word which means “person” or “individual human being” and in oral language it pronounces “humuun”, in plural form it becomes “humunuud”. This study result published on his work “Complete history of Mongols”. Second. Xiongnu native territory. Various Chinese scholars consider that Xiongnu native territory was in present day Inner Mongolian Ordos-Bor tohoi and Yinshan mountain area. Particularly, Lin Gan, on his “Comprehensive history of Xiongnu”, mentioned that native territory where Xiongnu people settled was Bor Tohoi and Yinshan mountain. Moreover, historian Surbadrahu on his book “Origin of Mongolian Nation”, Xiongnu people were born in Yinshan Mountain (Dalan Khar Uul), and then raised along Huanghe river area. Their political center was Tumen city (Tou-man-cheng头曼 城) in Ordos or in present day Wuyuan. Yinshan Mountain, flat steppe of the Huanghe River is famous by its hunting resources and rich pastoral resources for animal husbandry. Elsewhere, historian Togtohu on his book, “History of Felt shredding people – Xiongnu nation”, wrote that Xiongnu people were settled along Huanghe River and Bor tolgoi area and then later on they moved to Moni Mountain area and made it as their political center. Moreover, based on Shiji - Xiongnu liezhuan he made assumption that “Qin conquers other six states and Qin Shihuang emperor mandated Meng Tian to attack Xiongnu in force of his 100.000 solders, and took all territory south of the river”. Meng Tian set fortresses by the Huanghe river, hence Tumen failed to resist Chinese expansion, so he drawback to north. Later on, when Maodun ascend to throne, he achieved to get Qin occupied Xiongnu land and took back Chao-na, Fushi which were located south of the fortified line belonging to Han. From this note he made assumption that Meng Tian occupied land was



O
148
originally belonged to Xiongnu. Therefore, prior to moving north, Xiongnu was pastured over extensive territory which were located southern side of the Huanghe river”. If we could believe Chinese historical sources, Xiongnu people were lived both south and north sides of the Great Wall, Bor Tokhoi steppe, Ordos plateau. Also the historical record says that when Qin Shihuang build the Great Wall he pushed Xiongnu 700 li to the north, which indicates that their further location was around Yinshan mountain area. Three. Xiongnu political and social structure. According to ancient Chinese history in 221 BCE Qin Shihuang united six states under his power and that was the first feudal state in Chine. Almost same time in 209 BCE, Maodun Chanyu, united northern tribes such as Donghu, Dingling, Wusun, Huzhe, and succeed to establish his empire that extended up to Baikal lake to the north, Liaohe river to the east, north side of the Tianshan mountain to the west and Great Wall to the south. In modern Chinese historiography assuming that political structure of the Xiongnu state as: “Xiongnu state is the very first northern nomadic state based on slavery”. Moreover, Lin Gan asserts that “Nature of the Xiongnu society is enslavement”. According to Chinese scholars, Xiongnu state structure divides into three parts. Particularly, first, Chanyu palace as ruling court. It has direct command over middle part of the state. Second, the East Tuqi wang palace, which rules eastern part of the Xiongnu. Third, the West Tuqi wang palace, that rules west part of the Xiongnu. Chanyu is the leader of the Xiongnu people and as well their State. According to Xiongnu people he is called “Son of the Heaven - the Chanyu”. Four. Economy of Xiongnu. Ancient Chinese historical texts mention about Xiongnu economy that they have nomadic life style and the economy essentially based on animal
husbandry. For instance Lin Gan on his “Comprehensive history of Xiongnu”, mentioned that “Xiongnu main economy is based on animal husbandry. They move according to water and pastoral source, hence they have nomadic life style”. Elsewhere, scholars noted that Xiongnu house hold animals mainly horses, cows, and sheep. Additionally, they keep few camels, mules, and their animal husbandry is developed to very advanced level. Another example, chief commander of the Han dynasty has attacked Xiongnu by the Bor tokhoi, Ordos area in 127 BCE and captured about millions of cows and sheep. Besides the animal husbandry, Xiongnu people were practicing hunting, farming, and craftsmanship. Especially, their craftsmanship was developed very advanced level. About this fact, Lin Gan underline that, Xiongnu inherited their technology from Iron Age in 3rd century BCE, following that their technology rapidly upgraded. Various gold, silver, bronze and iron artifacts from diverse archaeological sites indicate their advancement in craftsmanship. For example, an excavation of Xiongnu burial of the Warring States period, in Ar Tsaidam site, Khiangan banner of Inner Mongolia in 1973, has discovered 218 golden artifacts (total of 4000 gram) and five silver artifacts. Among them Xiongnu golden crown was most precious artifact. In a catalog “Historical Heritage of Inner Mongolia” describes the golden crown, the solid golden grown consists of two parts, eagle shaped solid decoration part and the strap part. Main decoration of the crown is open winged eagle and four wolves holding sheeps each one in their mouth. For the glance, total design of the decoration looks like enormous golden eagle observing the wolf snatching a sheep in his mouth. Also the description cited that the crown might have been belonging to Xiongnu leader. (A.P.)



P
149
P
ERLEE, Khodoogiin (19111982). Mongolian historianarchaeologist. Originally his surname is Damdin, but he is famous among the people as Khodoogiin (from Countryside). Was born in Jonon Beisiin banner of Setsen Khan aimag by old administration, present day Binder soum of Khentii aimag, in a herder Damdin family in 1911, and entered the junior school in 1928, teachers school in 1929, and completed the National University of Mongolia in 1949. Since he started to work at the Department of History, of the Institute of Science in 1941, he had been a head of a sector, and senior research fellow of the Institute of History, Mongolian Academy of Sciences for continuously 40 years. Received Ph.D in 1959 by the topic of “Kidans and Their Linkage with Mongols”, and Sc.D in 1978 by his 30 years of scientific works abstracts named as “Some Issues for the History of Nomadic Civilization of Early Mongols”. He elected as a member of Council for International Early History Association in 1962, and in 1967, elected as the Correspondent member of Academy of Sciences of Mongolia. His major research interests as a historian were the problems of history of civilization and early Mongols ethnogenetic issues. He has published influential scientific works in history were: “Mongols of Onon and Kherlen” (1959), “Mongol States and Khanates Prior to Establishing Mongolian Feudal State” (1966) and “The investigation of the Oral History of Three River Mongols” (1969). Kh.Perlee is a pioneer Mongolian archaeologist. He centered his archaeological study in Onon, Kherlen and Tuul rivers basin to join the results for the study of ethnogenesis of Mongolian tribes and their distribution and ethnic constitution.
As an archaeologist he many significant sites as paleolithic camps, petroglyphs, Bronze Age burials, Xiongnu settlements, Turk burials and scripts, Kidan burials and settlements, Mongolian period of rock paintings, and Mongol and Chinese scripts in the Three rivers basin. His first archaeological publication was “Archaeological Study in the Khuitnii Gol basin in Avdarbayan soum, Tuv aimag” (1942). He compared the data of history and archaeology of Three rivers` Mongols and wrote scientific works as “Importance of the Archaeology to Study Mongolian Early Ethnicity” (1956), “Ancient Mongolian Funeral Custom” (1956), “Immigrants from Early Mongol and Their Correlation with History of Migration” (1966), and “Exploring the early Ancestors of the Mongols among the Nomads“(1976). He connected the sacrificing head and scapula tradition in the slab burials with the traditional Mongolian sacrificial customs and demonstrated his view that ancestor of the Mongols were included in slab burial tribes, especially ancestors of the Mongols must be studied from slab burial tribes. Simultaneously, Kh.Perlee analyze early and medieval age of settlements and cities in Mongolia, wrote a monograph “Early and Medieval Age cities and settlements in the Peoples Republic of Mongolia” (1961) by the compilation of the historical archaeological data. Moreover, based by archaeological artifacts and oral data he studied nomadic metallurgy history (since beginning Bronze Age to XX c.), divided metallurgy history, and cleared up the character of development in his work dedicated to the study of Central Asian metallurgy history. Scientific works of Kh.Perlee contributed greatly to the Mongol studies and Central Asian nomadic civilization. (Ch.A.)
PIHOUBA (end of 4th c. CE-402 CE). King of Rouran. Another name is Bitubat. Became ruler of East Rouran after his father Desigun as the eldest son. After the death of his brother Wengedi, he took care of his son Shelun, and unified the Rouran people for certain period. But he captured and killed by Shelun. (Ts.T.)
POLITICAL AND MILITARY STRUCTURE. The political and military structur of Xiongnu as we know from historical records is closely related to founder of Xiongnu imperial organization, Maodun Chanyu. He was born in 234 BCE and founded the Xiongnu Empire in 209 BCE. The years of his rule were 209 BCE to 174 BCE, the



P
150
most powerful period of the Xiongnu Empire. Researchers suggest that the meaning of the word Chanyu is “good man (Sain Khun)” in Mongolian and probably the Chinese source writers mispronounced the word. Also the trace of the “Good man (Sain Khun)” reflects in today’s word Minister (Said). The meaning of these words coincides. The Xiongnu people denominated their Chanyu as Chengli Gutu or the Son of Heaven. It is connected with the Xiongnu people that started to dignify him as a king of the heaven. Maodun Chanyu made significant changes in the political, military and economic structure of the Xiongnu. Shiji describes the Xiongnu political and military changes as following: “When Maodun came to power, however, the Xiongnu reached their peak of strength and size, subjugating all of the other barbarian tribes of the north and turning south to confront China as a rival nation. As a result of this, it is possible to give an account here of the later Xiongnu rulers and of the offices and titles of the nation. Under the Chanyu are the Tuqi Kings of the Left and Right, the left and right Luli kings, left and right Generals, left and right Commandants, left and right Household Administrators, and left and right Gudu marquises. The Xiongnu word for “wise” is “tuqi”, so that the heir of the Chanyu is customarily called the “Tuqi King of the Left”. Among the other leaders, from the Tuqi kings on down to the Household Administrators, the more important ones command 10.000 horsemen and the lesser ones several thousand, numbering twenty-four leaders in all, though all are known by the title of “Ten Thousand Horsemen”. The high ministerial offices are hereditary, being filled from generation to generation by the members of the Huyan and Lan families, and in more recent times by the Xubu family. These three families constitute the aristocracy of the nation. The kings and other leaders of the left live in the eastern sector, the region from Shanggu east to the lands of the Huimo and Chaoxian peoples. The kings and leaders of the right live in the west, the area from Shang Province west to the territories of the Yuezhi and Qiang tribes. The Shanyu has his court in the region north of Dai and Yunzhong. Each group has its own area, within which it moves about from place to place looking for water and pasture. The Left and Right Tuqi Kings and Luli kings are the most powerful, while the Gudu marquises assist the Chanyu in the administration of the nation.
Each of the Xiongnu twenty-four leaders in tum appoints his own “chiefs of a thousand”, “chiefs of a hundred”, and “chiefs often”, as well as his subordinate kings, prime ministers, chief commandants, household administrators, juqu officials, and so forth. In the first month of the year the various leaders come together in a small meeting at the Chanyu’s court to perform sacrifices, and in the fifth month a great meeting is held at Longcheng at which sacrifices are conducted to the Xiongnu ancestors, Heaven and Earth, and the gods and spirits. In the autumn, when the horses are fat, another great meeting is held at the Dailing when a reckoning is made of the number of persons and animals.” Shiji and Hou Han Shu mention about the law of the Xiongnu as follows: “According to Xiongnu law, anyone who in ordinary times draws his sword a foot from the scabbard is condemned to death. Anyone convicted of theft has his property confiscated. Minor offences are punished by flogging and major ones by death. No one is kept in jail awaiting sentence longer than ten days, and the number of imprisoned men for the whole nation does not exceed a handful” . “The Huyan clan of the Left and Lan and Xubu clans of the Right shall be in charge of judge and sentence the offenders and orally report the measurements to the Chanyu; but they do not process documents”. According to this statement administrative and political structures of the Xiongnu are briefly and clearly described. In the times of the prosperity of the Xiongnu, they conquered a broad area that started from the Korean peninsula to Ili Tarvagtai and from Baikal Lake to the Great Wall of China and Ordos. The Chanyu gained control in the central entity and he appointed the Left and Right Tuqi Kings, Left and Right Guli Kings, Left and Right Great Commander, Left and Right Duwei, Left and Right Danhu, Left and Right Guduhou and Left and Right Ten Thousand Chief, Thousand Chief, Hundred Chief and Ten Chief. Also he appointed Dinling King, Donghu King, Huce King and Jiju King in order to control the conquered states including Dinling, Donghu, and Huce. The each division divided into ten thousand, thousand, hundred, and ten, which are the political and military arrangement. This structure let all men of the division to become a solder. The Xiongnu people were pastoral nomads and every mature man should become a soldier and constitute the Xiongnu army. According to the Han historian there was one



P
151
soldier in five people of the Xiongnu. When commanders call the soldiers they must come to the place. If a soldier late then he put to death. The men of the Xiongnu learnt to shoot with bow and arrow from childhood and constantly trained going to hunt together. This system of ten had been the main type of the political structure from the Xiongnu to the Great Mongolian Empire, according to the Mongolian, Chinese, and Persian sources. Seemingly this type of structure was the most suitable and classic type to rule the nomadic tribes. (N.E.)
POLOSMAK, Natalia Viktorovna. Russian archaeologist. Doctor of History, professor, leading research fellow of the Institute of Archaeology and Ethnography Siberian Branch of the RAS, Novosibirsk. From 1990 to 1996, made an investigation of the monuments of the Scythian period on a high plateau of Ukok in Republic of Altai. Opened and explored the undisturbed “frozen” Pazyryk burials, containing numerous items of organic matter. The results of these studies are reflected in a number of collective and monographic works and articles published in Russia and abroad. In 1997 she defended her thesis for the degree of Doctor of Historical Sciences on “Pazyryk culture. Reconstruction of the philosophical and mythological concepts”. In 1998 she was elected a corresponding member of the German Archaeological Institute. In 2004 she was awarded the Russian Federation State Prize in the sphere of science. Winner of National Award of 2007 for the “Property of generations” in the category of high academic standard of archaeological research. In 2008 she was elected an honorary doctor of the Institute of Archaeology of Mongolia. Since 2005 she directed with Prof. D.Tseveendorj a excavation and research project on Xiongnu elite kurgans of Noin-Ula. As a result of the work they excavated and studied four elite kurgans, and made restoration of number of unique findings in Novosibirsk and transferred to the Museum of the Institute of Archaeology. Many of these findings are included in the list of National Heritage of Mongolia. The most important works of N.V.Polosmal are followings: “Vsadniki Ukoka”. Novosibirks, 2001; „Steregushiye zoloto grify”. Novosibirsk, 1994; “Fenomen altaiskikh mumii”. Novosibirsk, 2000; “Arkhyeologicheskie pamyatniki ploskogorʹya Ukok (Gornyi
Altai)”. Novosibirsk, 2004; “Kostyum i tekstilʹ pazyryktsev Altaya”. Moskva, 2005. (N.E.)
PSARRAS, Sophia-Karin. American Sinologist and independent scholar. Graduated from the University of Toronto (Trinity College) specialized in Chinese Studies in 1984. Received Diplôme d’Etudes Approfondies (preliminary doctorate) with mention cum laude from the Université de Paris VII in Chinese Studies, defended her Diplôme de Doctorat (Ph.D.) with the mention summa cum laude from the Université de Paris VII in Chinese Studies with a dissertation “Chinois et Xiongnu à l’époque Han: rapports culturels, rapports politiques” in 1990. Her series of publications on the Xiongnu archaeology, art, history and its relation with Han China contributed significantly on the understanding of its culture. Some articles and quasi monographs including: “Han and Xiongnu: A Reexamination of Cultural and Political Relations,” Part I: Monumenta Serica 51 (2003), 55-236; Part II: Monumenta Serica 52 (2004), 37-93; “Pieces of Xiongnu Art,” Central Asiatic Journal 40.2 (December 1996), 234-259; “Xiongnu Culture: Identification and Dating,” Central Asiatic Journal 39.1 (June 1995), 102-136; “Exploring the North: Non-Chinese Cultures of the Late Warring States and Han,” Monumenta Serica 42 (1994), 1-125; “Redating Xiongnu Culture,” in: The International Academic Conference of Archaeological Cultures of the Northern Chinese Ancient Nations/ Zhongguo gudai beifang minzu kaogu wenhua guoji xueshu yanjiuhui (Hohhot: Neimenggu kaogu yanjiusuo, 1992), vol. 3; “The Xiongnu and the Later Han: A Reappraisal,” in: Bernard H. K. Luk, editor, Contacts Between Cultures (Lewiston, N. Y.: Edwin Mellen Press, 1992), vol. 4, 82-85; “Chinois et Xiongnu à l’époque Han,” Dialogues d’histoire ancienne 16.1 (July 1990), 161-169. (Ts.T.)
PULIHOU River. Geographical name mentioned in history. In 72 BCE, during the Han attack to Xiongnu made by the force of 160.000 Han warriors the name of this river mentioned once. Han army attacked by 5 ways including 30.000 warriors headed by Fan Mingyu the general of Duliao, departed from Jang’ye passed through 1200 li (about 480 km) and attacked to Xiongnu near Pulihou river, seized 700 people and 10 thousand cattle. Pulihou river might possibly been in the north of Alasha region of Inner Mongolia. (B.B.)



Q
152
PULEI Lake. Geographical name mentioned in history. Scholars consider that the name of this lake means “barus or bars” and identified as the present day Barkul lake. According to the sources, some of nobles of Xiongnu were lived near this lake in order to block Han army advancements to oppress western city states. For example, in 151 CE Xiongnu Huyang wang attacked to Hamil, Hang officer Mao Kai was fought him near the Pulei lake and defeated. (B.B.)
PUNU River. Geographical name mentioned in history. In 90 BCE Han army headed by Shanqiu Cheng directed toward Xiongnu with 30.000, reached in Zhuxieqing but did nor encounter any Xiongnu army and back to his land. But general Li Ling, who betrayed to Xiongnu, pursued him with 30.000 Xiongnu cavaliers and fought for 9 days until reaching Punu river. Punu river was
probably in the north of Yingshan mountain. (B.B.)
“PRE-XIONGNU AND XIONGNU: ARCHAEOLOGICAL STUDY OF HISTORY AND CULTURE”. Xiongnu studies scholarly book in Chinese. Author Ma Liqing, published in Huhhot, 2005. The author introduced the culture and history of Xiongnu systematically, compared with early sources and archaeological data and used previous scholars’ works. Moreover, wrote about character of Xiongnu culture by the archaeological data and tried to classify the Xiongnu culture by region and development stages. Also she compared Xiongnu in Mongolian territory and neighboring regions, and revealed his opinion of changing the borderland, social structure, and urban and economic development of Xiongnu. (Ch.S.)



Q
153
Q
IANGUN. The name of historical nation mentioned in history. Probably a Turkic speaking people lived in the north west of Xiongnu. The name also happens to be written as “Giangun”. They lived at the beginning in the North-west of Mongolia, and then moved to the Yenissei river basin during Xiongnu empire. Later, the word was recorded in historical works in the form of “Jiegu”, “Hegu” and “Hegusi”. Most scholars consider them to be the ancestors of Kirgiz people. In 208 BCE, Maodun Chanyu invaded north west and took control over the Qiangun. (Ts.T.)
QIANQUI CHANYU (179-188 CE). South Xiongnu Chanyu. Chanyu originated from Huyan tribe, which related with Luandi clan through marriage alliance during Xiongnu Empire. Before becoming Chanyu, he was a West Tuqi wang. Chang Su, Chinese officer responsible of South Xiongnu murdered his father Huzheng Chanyu and suggested to appoint Qianqui for the throne. Even though Chang Su was executed for his crime, Han court approved the appointment of Qianqui for Chanyu. Han could not interfere in inner affaires of South Xiongnu before Chang Su assassinated South Xiongnu Huzheng Chanyu. Following their tradition Chanyu was selected by Xiongnu people and noblemen. So after appointment of Chanyu from Han court Xiongnu people protested against it. Qianqui Chanyu ignored protests from his people and forced the army to enter into war following orders from Han government bearing great loss. So in 188, Yilo and other noblemen from South Xiongnu killed Qianqui Chanyu, puppet of Han court. Han government appointed his son Yufuluo Chanyu, who was the last Chanyu of South Xiongnu. (Ts.Т.)
QILIAN Mountain. Mountain mentioned in history in relation with Xiongnu history. Identified as the Rihthofen mountain range in actual day Gansu, China. Also called Tenger Uul Mountain, Tsagaan Uul Mountain. The mountain was under the Xiongnu Western wing rulers as Xuchu wang and Hunye wang. This land is
historically also named Little Yuezhi. It believed that before the rise of Xiongnu the Yuezhi nation used to live in this land. In 177-166 BCE the land was taken by Xiongnu. As a result of two big wars started by the General Huo Qubin around 121 BCE, Qilian Mountain area was overtaken by Han. Xiongnu people used to sing that they lost their Qilian Mountain and their livestock ceased to increase. (B.B.)
QIN EMPIRE (221-206 BCE). The first empire of China. The name China derived from the name Qin. Basic territorial border, administrative structures are inherited to latter empires from Qin. Chinese writing was elaborated and Chinese Great Wall was built during Qin Empire. However, many ancient books were burnt in order to avoid annoyance of the first emperor Shihuangdi. After 3 years following death of Shihuangdi, farmers uprising overturned the government and Empire and Han state was founded. Military leader of Qin Empire, Men Tian attacked Xiongnu and overtook their native land Ordos and Yingshan Mountain and pushed them back to the north. Due to this nomadic tribes had to unify to build the first empire under Xiongnu leadership. (Ts.T.)
QIN SHI HUANGDI (259-210 BCE). Founder of Qin Empire. Personal name is Zhao Zheng. His father was king of Qin. Qin state was known for its cruelty among central Chinese states, but based on the state philosophy of legism he succeeded to build strong bureaucracy. In 221 BCE, with Li Zhi’s support Zheng managed to unify other 6 states and created Qin Empire. He named himself Shi Huangdi (first emperor) and started reform to put up entirely centralized governance. He was interested in spells, charms and alchemistry (tried to turn iron into gold) and looked for water to reach immortality and executed many Confucians who criticized the emperor for these deeds. Ancient histories described him as a cruel, rude, skeptical and evil minded person, but modern historians highlight his contribution to build a strong bureaucratic administration. Even though Qin Empire was ended, the following states inherited the centralized administrative structure. Shi Huangdi



Q
154
was buried with 8000 terracotta warriors in an enormous tomb. He thought that the main threat to Qin Empire was from the north and had aggressive policy toward them. In 215 BCE, he started war against Xiongnu and conquered native land from them, afterwards spend colossal resources to build Great Wall to protect northern border, which later was one of the reasons of the fall of Qin Empire. (Ts.T.)
QUIVER. A long case to carry arrow and arrowhead in. It is made of leather, felt, wood and birch-bark. In the Xiongnu burials some quivers made of birch-bark were found. In 12-14th c CE, the Mongols usually used a birch-bark quiver to carry their arrow-heads and arrows. The birchbark is a common material which is light, thin and resistant in dampness. It has two kinds: one is open and another has a cap, and can contain 20-30 arrows. The Xiongnu military equipment especially the quiver is an uncommon archeological finding. A birch-bark quivers
have been found from the satellite tomb of the Xiongnu elite grave of the Gol Mod site and from the tomb No.2 of the Duurlig Nars. The quiver in the Gol Mod site was broken down and the half was disappeared by the plunder. It is approximately 71.3 cm in length and 13.1 cm in width on the top but 9.6 cm at the bottom. However, the quiver in the Duurlig Nars site is a long with a red lacquer patterns on black background. It was put along the body. Except for them, a soldier carrying a bow and arrow and riding on a horse is displayed in a rock painting at the Yamaan Us. The researchers prove it is dated to the Xiongnu period. On the painting, the quiver is fastened on the back. So the quiver was carried on the back to make it easy to withdraw the arrow over the shoulder at any battle. This way of carrying was more common among the Xiongnu than other ancient nomadic tribes. Although the birch-bark quiver is not kept well as before, it is an important fact for studying the Xiongnu military weapons. (N.E.)



R
155
R
EBELLION OF FIVE HU (Rebellion of Five Barbarians). The 5 nomadic nations` rebellion against Jin dynasty in Northern China. Hu is the Chinese offensive name of nomadic nations. Xiongnu, Xianbei, Jehu, Di and Qian altogether five nations rebelled against China. The reason was deep oppression for nomadic nations from the Jin state. The rebellion was headed by one of leader of Xiongnu Liu Yuan, rebel power enlarged speedily and attacked to Luoyang in 309. The army of Jin lost 100.000 warriors near the Shangtong city in 310, and lost the capital and Jin emperor was captured by rebellions in 311. Chinese tried to enthrone new emperor in Chang`an city, but Liu Chung, son of Liu Yuan occupied the city. Countless Chinese people could not support nomadic nations domination and moved away to the south. The rebellion ended successfully and the whole Northern China, north of the Huai river controlled by nomadic people. Jin dynasty moved to the south and built new capital city near present day Nanjing. In this way, Five Hu or Alien 16 states` period continuously existed until 436 in Chinese history. (Ts.T.)
“RECENT ARCHAEOLOGICAL SURVEYS IN INNER MONGOLIA”. Article in “Kaogu xue bao”, 1983, No1. Author Tian Guangjing. Author divided the Xiongnu culture by 3 stages as Bronze Age, Early Iron Age and Iron Age and he wrote that it is proper that Bronze Age culture of Shang Dynasty (14-11th c. BCE) which discovered in Ordos have a connection to Xiongnu culture of Han period, thus Xiongnu culture originated from Ordos Bronze culture. Furthermore, Guifang, who were in the north of Shang dynasty and nomadic tribes of Xianyun, North Di were the ancestors of the Xiongnu. (Ch.S.)
“RECORDS OF THE GRAND HISTORIANCHAPTER OF XIONGNU”. Written by Sima Qian, historian of ancient Chinese Western Han dynasty and 110th chapter of his fundamental work is dedicated for Xiongnu. Chapter of Xiongnu contains 4 parts:
Part I: Affiliation between Han and Xiongnu, Xiongnu major accounts, Xiongnu custom and social structure included in this part. Part II: Contains history of fighting between Han and Xiongnu in the beginning of Han dynasty and their peaceful relationship. Part III: During many years of fighting of Xiongnu and Han, Han dynasty became more powerful than Xiongnu while Wudi Emperors reigning. Part IY: Authors view about the fighting of Wudi Emperor and Xiongnu contained in this part. This book is the unique source of Chinese historical literature, and considered most prominent source about history of Xiongnu. (Ch.S.)
RELIGION. According to archeological research, it is clear that there was common burial ritual among Xiongnu and they had common perception about religion and belief. Xiongnu people perceived that deceased ones would continue to live in their afterlife, herding their livestock, eating and drinking as in real life. Decorative articles, weapons, utensils, pottery, chopstick for eating, also the most necessary livestock in afterlife, such as cow, sheep, goat and dog were buried with deceased. Also tools that were used by deceased before death, such as spinning wheel, pottery making tools were buried with them, which indicate the common set perception of afterlife. According to historical records, Xiongnu Chanyu prayed to the sun in the morning and to the moon in the evening. Not only nobility, but also ordinary men worshiped sun and moon and had “sun”, “moon” shaped pattern was main decoration. Faith of Xiongnu people in religion, totemism, animism, magic derives from early ages, but was evolved through time. For example, articles found in burials, such as bronze mirrors, pottery were intentionally chipped to separate spirit from these objects; Xiongnu believed that their ancestors were some mystical animal and pattern with animals are common in art. There were special mountains for worship and offering and special persons responsible to carry out these rituals. Only black animals and black flags were used in these rituals. South Chanyu carried out special offerings and worshiping rituals for Maodun Chanyu and the cost was equal with worshiping rituals of the sky. G.Sukhbaatar wrote, there



R
156
were special places of offerings and worships, all mountains and hills of Longcheng city were worshiped and those might have been smaller mountains like Gua Dov. But Z.Batsaihan doesn’t agree that Gua Dov to be considered small worshiping place, Gua Dov according to him must have been a center for state worshiping, in considering Xiongnu fortress in the valley of Kherlen river where some traces of offerings are discovered by archaeological incestigations. Kh.Perlee also thought these fortresses were places for worship and offering. Ts.Dorjsuren and G.Sukhbaatar defined a hat with unique shape found from the Tomb No.6 of Noyon Uul as a hat of shaman. There is no doubt in the conclusion of these researchers, but it is interesting that a shaman hat was found in elite tomb. Main duty of Xiongnu Chanyu was to protect its border and territory, appoint nobilities, carry out war and make peace agreement, also they were to lead state offering ceremonies to worship spirits and to communicate directly with sky and turn messages of sky into law and reinforce its implementation. From the title of Chanyu – chenli gutu, which means “son of heaven”, ideology behind power and privilege of Chanyu was defined. Also Chanyu carried out their duty of governing of their tribal state along with the duty to lead rituals, which is derived from period of tribal society and it is clear from the research on some words of Indo-Iranian language. Also the decoration with image of mystical animals, such as dragon, unicorn antelope, and ox in clouds prove the above assumption. One of the main parts of Xiongnu religion is sacrificing cow, horse, goat and sheep. In some burials, cattle, skull of cattle with three short ribs, tail and four foot put in certain order, specifically three short ribs were placed under skull, behind it, tails and two front foot were placed symbolizing that animal is alive. During Bronze period horse played important role in livelihood of nomads and worshipping of horses were widespread. But later on during Xiongnu period, cattle became sacred and worshiped animal. Studies show that Xiongnu worshiped land, sky, sun, moon and ancestors. In addition, altered version of totemism, animism and magic co-existed. There must have been shamans responsible for running public places for worship, sacrificing was ancient ritual developed in relation to husbandry carried out during offering ceremonies and funerals. But some ancient religious rituals turned into tradition. (Ch.Ye.)
RELIGIOUS CENTERS. In ancient Chinese records, it is mentioned that Xiongnu had special places to gather annually to worship their nature and carry out offering ceremonies. According to Xiongnu Chanyu order, centers for shamanistic offerings were established in many places, some were still operating until late Xiongnu period on the Huanghe river valley, south of Ordos territory. During Wudi Emperor period of Han, Xiongnu was defeated and separated into two parts, some moved to west and some submitted to China. During that time among many who got captured by Chinese, there were many shamans. Han Wudi believed in shamanism and constructed “Jing-Lu” monastery for shamans, according to historical records. This shows that shamanism was wide spread in Xiongnu and shamans had higher position in society. Nobles believed in shamanism and supported the establishments for shamans to carry out rituals and offering ceremonies. Out of around 10 ruins of settlements discovered on the territory of Mongolia, researchers think many of them have connection to the above mentioned religious centers. There were many religious establishments in Xiongnu territory. In 1952, Kh.Perlee concluded after excavating partially Gua Dov ruin, and some other fortresses, that those were offering places. According to Kh.Perlee, “Gua Dov and other constructions did not last long and there is no remain of household activity, which indicate that Xiongnu gathered in these places in January, May and September to carry out offering ceremony and during other times of the year only few persons in charge of the objects used in sacrifices were left. Gua Dov is a place of worship for Xiongnu and for burial of their ancestors.” No household artifacts were found during excavation of square burial in Tereljiin Dorvoljin, Burkhiin Dorvoljin sites which indicate that these were place of offering and sacrifice like Gua Dov. It is clear that Longcheng, legendary capital of Xiongnu was a big administrative, political and religious center. Many constructions were built for religious rituals on the territory of Xiongnu and it is likely that more ruins of religious centers might be discovered. Xiongnu religious centers were not excavated and studied comprehensively and this would be done in order to study belief and religious rituals of Xiongnu people. (Ts.Т.)



R
157
“REPORT OF DAODUNZI XIONGNU TOMBS EXCAVATION IN TONGSHIN COUNTY, NINGXIA AUTONOMUOS REGION”. Kaogu, 1987, No1, published by Ningxia museum. It situated on the east terrace of Daodunzi village, 8 km from Wangtuang center of Tongxin county of Ningxia Autonomous region of China. This site was discovered by the local peasants during terracing works in 1983 and excavated by Cultural heritage organization 2 times all 32 tombs in 1983 and 1985. Six tombs of these were niche burials. During the excavation many artifacts were discovered, including decorated pot, beads, leather quiver, wushu coin (Spring-Autumn State and War state), bronze bell, bronze knife, animal shaped belt decoration and horse bones are discovered. These tombs related to the middle to the end of the Western Han dynasty on the basis of analyses of archaeological data. (Ch.S.)
“REPORT ON THE RUIN OF CITY AND TOMB DATED TO HAN DYNASTY”. The Institute of Archeology in Shanxi province: Beijing, 2001. A book which contains the report of the archeological excavation and research on the burial site and ruin of city dated to Han and Xiongnu period, discovered near Dabaodan village, Shengmu county, Shaanxi province, in 1996-1998. It informs the result of the excavation and research on 26 graves with a line engraved stone sculpture (16 have sculpture) and the ruin of city located close to the burial site. This comprehensive report contributes for the historical and archaeological studies on the northern nomads as it has contained well documented sketch-map and imprints from the sculptures. (Ch.S.)
“RESEARCH ARTICLES ON THE XIONGNU”. Author Lin Gan. Beijing, 1983. The collection of articles about the Xiongnu published in China from 1919 to 1979 was compiled and published. It is consisted of articles about the Xiongnu history, for instance, the Xiongnu social structure, and relationship between the Xiongnu and Han state. It used the Xiongnu archaeological research information excavated in the territory of Mongolia and Soviet-Russia prior to 1961. (Ch.S.)
RONG. Chinese common name of group of ancient nomadic tribes in Central Asia. Recorded in Chinese historical narratives from Zhou dynasty period (1123-221 BCE). Lived in vast
areas of north western side from Chinese central plain. Their ethnicity is the subject of discussion. Mainly referred as proto-mongol or proto-tangut tribes, but in Chinese historical tradition still has strong reference that the Rong people had colored eyes and rich beard. Probably this kind of anthropological type was dominant in actual Qinghai and Gansu ancient peoples until the 3-4th centuries CE, when the mongoloid features became major anthropological type. In various historical records, dozens of Rong tribes were mentioned, as following, Shan Rong (Mountain Rong), Bei Rong (Northern Rong), Xi Rong (Western Rong), Quan Rong (Dog Rong). The most prominent among them was Northern Rong and the Mountain Rong. They were mentioned in several occasions as their attack on Zhen State in 714 BCE, and on Qi State in 704 BCE. Mongolian historians suggests that these two tribe located in northern part of China could belongs to proto-mongol population. The hieroglyph Rong means “warring”, “warrior” and also “battle” or “weapon” in Chinese. Most scholar consider Rong tribes as hunters and semipastorals, distinct from Han culture of Central Chinese plains. (S.U.)
ROURAN EMPIRE. The name Nirun or Rouran was noted first in historical sources at the beginning of the 4th century CE. The Rouran, considered as the proto-Mongols is originated from Xiongnu in some sources, and from Donghu or Xianbei by other ones. According to historical records, The King of Rouran Anagui considered himself to be same ancestors with the empire Great Wei dynasty of Tuoba. During the Wei dynasty Mudi emperor (304-316 CE), one slave named Mugulu escaped from Tuoba and leaned on Chintulin tribe for support who lived behind Gobi. Mugulu’s son Guiluho (Charugui) was a brave warrior. Thus, he united several tribes into a bigger federation and they named themselves as Rouran. First, they paid taxes to the Wei dynasty. They gave horses, livestock, animal fur and skin including skin of foxes and sables for tax to the Wei. In winter, they settled down in south of Gobi. In summer, they settled down behind the Gobi. After Charugui, several kings were on the throne, Rouran people constantly fought against Wei to free themselves from their hegemony. It is the King Shelun (402-410 CE) who united several Rouran tribes and founded the state. The King Shelun emerged as a key figure among other prominent lords of many



R
158
other tribes and distinguished with his agility and smart policy. Shelun gave himself the title The King Deidai in 402 CE, and later kings of Rouran used different titles. In the same year, Rouran defeated Huliu tribe of Tele people. Moreover, Rouran state became more powerful after it defeated Zhibaizhi, the leader of the remaining tribe of Xiongnu that was inhabited in the Northwest of Rouran. They began to attack the Wei dynasty which was located in the South of Gobi. Shelun united all nomadic tribes and invaded High carts (Uighur), Turks, West region oasis states as Hami, Karashar (Ansi, Yanzi), Turfan, Cheshi, Kucha and held tributes from these countries. Shelun not only made external expansions, but also made domestic structural changes. They adopted the administration structure, traditional for nomadic states inherited from Xiongnu and Xianbei. Like Xiongnu and Xianbei, Rouran had a decimal administration and military organizational structure. In addition, it was the most appropriate, classical form to organize nomads into state-military organizational pattern. Thus, ranging from Xiongnu to all Mongolian nomadic states such as Xianbei and Rouran adopted this organizational pattern and it was a main type of the state and military organizational pattern during whole nomadic history. (S.U.)
RUDENKO, Sergey Ivanovich (18851969). Russian prominent archaeologist and anthropologist. He had been participated in
the ethnographic and anthropological fieldworks in Siberia from his student time. He made his first fieldwork in 1924, in Altai and discovered famous Pazyryk elite tombs, which he has excavated in 1929. He was repressed in 1931 but returned to research work in 1942. In 1954 he established the Laboratory of archaeological technology in the Institute of Archaeology, Soviet Academy of Sciences. His most important scientific works, are followings: “Art of Scythians of Altai” (1949), “Mountainous Altai Findings and Scythians” (1952), “Art of the people of Central Altai in Scythian Period” (1960), “Art of Altai and the Middle East” (1961), “Xiongnu Culture and Noin Ula tombs” (1962). (M.Ts.)
RUODI. Typical additional title of Xiongnu Chanyu in later period. Chinese commentators explaining this work as Xiao- meaning the filial piety and/or fulfilling parents will. This hieroglyph was commonly used within the title of Chinese emperors. If the Xiongnu used the Ruodi title in referring the Chinese title Xiao, there is no discussion on the meaning of the word as it was in Chinese. (No.B.)



S
159
S
ACRIFICIAL CUSTOM. Some written sources and archeological materials prove that Central Asian nomads buried the deceased with some accompanied items of offerings separately. This customs is expressed by a Mongolian term khoilogo. This term Khoilogo is explained in different views but most common explanation is “to let go”. According to some researches, they made an offering and let the dead escort by animals since the Bronze Age. Consequently, it is a proof that from this time animal husbandry became main economic activity of the ancient population in Mongolia. Also all the graves like slab grave, Pazyryk tombs, Xiongnu (of elite and ordinary peoples), Turkic, Uighur and Middle Age graves, have been followed by the death-escort ceremony for all the ages through keeping the tradition on one hand but being renewed on the other hand. Xiongnu people launched to put some parts of the animal as a representation of whole animal. They slaughtered horse, flayed its skin, and wrapped its bones including head, neck, back, four legs, and the five entrails such as heart, lung, gullet and liver in the skin, named Zuld. They put the Zuld because of their consideration that it could represent such
animals entirely. Also non-domestic sacrificial animals such as bird, antelope, deer and wild ass were put to escort the deceased. An interesting case is the custom related to the dog accompany in Xiongnu burials. The Xiongnu graves, accompained by the dog have been found at the Burkhan Tolgoi, Baruun Khairkhan and Derestuy in Mongolia and Transbaikalia. (Ts.T.; No.B.)
SADDLE. In 200 BCE, when Xiongnu Modun Chanyu and Han Gaodi Emperor fight in Baiden Mountain, Xiongnu piled their saddles as high as wall. Also, according to Hou Han Shu records, during the end of 1st c. CE Xiongnu attacked Chinese
and they tied heads of executed locals from saddles and put women behind themselves. G.Sukhbaatar concluded that “Xiongnu saddle was made of hard material (wood). Since saddle made of soft materials would not make a big pile with significant height, also soft saddle, i.e. cushion are not for carrying things, if you hang things from cushion, it would fall to one side”.
Egyin Gol site, tomb 60b excavations
Noyon Uul, Tomb 6



S
160
The only artifact so far discovered in relation to the study of Xiongnu saddle is two arches of saddle made of attached 4 wooden sticks, found in the Tomb No. 6 of Noyon Uul. The distance between two arches, in other words seat size is 50 cm. Arch is upholstered with leather and on top of it there is coarse velvet upholstery with embroidery of animal images of woolen colorful threads. G.Sukhbaatar used this artifact to assert the existence of wooden saddle, but S.I.Rudenko on the contrary thought this object was harness saddle for horse to pull carts. S.I.Rudenko wrote that “There was no saddle during Xiongnu period, no stirrup, but there could have been cushion filled with wool and grass, like the ones found in the burials of Altai Pazyryk culture. Saddle girth was attached with bone buckle in Altai style”. Ts.Dorjsuren also concludes that this object is a saddle, and straps holes for stirrup and girth just like modern Mongolian saddle. Xiongnu saddle was bigger, which was more comfortable for a rider and a horse. Ts.Dorjsuren wrote that the saddle discovered in the tomb No.6 of Noyon Uul is only real artifact proving the existence of saddle during Xiongnu period. Z.Batsaihan concluded that the artifact is a saddle and it is the oldest wooden saddle known. It can be concluded that Xiongnu scholars have different opinion on this particular artifact found in the Tomb No.6 of Noyon Uul. At this moment it is not possible to conclude that Xiongnu had wooden saddle based on plausible evidence. However, there might be new artifact that would shed light into this study. Under that wooden shoohoi (small saddle), there was padding made from thick felt and on of it a cushion. Two layered padding (cushion) were discovered from the tomb No.6 and the remains of these were found stuck on the wooden shoohoi. Underneath padding is made of double layer: white felt of 57×65 cm size trimmed with colorful cotton. On top of it there is a bigger cushion of 65×65 cm, one side of which is made of thick leather and the other side made of woolen textile material. The side made of leather is decorated with lacquer black paint. (Ts.Т.)
SANTIIN SHIREE Settlement. Xiongnu period settlement in Mongolia. Located in Bayan soum of Tuv aimag. Mongolian archaeologist Kh.Perlee discovered in 1968, and gathered some coustruction materials as earthen ware and roof ceramics. Kh.Perlee wrote that there was a roof ceramic tiles manufactured locally in this site.
Also there was found piece of jade with bird figure, but the size of this jade did not noted down. (Ts.T.)
SETTLEMENTS OF ARTISANS AND AGRICULTURES. Although most of Xiongnu people were nomads a few of them lived in relationship with cities and urban area. They found settlement and lived there processing and making pottery, metal ware, husbandry origin raw materials necessary for their living. Kh.Perlee considered that “Manufacturing was one branch of economy in Xiongnu... Manufacturing develops in relationship with agriculture, trade and settlement...“. Among Xiongnu people, artisans who were partly connected with herdsmen must have lived and worked by building houses. He also mentioned that it had been impossible to determine which one was connected with manufacturers due to the fact that the Xiongnu urban life research has just had its beginning on the territory of our country. However, as a result of the latter years researches trace of settlement where people were engaged in manufacturing was revealed among the cities and settlement which were found during the archaeological excavations done on the main territory of Xiongnu. These settlements and villages including Boroo, Ivolga and Dureny village were located on the valleys of rivers. It became clear that Boroo settlement had been one of the big centers of hand crafting, pottery making and manufacturing of Xiongnu. After one of our first archaeologist Ts.Dorjsuren found this village in 1962, there was test excavations in 1990 which proved that the village was of great significance for studying the Xiongnu culture. In 2005-2007, Mongolian- Swiss archaeologists unearthed and revealed trace of the fact that they manufactured pottery, ironware and were engaged in agriculture. (Ts.T.)
SETTLEMENT OR CITIES. The first settlements began to be built in Central Asia from the Bronze Age, but the first permanent villages discovered in the land of Mongolia are dated back to the Xiongnu Period. The most studied one is the settlement of the Ivolga, located close to Ulaan-Ude, Buriatia. It is considered to be the settlement which was most largely excavated. Kh.Perlee, the pioneering researcher of Xiongnu settlements or cities in Mongolia, noted in his book entitled “The Review of Cities and Settlement Dated to the Ancient and Middle Periods in Mongolian Peoples Republic” which was published in 1961, but still do not lost its



S
161
actuality until now, the result of the research on the settlements or cities of the Xiongnu period, through classifying and describing them. Also he used some Chinese sources as well to identify the Xiongnu settlements and clarifying their function on Xiongnu society. He wrote in this book about three cities (Longcheng, Zhaoxincheng and Fangfuzhencheng) with their unknown locations but only names noted in some Chinese sources, 9 walled settlements which he excavated and determined their dates, the Ivolga settlement in Buriatia and the Abakan Palace in Khakasia. In 1966, Mongolian famous archeologist and Xiongnu researcher, Ts.Dorjsuren discovered the settlement of the Boroo. Mongolian archaeologists excavated later this settlement in a small scale and moreover the ruin of Bayanbulag, located in Nomgon soum of Umnugobi aimag in years of 1987 and 1990. Recently the excavations continued on Boroo Settlement between 20052007, and the Tereljiin Durvuljinsince 2007. The archeological research work on the settlements or cities dated to the Xiongnu period in Buriatia began in the end of 1920s. In 1927,
V.V.Popov excavated half of a small dwelling inside the Ivolga settlement which is named after the river Ivolga, located in 16 km in the south of Ulan-Ude. In 1928-1929, the BuriatMongolian expedition led by G.P.Sosnovsky finished the excavation of the dwelling excavated by V.V.Popov and explored another three small dwellings. In the same years, Yu.D.TalkoGryntsevich and G.F.Debets excavated three dwellings in the Dureny settlement. In 1929, the Second BuriatMongolian expedition led by A.P.Okladnikov began their research work on the settlements or cities dated to the Xiongnu period. A field excavation team led by V.P.Shilov and A.V.Davydova excavated the Ivolga settlement in 1949-1950 and discovered thousands of findings. In 1955, the team directed by A.V.Davydova continued the excavation in Ivolga and in the years of 1955-1956 and1961-1962, they did large scale excavation and research work on this site, especially on its structure and organization and found numbers of findings related to the culture, economy, handcraft and social system of Xiongnu. Then they published the result of the research work in special volume. Recently, some monuments such
Roof tiles, Tereljyn durvuljin site
Ceramic face board, Gua dov site
Roof tile, Gua dov site



S
162
