These decorative ornaments had varying ornaments depending on the status of the aristocrats; these also differed by weight and size – in particular, weight categories of 50, 70 and 80 liǎng (1 liǎng, or tael, equals to 37.3 g) existed for these decorations. These items were unearthed from tombs which also contained lacquerplated chariots, further evidencing their utility as equestrian decoration. The positioning of these ornaments inside tombs, namely to the left or right of the inner chamber, is also not coincidental. In order for a horse to pull a carriage, wagon, or sleigh, a reliable and practical harness is needed. With the advent of saddles emerged a need for broad strappings to firmly hold the saddle under the horse’s barrel, and thus emerged the breast-strap and crupper. From the above-mentioned information, we may assess that the rounded-shaped decorative silver ornaments were attached to breast-straps and the elongatedshaped ones were attached to cruppers. Stylized depictions of various beasts are present not only on silver ornaments but also in many occasions sewn onto felt rugs or carved onto bone decorations of the Xiongnu. The presence of decoration with mythical beasts in the noble tombs of the Xiongnu might be connected to their
Unicorns, Gol mod, Tomb 20



U
186
revered position in society. On the other hand, the presence of such beasts within tombs could also be related to perceptions of the afterlife among the Xiongnu (see Martin, this volume). Some scholars believe that these depictions can be interpreted by the cosmological myths of the Xiongnu. Besides the “belget guroos” (or “unicorn”), silver artifacts unearthed from the Xiongnu noble tombs also feature yaks, deer, mountain gazelles and dragons. Identical decorative silver ornaments discovered in tomb 6 at Noyon Uul site depict what is generally believed to be a yak, but some scholars perceive it as a stylized depiction of a predator. A silver ornament depicting a single-horned antelope excavated recently from Tomb 20 at Noyon Uul is similar to those unearthed at Gol Mod site. In addition, ornaments from China have floral decoration, and some also feature velvet scoters, lions and chimerical creatures known in Chinese mythology as “qilin”. This beast was described in detail almost two millennia after these noble Xiongnu tombs by a 19th century Mongolian historian V. Inzhannash in Chapter 56 of his “Khökh sudar”, completed in the 1870s. He narrates the legend of a sudden appearance of a beast with the body of an antelope and a single horn on the head before Chinggis Khaan’s troops as they stood at the very doorsteps of India. It demanded, in human voice, for the troops to report to the Great Khan and tell him to withdraw immediately. When Chinggis Khan inquired his minister Yelü Chucai on what the beast was and why it spoke the human language, the minister replied that it was the “jiaoduan” antelope that inhabits the farthest corner of the universe. Although this legend narrated in “Khökh sudar” has origins in a much later period, the physical appearance of the beast, described in great detail, matches the depictions on the silver ornaments, and the fact that it is narrated as speaking the human language, conveying divine will, and living in “the farthest corner of the universe” attracts much interest. Most likely, these attributes were inserted to the passage for the purpose of emphasizing the unearthly, divine origins of this creature. Still, the presence of several gender-specific terms for this animal in Mongolian language dictionaries (listed as “jiu duwan göröged”) proves of the existence of certain perceptions about it among the Mongols. According to the “Zhi yuan” dictionary judges of the ancient Mongols wore headgear with a single horn on top. Because these were
worn by guardians of the law, most likely the horn symbolized the manifestation of integrity and justice. Many legends about a beast with one horn exist in cultures of East Asia. Among the Tibetans it is called “hariharimo”, and the Vietnamese have a similar concept as well. In China, it is called a “qilin” (transliterated as “kirin” and “kilin” in Japanese and Korean), and appears as early as narratives of the famous scholar Confucius. On many occasions the Chinese qilin has a dragonshaped body, but a lion- or deer-shaped body is not uncommon. The qilin is considered to be an extremely tranquil creature and is believed to never attack with its horn. A single horn symbolizes a unitary beginning of authority, in the sense that a single ruler shall govern the universe. Moreover, a man who rides a qilin is believed to be a wise and immortal sage. Even the phrase “child of a qilin” is sometimes used for gifted children. The qilin does not kill other creatures, nor does it not pound the grass; it commutes in the air and consumes magic seeds – characteristics that in some cases coincide with what is written in “Khökh sudar”. Legends in the West also refer to creatures with one horn – licorn, unicornus, monoceros, Einhorn and the like. As early as the Hellenic period, a legend exists about a unicorn that befriends the goddess Artemis, and they are depicted together on many paintings of the later Medieval artists. In some Western encyclopedias, the unicorn is described as a beast with a deer body, a horse head, and a single crooked horn, and is believed to be captured and ridden only by a virgin. One can see that both the Oriental and Occidental cultures possessed legends of unicorn beasts with similar characteristics – such as wisdom, piety and justice – but the perceived physical appearances of this mythical creature differ from culture to culture. It is interesting that the “belget guroos” unicorn depicted on silver ornaments from tomb 20 at the Gol Mod Xiongnu necropolis differs sharply from the Chinese “qilin” unicorn, but is virtually identical to the medieval European depictions. Especially similar are the key elements of an artiodactyla torso, equine head, and long curved horn. If we assume that these decorative silver ornaments were only used by the members of Xiongnu aristocracy, then the chariots found in their tombs were likely put there to carry the souls of the deceased to heaven and perhaps the unicorn beasts were present to serve as guardians of that path to afterlife. (Ch.Ye.)



U
187
URSULA Brosseder. German archaeologist and Xiongnu scholar. Born in 1970. Assistant professor, Pre- and Early Historical Archaeology Institute of the University of Bonn, Germany. Her research theme is the Xiongnu, Eurasian HunSarmatian period nomadic societies, and European Iron Age (ceramics from the Hallstatt period). She has been working in Mongolian-French joint expedition at Gol Mod site in Khairkhan soum, Arkhangai aimag since 2005. She is not only a co-author of several books and reports on archaeological research at Gol Mod but also an author of over 10 articles of Xiongnu study that published in German, Mongolian, French and English languages. (Ch.Ye.)
Sketch of the Uvgunt tomb
UVGUNT tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. Located at the west slope of a ravine between the Uvgunt and Uudent rocky hills, south slope of the Ikh Dulaan mountain, in 27 km to the south-east of Buregkhangai soum of Bulgan aimag. In total 46 Xiongnu burials identified at this site and 12 square and circular enclosure kereksurs in the south-west side of Uudent Tolgoi hill. This site discovered by Bronze and Early Iron Age Study group of the Mongolian-Soviet Joint Expedition on History and Culture in 1983 by oral information of the local inhabitants, and then excavated 2 Xiongnu burials. Later, Mongolian-Belgian joint team excavated 6 burials here. (G.E.)



W
188



W
189



W
190



W
191
W
ANG MANG (45 BCE23 CE). A founder of a short lived Xin Dynasty (9-25 CE) during the time of the disintegration and re-unification of Han Dynasty. His family had a close relationship with the Imperial family of West Han Dynasty. In 8 BCE, Wang Mang was appointed to the Emperor Advisor on the case of working until the death of the Emperor. After the new Emperor died in 1 CE, he was re-appointed. Soon, Wang Mang gave his daughter to the next Emperor, Pin Di who died in 6 CE, as a wife. Then he killed around 50 successors and became the Emperor. In 9 CE, he raised himself to be an Emperor and declared the foundation of Xin or New Dynasty. Because the Huanghe River changed its river-bed, his State faced starvation, plague and social crisis. He forbade the farmers to gather in crowd but many revolts arose one after one. In 23 CE, the insurgents burned Chang’an city and reached the Palace and killed Wang Mang. Due to his aggressive policies to the nomads, the external situation of his State was getting complicated. Same time this was a positive situation for Xiongnu to be re-established and Wuhuan to fight against China for its independence, in contrary. (Ts.T.)
WANG ZHAOJUN (1st c. BCE). Chinese spouse of Huhanye Chanyu, and later Fujulei Chanyu. She is famed as one of the Four Beauties of ancient China and her story as a ‘political bride’ has handed down to generations. Was born to a prominent family of Baopin village, Zigui country (now in Hubei province) in the south of the Western Han dynasty. In 33 BC, Huhanye Chanyu visited Chang’an as part of the tributary system that existed between the Han and Xiongnu governments. He took the opportunity to request to become an imperial sonin-law. From the court ladies only Wang Zhaojun accepted to go Xiongnu land. Relations with the Xiongnu subsequently improved with this departure and Wang Zhaojun becomes much attached with her new fatherland. Wang Zhaojun became a favorite of
the Huhanye Chanyu, giving birth to two sons. Only one of them seems to have survived, Yituzhiyashi. They also had at least one daughter, Yun, who was created Princess Yimuo and who would later become a powerful figure in Xiongnu politics. When Huhanye died in 31 BC, Wang Zhaojun requested to return to China. Emperor Cheng, however, ordered that she follow Xiongnu levirate custom and become the wife of the next Chanyu, the oldest brother (or her stepson, born by her husband’s first wife) of her husband. In her new marriage she had two daughters. Her historical figure is highly politicized in China, and serving as a symbol of the integration of Han Chinese and ethnic minorities of China. There is a Memorial to Wang Zhaojun in Inner Mongolia. Although it is called a “Tomb”, she is not actually buried there. Her date of death and true grave is unknown. (Ch.Ye.)
WAR BEHIND THE GOBI. War taken between Xiongnu and Han on the territory of today’s Mongolia in the 1st half of 119 BCE. Izhixie Chanyu, Left Tuqi wang and general Zhao Xin led Xiongnu army. Wei Qing, Huo Qubing and Li Guang led Chinese army. Xiongnu military force consisted of more than 180.000 cavalrymen. In contrary to it, Chinese military force consisted of 100.000 cavalrymen and 200.000 infantrymen. In 119 BCE, the Emperor Wudi with his lots of spare horses of his cavalry led by two of his best generals Wei Qing and Huo Qubing set off to invasion against Xiongnu military forces by two routes. Wei Qing was ordered to cross over the Gobi, to make his way through the Xiongnu land, to encircle the Chanyu palace and destroy it. As soon as Chanyu got to know about it he had his queens and children move to the far north and began to wait for Wei Qing



W
192
in the north of Gobi by leading his soldiers himself. While Xiongnu and Chinese soldiers were fighting with each other there was a big storm during which Xiongnu people lost their advantages of fighting with archery over their rivals. At that night, Chinese soldiers encircled Xiongnu forces and involved they hand fight which was their advantages. Although Chanyu could break through the blockage with his elite soldiers, he incurred serious damages. Similarly, Wei Qing also incurred serious damages; it was impossible for him to continue his fight and returned back to his country. On the other front, Huo Qubing fought with the Left Tuqi wang of Xiongnu, encircled his soldiers and defeated them by killing 70.000 people and taking captive more than 80 nobles. Finally, he returned to his country with lots of trophy. After his war, the Xiongnu had to restore their strengths and gain power for 7 years. Thus, they could not go to war against China. Although the Han became most powerful military during a certain period of time it was impossible for them to attack the northern countries for several reasons. Namely, they lost more than 80% of their battle horses, thus, it became impossible for them to form cavalry. On the other hand, many years continuous war caused the increase in taxation and it resulted in hunger and peasant riots. In addition, they encountered challenges in Tibet in the west, Korea in the east, Yue people of Vietnamese origin in the south. (Ts.T.)
WAR OF 166 BCE. Xiongnu-Han war. Laoshang Chanyu of Xiongnu entered with his 140 thousands of horsemen into the Chinese border in 166 BCE, snaked up to 40 li (20 km) to capital city Chang’an, burnt summer palace of Han emperor and seized many inhabitants, cattle and goods. When Chinese army reached to call for fighting Xiongnu warriors were already moved away. The reason of war was undoubtedly to make pressure to re-establish heqin or Peace and Marriage treaty as is made during his predecessor Maodun Chanyu. (Ts.T.)
WAR OF 129-128 BCE. Xiongnu-Han war. In this year the Wudi emperor of Han issued an order to initiate the war to Xiongnu, and send his 4 prominent generals as Wei Qing with their elite warriors by 4 ways toward Xiongnu. General Wei Qing rode out of Shanggu as far as Longcheng. Killing or capturing 700 of the enemy. Gongsun He proceeded from Yunzhong, but took no captives. Gongsun Ao rode from Dai province, but was defeated by Xiongnu and lost over 7.000 of his men. Li Guang advanced from Yanmen, but also was defeated and captured, though he later managed to escape and return to Han. On their return, Gongsun He and Gongsun Ao were thrown into prison by the Han officials but were allowed to purchase a pardon for their offences and were reduced to the status of commoners. In the winter the Xiongnu several times crossed the Han border on plundering expeditions, hitting hardest at Yuyang to revenge Han invasion. Wudi dispatched general Han Anguo to garrison at Yuyang and protect it from the Xiongnu. In the autumn of following year Xiongnu invaded Han in three routes. 20.000 Xiongnu horsemen invaded Han border lands, murdered the Governor of Liaoxi, and carried off over 2.000 prisoners. They also invaded Yuyang, defeated the army of over 1.000 under the command of the Governor Han Anguo, and surrounded his camp. Relief forces arrived from Yan just in time, and the Xiongnu withdrew. Xiongnu invaded also Yanmen and killed of carried off over 1.000 persons. Han then dispatched general Wei Qing with a force of 30.000 cavalry to ride north from Yanmen, and Li Xi to ride out from Dai province to attack Xiongnu. They succeeded in killing and capturing several thousand Xiongnu. This war was one of the most hard and bloody phase of Xiongnu-Han wars. No fighting part take advantage of this phase of the war, but both lost important part of their troops and sensibly weakened. After the war Chanyu palace moved to the north of Gobi in present day Mongolia from the southern Gobi. Former Han general Zhao Xin was advised such a suggestion to Yizhixie Chanyu to prevent surprise attacks of enemy cavalry. (Ts.T.)
WAR OF 90 BCE. After significant victories over Han in 101 and 99 BCE, the Xiongnu began to make plunder attacks to Chinese border regions. As a reaction to these attacks in 90 BCE Han dynasty prepared to respond with large army massive attack. Han army entered the Xiongnu land in 3 routes. The east wing Han army in 30000 soldiers marched through lengthy way and finished their provisions and went back. In their way back they attacked by



W
193
Xiongnu warriors during 9 days, only after Han West army with 40 000 soldiers saved remained warriors from starvation. However Li Guangli general with his 70 000 soldiers main army were successful at the beginning, but when Li Guangli heard that his death sentence for his family’s` crime, and decided to keep family`s honor by his bravery and attacked to Xiongnu army in unprepared way. Near actual Tuul river Li Guangli moved all warriors and fought with 20 000 Xiongnu warriors and could force them to retreat. He continued his attacks and when reached to Yanjiang shan (Khangai Mountains) all his generals decided to catch him and convey to Emperial place. Li Guangli knew this trap and executed them directly, but no way remained to fight effectively further more for him. However he planned to fight towards to his land, but Hulugu Chanyu of Xiongnu digged a deep ditch cross to his way back and attacked with 50 000 warriors and Li Guangli surrendered. By this defeat, Han dynasty could not revived for a long time, and Xiongnu refreshed forces begin to play more and more important role in the region. (Ts.T.)
WAR OF KHAMI. Also named the battle of Yiwulu. It was a battle between North Xiongnu and Eastern Han, near Kumul and Khami, Xinjiang, in Febrary, 73 CE. The Xiongnu troops were commanded by Huyan wang whereas Han troops by Dou Gu and Gen Chong. The number of the Xiongnu soldiers is unclear but the Chinese source says they lost 10000 soldiers’ at the battle. Han soldiers were numbered by 12000 cavalry, consisted of Chinese soldiers as well as auxiliary forces from the South Xiongnu and Qian. As the threatening attack from the North Xiongnu got more frequent in the beginning of 70s CE, Han Dynasty called up a large army in four directions, commanded by Dou Gu and captured the Yiwulu region or the northeast of Xinjiang. Then they build numbers of garrisons for farmer-soldiers there and aimed to control the West and to make it be a base. After the reinforcement there, Dou Gu sent two officers Ban Chao and Guo Xun with a few soldiers to three city states-Shanshan, Yutian and Shule. They all became independent from Xiongnu and came to be controlled by China. And then the Eastern Han rebuilt the governor’s office of the Western Protectorate in 74 CE. The Xiongnu with its allies captured Jushi and Yutian again and killed the Chinese governor, Chen Mu. So the Eastern Han dismantled the governor’s office of the Western Protectorate in 75 CE. At that time, Bao Chao who left to protect Shule and Yutian, received the support of local people and lords, controlled some cities like Shicheng and launched to remove who joined the Xiongnu. In 80 CE, he added some city-states under
the control and could get the additional soldiers. Since an armed revolt arisen between the followers of the Xiongnu and of the Han in the West lasted tens of years, the Xiongnu was not able to call up the soldiers and lost its control caused by the domestic unrest. In 91 CE, the Eastern Han rebuilt the governor’s office of the West, appointed Ban Chao and chose Qiuqi or Kucha as permanent city. In 94 CE, Ban Chao dominated Yanqi and Yuli cities as the followers of the Xiongnu and did away the last base of the Xiongnu in the Western regions. At that time, the North Xiongnu collapsed itself. However, the governor’s office was dismantled again in 107 CE, because it lost its control due to the locals’ protest. (Ts.T.)
WEI QING (?-106 BCE). A military commander of Western Han. Since his sister became a concubine of Han Wu Di Emperor, he had a chance to enter the Palace. Moreover, he was paid an attention of Wu Di Emperor and opened the way to become a military commander. When Xiongnu mounted an attack on Shanggu district in 129 BCE, Wei Qing, Gongsun Ao, Gongsun He and Li Guang battled with 10000 cavalries in intercepting way. Only Wei Qing won the battle, killed more than 700 soldiers who guarded the Longcheng, Xiongnu Sacrificial Place, and captured the city. It was the first victory over Xiongnu. So Wei Qing was awarded a high rank and title. Next year, he killed over 1000 soldiers of Xiongnu and deserved a great merit. In 127 BCE, he defeated the princes of Loufan and Baiyan tribes, killed thousands people of Xiongnu and took over a million livestock animals. Consequently, Xiongnu lost Ordos and experienced a great loss. He erected a fortified wall-Shoufang in his domain and made it be a base to attack against Xiongnu. In 124 BCE, when West Tuqi wang of Xiongnu attacked the Shoufang, he was defeated by Wei Qing’s besiege. After the victory, Wei Qing became a general commander or generalissimos. In the following year, he invaded Xiongnu and killed or captured 20.000 soldiers, but his second commanders were defeated



W
194
by Izhixie Chanyu of Xiongnu and Zhao Xin gave in. At the battle mounted in 119 BCE, Wei Qing commanded the main force of the army, defeated Izhixie Chanyu and killed or captured thousands of soldiers. Nevertheless, n other famous Han commander, Li Guang committed suicide because he did not win the battle. The mass of people blamed Wei Qing for this suicide. So, since this battle in 119 BCE, Wei Qing never commanded the army. Then he worked as an advisor of the Emperor and died in 106 BCE. (Ts.T.)
WEN CHENG DI (440-465). Real name is Touba Jun and a successor of his grandfather Touba Tao. His father is Touba Huang and mother is a relative of Rouran Emperor. He was ascended the throne at the age of 12. He is recorded in history as he refused the strict policies and campaigns carried out by his grandfather to renew the laws and calm the people. But he continued the military and diplomatic strict policies to Rouran and High Carts-Oguz. His son, Touba Hung prince succeeded to implement his policies (Ts.T.).
WENGEDI (end of 4th c. CE). Emperor of Rouran. Also called as Ongudai. After the death of his father Desigun, the state was disintegrated. The Eastern part was ruled by Desigun’s eldest son Pihuoba, while the Western part ruled by the second son Wengedi. He managed to gain independence from Touba Wei in 376, and tried to capture the territory ruled by the previous Xiongnu together with Liu Weicheng of the South Xiongnu. In 386-395, the Tabgachi made an attack in Rouran with a big force and weakened their force. Rouran got more powerful in the period of Wengedi’s son Shelun. (Ts.T.)
WESTERN WEI (535-556). Firstly established as a result of the disintegration of Touba Wei. After Yuwen Tai general, killed Yuan Xiu, the last emperor of Touba Wei in 535, he let Yuan Baoju play an puppet emperor of the Western Wei. As the Western Wei had more powerful economy but less land and population
than the Eastern Wei, they could protect themselves well. Furthermore, they captured some places dominated by Liang State in the south. In 557, a son of Yuwen Tai demoted the previous emperor Yuwen Jue and founded a new state named Northern Zhou. (Ts.T.)
WHITE XIONGNU (420-552). In Greek Hephthalite, in Indian Huna. Тhere are always discussions on their origin, but some scholars believe they are related to Xiongnu. The core group of White Xiongnu was warrior nomads, but most of White Xiongnu were farmers. The earliest reference about them is from 125 CE, which says that they lived in the desert of Dzungaria. In 425 they conquered Sogdian Horasan. Since then Iran was recurrently attacked and taxed. In 503-513 they were pushed out of Iran and were defeated by Hosrou I in 557. White Xiongnu attacked India and conquered Gangha River valley, but in 528 they were thrown out. They had some influence on Indian society, shaking the cast system. It is believed that some White Xiongnu remained in India. (Ts.Т.)
WOOD PROCESSING ART. During the period of Uchjulu Chanyu (8 BCE- 13 CE) Han court asked to take small parcel of Xiongnu land where there are mountains and forest but Xiongnu refused to give it and explained that “we make ger and cart by wood, so we can not give this land”. Also there’s information that they used to make bow by wood from this mountain and Yingshan Mountain which is located to north side of this mountain. Even though there is not any other direct information about art of
Wooden coffin, Shombuuzyn belchir, Tomb 7



W
195
wood processing and carpentry in the source wooden parts of tomb, coffin and some wood works shows that carpentry was quiet well developed in Xiongnu period. It is clear that they used to use saw. Metal saw were found from tomb of Duulga Uul and tomb of Khudgyin Tolgoi tomb. Saw mark is observed clearly from wooden burials of Noyon Uul tomb. It shows that saw and carpenters plane are used very broadly. They used to twist the rope which is tied in bow fast for making fire so there’s adequate reason to consider that they had a drill. All wooden parts for building house was prepared and cut by axe in Ivolga settlement and metal axe and chisel which are considered as rare findings were found from this settlement. Pine was used broadly among the Xiongnu. Pine is not only used for making the big tombs of elite, it is also used for making tombs of common people. Saddle and dishes also were made of pine. Cart-wheel used to be made of elm which is more flexible tree and other parts of cart were used to be
made of birch tree. Small table made of birch was found from Noyon Uul tomb. (Ts.T.)
WOOL AND FELT PROCESSING. One of the main products of nomadic animal husbandry is the wool. Felt and other products made by wool are important to daily life of nomads. We can’t imagine nomads’ ger without felt. Russian scholar S.I.Rudenko wrote that Xiongnu used to breed 10 micron fine wool sheep as well as flock wool sheep according to findings of archaeological
Wooden lid with pattern, Tomb 28, Duulga uul site
Bull images on felt carpet, Noyon Uul, Tomb 20
Piece of embroidery with a hunting scene, Noyon Uul, Tomb 20



W
196
excavation. There’s assumption that they used to breed fine wool sheep (Sudan sheep) which does not exist now. Whorls found from Xiongnu tombs approves that they used spin and make woolen textile. Also according to Russian scholar A.B.Davydova they used to knit woolen textile by bone sticks which is found from the tomb. Felt carpets found from Noyon Uul tomb shows that Xiongnu’s felt that they used to use felt goods broadly. Also in then source it says that “Everybody including the chiefs used eat meat, dress by animal skin and wear felt garment”. (Ts.T.)
WOOLEN TEXTILE AND LEATHER PROCESSING ART. According to the historical narratives Xiongnu nobles eat meat, dress by leather and felt. In another source it was mentioned in advice of Zhonghang Yue to Laoshang Chanyu “if you go through the bush with silk clothes given as present from Han it will be ripped immediately so you will see that these kinds of clothes are not good as leather and felt clothes”. There’s information that traditional clothes of nomads were considered as best clothes in China and Wuling wang of Zhao kingdom of Warring States period, used exactly the same clothes as nomads.



W
197
It is clear that felt and leather goods are long lasting and useful which is appropriate for nomadic life so nomads used to process and used it for long time. It is very interesting that clothes, pants and shoes belongs to beginning of Iron Age period are still found from tomb of permafrost Pazyryk and these are similar to findings of tomb of Xiongnu in Noyon Uul. In Noyon Uul elite tombs the clothes were made of silk and other expensive materials and according to the herders the clothes are similar but it is easy to assume that herders used to make clothes from leather and felt.
It is interesting that A.B.Davydova considered one kind of tools which is found lot from Ivolga site and made from blade bone of small cattle as a tool for leather processing. The same types of several tools were found by excavation of Boroo site. G.Sukhbaatar mentioned that there’s no accurate evidence to consider Xiongnu’s knit wool themselves yet. Also he said that his thought was right that pattern, technique and paintings of fine wool textile were not made by Xiongnu and it may was made in Greece. But according to S.I.Rudenko craftsmen from west may used to live with Xiongnus and teach them method of woolen textile.



W
198
The red yarn of woolen textile found from Tomb No. 6 of Noyon Uul was painted by madder for paintings. According to the Mongolian botanists madder grows in Xiongnu’s land but madder for paintings doesn’t grow there. But A.A.Voskrenski who researched about painting technology mentioned that there may were craftsmen who are specialized on paintings. Xiongnu used to use sewing such as embroidering, stitching, hemming and whip stitching for clothes and embroidery. (Ts.T.)
WU DI (156-87 BCE). Chinese Emperor of Han dynasty. Orginal name is Liu Che. Was born in 156 BCE and passed away in March 29, 87 BCE. During his reign the influence of Daoism and authority of Han increased much in trans-border. He was attacked to Xiongnu by the stage by stage plan and the war continued for almost 40 years but could not reach achievement. He united Southern China, Northern and Central Vietnam and conquered Korean peninsula. During his reign, Han warriors were reached to Fergana (present Uzbekistan). Wu Di wars emptied out state fund and new policy carried out in order to increase the account and controlled the salt, iron and wine productions. However, during the war of Wu Di, Han dynasty state deteriorated much than Xiongnu and was made an agreement of peace. (Ts.T.)
WU EN (Unen-Yosutu). Chinese Archaeologist. Born in a Mongolian family of Khorchin Zuungar banner, Jirem aimag, Inner Mongolia in 1937. Graduated from the Department of History, the Beijing University with a major of archeology in 1962. He started his career from the Institute of Archeology, Academy of Social Sciences, in Beijing and was appointed as Vise Director in the same institute, as an Executive editor of the review “Kaogu” and as a director of Board for granting Archeological degrees. He is an honored professor of the Institute of Archeology of Germany. The main field of his research work is archeological research on the period ranged from the Bronze Age to Early Iron Age in the North part of China. The excavation and research on Xiongnu graves in Daodunzi supervised by him in 1985, hit the headlines at that time and were a great work to make a significant contribution to the Xiongnu studies. He implemented a China-America joint project entitled “Archeological research in the Henhe River basin” and “Excavation and research on the burial place in Shar Tal”. He is accounted
be a scholar who made a great contribution to the archeological research on Xiongnu and other ancient nomadic peoples. Unen-Yosutu wrote many works concerning with ancient nomadic nations. Of them, a book entitled “A comparative archeological research in the north steppefrom the Bronze Age to the Xiongnu period” (Beijing, 2007) finished to write before he died, is considered to be the most comprehensive work on this matter in Chinese. (B.A.)
WUHUAN. Ancient Mongolian speaking tribe. Inhabited in south-east of Inner Mongolia, Hebei, Liaoning, Shanxi provinces. Originated when Donghu confederation falled down in 209 BCE by Xiongnu attack. Included in Xiongnu state and later followed the Han in 1st century BCE. When defeating Eastern Han, “Wuhuan 3 generals” supported the Yuan Xiao noble and defeated by Cao Cao in 207. Since that time, many warriors of Wuhuan followed Cao Cao and renowned as “Legendary horsemen under the Heaven”. (Ts.T.)
WULING WANG OF ZHAO (reigned 325 BCE – 299 BCE). Wang or King of Zhao state during the Warring States Period of Chinese history. His reign was famous for one important event: the reforms consisting of “Wearing Barbarian Uniform and the Use of Cavalry in Battle”. Wuling wang ascended to the throne at 325 BCE, about halfway into the Warring States Period. His reign coincided with the appearance of several other notable figures in the Warring States. With his new army formed in Xiongnu style he had pushed northward the nomadic tribes from the Yinshan south slope and Ordos regions, where constructed lines of fortifies wall to mark his newly occupied territories. Which was the significant loss for Xiongnu and resulted the growing confrontation between Chinese and nomadic peoples. (Ch.Ye.)
WUSUN. Nomadic nation of Xiongnu period. Inhabited in present Gansu province, east of Xinjiang with Yuezhi tribe. Since 176 BCE, moved to the north of Xinjiang and east of Kazakhstan. Wusun rulers title was Kunmo. Defeated by Xiongnu and became vassal, but later allied to Han Wu Di Emperor against Xiongnu. Archaeological sites of Wusun remained in China, Kazakhstan and Kyrgyzistan. Wusun people presented anthropologically by their mixed of europoid and mongoloid types. Last time noted in Chinese source in 436 CE. Considered to be ancestors of Tele and their



W
199
descendants were major population of Turkic Empire. (Ts.T.)
WUWEI CHANYU (114-105 BCE). Grandson of Maodun Chanyu. His reign years occur during the early period of Han Wu Di Emperor aggressive invasions toward Xiongnu. Same time in the western frontier Wusun people becoming hostile by the abetment of Han. Wuwei Chanyu tried to resolve the conflict the peaceful meanings different than Wu Di emperor, who is implementing military forces for it. Shortly before his succession, the Chinese sent Yan Chang as an envoy with an inappropriate proposal that demanded the Xiongnu Chanyu submit to the Han Empire. The aggravated Izhixie Chanyu detained him. Because the Han empire had already detained a number of the Xiongnu emissaries, and Xiongnu detained an equal number of the Chinese envoys, the mutual relations between the two were strained. The Chinese Court started again assembling an army and horses, but the commander Huo Qubing died, and for a long time the Chinese Court could not mount a northern campaign against the Xiongnu. The Xiongnu also stopped attacking the borders of China after suffering devastating war losses of 300,000 military and civilian people between 124 and 119 BCE. Two years into Wuwei Chanyu’s reign, the Chinese resumed their harassment. In 112 BCE the Chinese sent He Gun-sun with 15,000 cavalry and Zhao Ponu with 10,000 cavalry, but both campaigns ended without any battles. Since that time Xiongnu were attacked to Han continuously and Han was prepared for big decisive fighting for 2 years. In 110 BCE, Wu Di collected 180.000 cavalries from Shoufang and sent a messenger Go Gi to Chanyu. Go Gi reached in Chanyu palace and passed a haughtiness message to Chanyu that “If you are capability to fight, Son of Heaven waiting you on the border, if unattainable follow the Han”. Wuwei Chanyu becoming furious and sent him to Baikal Lake to sheepherd. Wuwei Chanyu sent envoys to the Chinese Court a few times with polite offers of peace and kinship. The Chinese Court sent Wan Wu to spy on the condition of the Huns. The Huns had a custom that allowed a Chinese envoy into the Chanyu yurt only if the envoy would leave his bunchuk (his staff) in front of the door and paint his face with black ink. Wu, a native of the Bei-di province, knew well the customs of the
Huns. He left the bunchuk, painted his face, and was received in the yurt. The Chanyu had grown fond of him. In 110 BCE Wu diplomatically suggested to the Chanyu to send his successor to the Chinese Court as a hostage, and by that to restore the heqin peace and kinship treaty. But at the same time, behind the backs of the Xiongnu, the Chinese Court opened connections with the Yuezhi who were located at that time in present Uzbekistan and Bactria, occupied Zu-cuan to block the Xiongnu from Qiang, and married a Chinese Princess to the Wusun ruler to separate the allied states in the west from the Xiongnu. The Chinese Court then sent Yan-sin to the Xiongnu. The Chinese state officials believed that the Xiongnu, at their present weakness, could be swayed to submit to China. Yan Xin was a firm, direct person; and because he had a middle rank, Chanyu did not treat him nicely. Chanyu offered to receive him in the yurt, but Yan Xin did not agree to leave his bunchuk at the door, so Chanyu received him sitting outside of the yurt. Sin, appearing before the Chanyu, told him: “Chanyu, if you wish for peace and kinship, send the successor to the Chinese Court as a hostage”. Chanyu answered “That is contrary to the former (heqin) treaty. Under that treaty the Chinese Court, as a token of their peace and kinship, normally sent a Princess with silk fabrics, cottons and different provisions, and the Xiongnu on their part agreed to not disturb the borders of China; and now you wish that I, contrary to the former treaty, send a successor as a hostage”? The Xiongnu had a custom that if a Chinese envoy had no high rank, and was a scholar, they would interrupt his eloquence; and if he is young, they would egg his quick temper with caustic remarks. Each embassy sent by the Chinese Court to the Xiongnu, the Xiongnu would reciprocate with an embassy of their own to the Chinese. If the Chinese Court detained the Xiongnu envoys, the Xiongnu also detained an equal number of Chinese envoys. As soon as Sin returned from the Xiongnu, the Chinese Court sent Wan Wu again. Chanyu even expressed a desire to go to Chinese Court to be introduced to the Son of Sky and personally agree to be brothers. After his return, Wu notified the Court, and the Court ordered to build in Chang’an an estate for the Chanyu. Wuwei Chanyu passed away in 105 BCE. His son Ushylu, who was still a child, was proclaimed a Chanyu according to their custom of agnatic primogeniture succession, and was named Err Chanyu. (Ts.T.)



X
200



X
201
X
IA Dynasty (407-432). State of Tefu tribe of Xiongnu, which was centered in Ordos. Capitals were Tongwangchen (418-427), Shanggui (427428) and Pingliang (428-430). Founder Lui (Helian) Bobo. His father was an officer of Later Qin dynasty and chief of Xiongnu in Ordos. His son Lui Bobo awarded himself as Heavenly wang, after defeated Tufa and Xianbei of Southern Liang in 407, and declared his state. Lui Bobo changed his Lui surname, get Helian or Heavenly clan name. The major population of Xia dynasty consisted by southern farmers of Han nationality, but the authority was handled by the northern nomads. Also Helian Bobo established the capital in Tongwang Cheng city, in the south of present Ordos. In that time the main competitor was the Later Qin dynasty and attacked endlessly, but Wei dynasty of Touba was the mortal competitor for him. In 416, when later Qin falling down, western territories of the dynasty controlled by Xia dynasty. Consequently, Xia dynasty territory extended by Ordos, Northern Shanxi and East side of Gansu and northern Shaanxi. In 417 he conquered Chanan region, the former capital area of Later Qin dynasty in present day South Shaanxi. After capturing this ancient and prestigious capital, Helian Bobo had the right to throne him as Emperor. In 424 his sons competed each other for the reigning and middle son Helian Chang succeeded the throne. He conducted a policy to extend his land to the south, but pushed by Touba Wei and in 427 lost his capital Tongwangcheng seized. His brother Helian Ding succeeded him and struggled to Tabgach continuously but defeated in 431. (Ts.T.)
XIAN. Chinese word used as a title in Xiongnu. Means: 1. Wise, 2. Goodness man, 3. Glorious. Similar with Tuqi wang rank of Xiongnu. See Tuqi wang. (No.B.)
XIANBEI. Ancient Mongolian speaking tribes confederation in the present day Manchuria and Inner Mongolian territory. Xianbei tribes reclaimed whole Northern China, Manchuria and Mongolian territory and established many states during 3-5th centuries CE. Xianbei are originated from Donghu tribal confederation, which defeated by Xiongnu in 209 BCE and divided into Liaoxi Xianbei and
Liaodong Xianbei according to some sources. Since middle of 1st century CE, Xianbei get out Xiongnus` control and started to attack Xiongnu within Han dynasty. When Xiongnu dynasty falling down, about a hundred thousand herdsmen of Xiongnu followed the Xianbei union. Since middle of 2nd century CE, Xianbei dynasty established under the rule of Tanshihuai (141-181 CE), but dispersed into many small kingdoms and tribes in 255 CE. The bigger tribes of Xianbei are: Tabgach-Touba, Yuwen, Duan, Rouran, Kumosi, Kidan and Shiwei. Some of these tribes assimilated into China during Tang dynasty and lost their language and culture. Founder of Sui dynasty Yang Gang and establisher of Tang dynasty Li Yuans` mothers were Xianbei women, thus they were half Xianbei. Social structure of Xianbei was at the level of clan organization and basic economy was nomadic cattle-breeding. Mongolian historian G.Sukhbaatar wrote a fundamental work on Xianbei ethnogenesis, traditional custom and social structure. (Ts.T.)
XIANYUN tribe. During 9th century BCE, when Guifan name disappeared from historical sources Xianyun name started noted down in sources. They were constant competitor of Zhou dynasty until 5th century BCE. By major scholars` view the Xianyun is probability the ancestors of Xiongnu. Russian scholar A.A.Shtukin named them as “Gunnu” in his translation of Shijin. After K.V.Vasilev`s opinion, Xianyun is one of the ethno-political unit, which included in Taiyuan Rong. According to chapter 101 of Shizhi, Xianyun, and Xunyu tribes included in the ancestors of Xiongnu. Since Ying dynasty they called as Xiongnu. During the Western Zhou dynasty, Zhou people and their opponent tribes as Guifan, and Xianyun were used the cart, and by most of conception Xianyun was the scythic type of nomads. (S.U.)
XIAO WEN DI (467-499). Own name was Touba Hong. Succeeded the throne when his was 4 years old. He considered that carried out a destructive policy to sinicize the Tabgach tribe. The aim was to centralize state authority and make easy to rule many nations of Wei dynasty. He prohibited to wear national clothes, to speak native language and forced to marry with Chinese and get Chinese clan names. He changed his Touba and Tabgach clan by Yuan clan and moved the capital to the Luoyan in 494. Army officers remained in the old capital Pingchen and kept traditional customs which intensified the differentiation between sinicized Luoyan and traditional Xianbei Pingchen. His policy weakened Xianbei position in the Empire and during his



X
202
grandsons` reign period Wei dynasty divided into two in 534. His son Touba Ke succeeded him. (Ts.T.)
XIGOUPAN tombs. Xiongnu period graves in China. Located in Ordos region, Zuungar banner of Ikh Zuu aimag, Inner Mongolia. All 12 tombs are excavated during 1979-1980. Tombs Nos. 1, 2, 3 are related to the end of Warring States, and tombs Nos. 4-8 and 10-12 are related to Xiongnu period. These tombs have rectangular pits and contained humn skeletons in his spins without the coffins. Discovered many kinds of earthenwares, gold plate belt bronze adornment with sheep figure, bronze horse, bronze arrowhead, bronze ring, besides sheep bones and dog skull. Also golden deer, tiger and golden mythic animal figured objects were also found from tomb No. 2, gold, silver and jade adornments, necklace and belt adornments were found from tomb No4 and it considered as a tomb of Xiongnu aristocratic woman. The earthenware, which found from the tomb No. 5 and 12, has a small hole on the bottom and wave ornament in the shoulder are similar to Xiongnu large pots. (Ch.S.)
“XIONGNU”. The monograph written by L.N.
Gumilev. “Хунну. Центральная Азия в древности”
was published in Moscow, in 1960. It contains an introduction, 15 chapters and 91 parts in 292 pages. The author touched all important aspects of Xiongnu study: the origin, history of its establishment and political, economical, social and military activities as well as focusing his own view on the problematic questions. Chapter 1- “In the Fog of the Years” with five parts: In Ancient China; The foundation of Xiongnu; The situation in Eastern steppe; Rong and Xiongnu; The victory of Zhou and its result. Chapter 2- “The Despise in Steppe” had the following parts: Before Xiongnu; Powerful Xiongnu; Discovery of Siberia; The Xiongnu in the north; Neighbors of ancient Xiongnu. The next chapter with a name “On the Beach of Sand Ocean” has four parts: The first attack of Xiongnu on China; The fight between Rong and China; The culture of squire grave; About Xiongnu language. Next chapters The Great Wall; The Whistling Arrow; Dominating the People; A dragon, taken off; The Horses of the Heaven; A Mortal Combat; The Crisis of Xiongnu; The Battle of Brothers; The Freedom, gained again; The Disintegration; The Chipped Ring and The Last strike. All the names of the chapters show all the historical events and each chapter has some parts to prove them. (Ch.A.)
“XIONGNU (archaeology, ethnography, history)”. Author Z. Batsaikhan. Published
in Ulaanbaatar, 2002, with 291 pages in total consisting of introduction, 2 chapters, 4 articles, and appendices. A monograph in which deductions, and conclusions were made by analyzing on the issues of the Xiongnu’s territory, chronology, establishment, society, and culture based on history, archaeology, and ethnography materials. According to the author, it is significant to determine density and sparseness of nomad’s grave and monuments with regional divisions for ascertaining the nomads’ way of life, orientation, and territory and to clarify livestock’s structure based on comprehensive researches. Furthermore, as he states, the fact that advanced items of nomadic culture such as ammunition, adornments, animal arts, musical instruments, livestock brands, and tools were distributed not only among neighboring countries but also across the east Europe, should be concerned as big contribution from the nomads to the world’s civilization, and its development. In addition, it came to a conclusion in which the similarity between the Xiongnu, the Wuhuan-Xianbei and later Mongols’ funeral practices shows ancestry bond relations and the Mongol’s ethnogenesis process commenced from the Xiongnu period. (Na.B.)
“XIONGNU ARTIFACTS FROM AR TSAIDAM SITE OF INNER MONGOLIA”. Brief report. Kaogu, 1980, No4. Authors Tian Guangjing, Guo Suxin. In this article published the result of the excavation at Tokhoin Balar and Ar Tsaidam sites of Ikh Zuu aimag of Inner Mongolia in 1972, and about the artifacts from that sites as golden crown and golden adornments for belt of Xiongnu. (Ch.S.)
“XIONGNU BURIAL OF XIGOUPAN”. Brief report. Published in Wenwu, 1980, Vol. 7. Authors Gou Suxin, Tian Guangjing. The burial is located on east cost of Hustain river, 5 km to north west of Shi Gou Pan village, Zuungar banner, Ikh Zuu aimag (present day Ordos city) of Inner Mongolia. Following information from local herder, in May 1979 archeologists Gou Suxin and others took the measurements and excavated 3 burials in this burial site. In the report, there is documentation on the geographical location, burial structure, and arrangement, artifacts discovered during excavation and dated to Xiongnu period. (Ch.S.)
“XIONGNU COMMON PEOPLE GRAVES”.
Author Ts.Turbat. “Хүннүгийн жирийн иргэдийн
булш”. A monograph of 4 chapters, 17 articles, attached drawings and paintings with 143 pages in total, published in Ulaanbaatar in 2004. It is a monograph created by specific research on practices



X
203
of the Xiongnu circular graves based on the materials of 174 graves excavated in the territory of Mongolia and 400 graves of Transbaikalia. As the author stated, when the Xiongnu people obtained a fixed understanding about afterlife, they had a holy, offered place belonging to particular group where they buried the dead ones so that the Xiongnu people were had unique practices for 400 years of existence. He recognized that the Xiongnu cemeteries were divided into different groups with their chronology, society, and origination. The reason the Xiongnu people had accurate fixed rules and regulations on practices, due to a fact that during 4-3rd c. BCE square grave culture of Bronze and Iron Age nomad’s archaeological cultures were integrated and combined. He came to a conclusion that the Xiongnu ethno-genesis was researched and analyzed in terms of practices, the Xiongnu has lot in common with Mongols in most characteristics such as ceremony for grave selection, grave construction, sacrificing rituals, lying positions of dead people, and orientation and the Xiongnu and Mongols were direct ancestors. (Na.B.)
XIONGNU EMPIRE (209 BCE-93 CE). The first Empire established by the Central Asian nomads. The Empire existed for the longest among the established empires of nomads. Nomadic empire state structure and organization were derived from the Xiongnu period and it developed inheritance in spite of chronological change for two thousand years. Although the Xiongnu was consisted of multinations, the core bond is in connection with actual Mongols.
Historical Sources. There are two key types of resources for the Xiongnu history and culture research; archaeological and written resources. The archaeological resource is made up of commoners graves and elite tombs, multifunctional city and settlement, rock art sites, and production sites, such as pottery workshops. Also there are a number of chance findings. Written sources, for instance in Chinese and in Latin languages also plays major role in the Xiongnu study. Especially, Shiji of Sima Qian or, Han Shu of Ban Gu, and Hou Han Shu, Jin Shu of Fan Xianling are fundamental works to study the Xiongnu Empire and the south Xiongnu, while, historical books such as Priscus, Jordanes, Ammianus Marcellinus written in Latin are essential for European Hun study. Territory. During Maodun and his son Laoshang Chanyu’s reign the Xiongnu territory reached to Korean peninsula in the east, Tarvagatai mountains in the west, Baikal Lake in the north, and the Great Wall in the south. Urban civilized cities
and countries which used to be located in present Xinjiang’s location were dependent on the Xiongnu that imposed tributes from them. Part of the Silk Road was under control during most of the Xiongnu history. The core or center of the military and politics was in present Mongolian territory, and in the center and the west of Inner Mongolia. Chanyu’s palace once was on the mountain Yingshan in Inner Mongolia, and then on the east bank of Khangai mountain range. In late 2nd century BCE, the center of the Xiongnu politics was moved to the land between Khangai and Khentii. Ethnogenesis. During the 4th century BCE, by the combination of southern nomads from Ordos and present day Mongolian native inhabitants presented by slab grave population was originated the Xiongnu. They had anthropological characteristics with dominated Mongoloid look. The Xiongnu origination and ancestry drew attention of tens of researchers, and has been researched for more than 200 years. In Eastern and Western Xiongnu scholarship, the Xiongnu is thought regarding with Turkic people. As to see from the latest archaeological, paleoanthropological and paleogenetic studies and researches, the Xiongnu is proved as the ancestor of Mongols. Political history. Late 3rd century BCE, the fact that whole China was integrated under the rule of Qin Dynasty the first time of its history was a heavy challenge for neighboring northern nomadic people. In 215-214 BCE, Men Tian, prominent Qin general, with a large army of 300.000 soldiers force put the Xiongnu out of the south eastern Ordos and Yingshan Mountain area, which created an motive for the Xiongnu and all the nomads to unite their force. In 209 BCE, under the control of a young king, Maodun Chanyu, the Xiongnu Empire was established and the first empire state of nomads was created in short time. During the period of Maodun, his son, Laoshang, and grandson, Guncheng, the Xiongnu was strengthened the most to the peak, and became of the world’s most influential empires by uniting not only nomads but Tarim basin oasis nations, Siberian and Manchurian hunters, seminomads of Gansu and Qinghai lake and in total of 26 nations or states. At this time, the Xiongnu relatively developed peaceful relations with neighboring Han dynasty in compliance with the “Peace and Marriage treaty” and imposed annual tribute from them. During late half of the 2nd century BCE to fifteen years of the 1st century, the Xiongnu successfully overcame the heaviest 40 years of war initiated by a warlike emperor of Han state, Wu Di and protected its independence and sovereignity. In the 1st century BCE, due to internal and external factors, an



X
204
insurrection take part in Xiongnu state and in same time 5 Chanyus contended for throne. At this time some of the Xiongnu fought for the independence under the control of Zhizhi Chanyu, while some accompanied Huhanye Chanyu and accepted to be vassal nation of Han dynasty and joined them. In 9-25 CE, the Xiongnu re-declared their independence and became as powerful as the period of Maodun, when China taken under Wang Mang reign, who took over the state power illegally. During the middle of the 1st century CE, it was divided into the north and the south subdued by China due to the chaos within the Xiongnu. In 93 CE, the Northern Xiongnu was invaded by united military force of Han dynasty, the south Xiongnu, and Xianbei. Economy. The fact that the Xiongnu predominantly practiced animal husbandry, less crop farming, and was developing handycraft, was approved by a research conducted in various types of settlements of Mongolia and Buryatia. According the researchers, the Xiongnu animal husbandry was considered as classical nomadic animal husbandry. It established specific residences desperately with the aim of developing crop farming and handicraft. Some of the Xiongnu settlements such as Ivolga, Dureny, and Boroo had lot of similiraties in structure and organization. Hunting and fishing were also significant parts for the Xiongnu economic system as to see from archaeological materials. In addition, foreign trade and tributes were one of the important factors for the Xiongnu economy. The Xiongnu handicraft, iron, bronze, bone making art and woolen material processing were highly developed.
Social structure. The Xiongnu firstly created triplicate structure of military-administrative organization and decimal military unit system. The structure of military-administrative organization for dividing the state into 3 parts or wings was inherited until Middle Age Mongols. Also the decimal military unit system was common among nomads. The Xiongnu society used to emphasize tribes, and their clan relationships but had a society with complicated hierarchal system. The ruler of the Xiongnu, Chanyu’s royal relationship, elite tribes with relationships in law are the core of the Xiongnu State, and the tribes with similar clan relationships, and language are the main people of the Xiongnu. Many various nomads who once were leading or voluntarily joined with the Xiongnu used to substitute the Xiongnu. Art and culture. The Xiongnu art was based on the art of animal style, key form of art in steppe of that time. The carpet or felt rug found from Noyon Uul should be considered as classical fine arts example of the Xiongnu. A folktale that a long
song was created in the period of the Xiongnu shows relationship between the Xiongnu and Mongols. Xiongnu jews harp unearthed from Morin Tolgoi tomb and fife noted on historical sources were some examples of Xiongnu musical culture. Foreign relations. The two key countries of the Xiongnu foreign relations were Han China and the Western region city states. As Han put pressure on free trade, some times only market for crop and handicraft product markets were the western boundary city states. The Xiongnu and Han fought for these markets for ages. The Xiongnu played main role in opening the Silk Road. The fact that Chinese, Iranian and Greece products and items are often found shows the Xiongnu foreign trade with remote countries.
Archaeological sites. Most of the archaeological sites of the Xiongnu are within the territory of recent Mongolia. As there are over 100 cemeteries of the Xiongnu in Mongolia within 5000 graves have been registered, and approximately 500 out of them were excavated and researched. Among them, elite graves and tombs were revealed and found in 7 sites such as Noyon uul, Gol Mod, Duurlig nars, Gol Mod-2, Bor Bulag, Belhiin Am, and Takhiltyn Khotgor as well as 20 remains of settlements such as Boroo, Bayanbulag and Tereljyn Durvuljin. There are over 20 remains of cemeteries and settlements in Inner Mongolia and Ningxia and Shanxi provinces of PRC. Also there are 20 and more sites discovered in Russia as well. (Ts.T.)
“XIONGNU GRAVE IN SHANGSUNJIAZHAI, DATONG, QINGHAI”. Report on the archaeological excavation of a Xiongnu grave in China. An archeological expedition of Office for Cultural Heritage of Qinghai province. 1979. No4. This brief scientific report tells about the excavation on a Xiongnu grave discovered near Shangsunjiazhai, Datong xian, Qinghai province in 1977. The site is located in distance of 17 km in the north of the village and the tomb related to Xiongnu culture is located in the south part of the cemetery with 127 graves. It was identified in the surface by earthen mound in circular shape, and the height is 6.2 m and diameter is 23 m. The earthen mound was mixed with clay and hardened and strengthened by several layers up to 10-21 cm. Under the earthen mound a brick chamber was found. Many valuable finding, including several ceramic pots, bronze mirror and coins and jade objects. The most interesting object unearthed from this tomb is a seal of a Xiongnu nobleman subjugated to Han Dynasty. Although this seal with Chinese inscription was granted to a Xiongnu nobleman, researchers believe that the



X
205
tomb could belong to South Xiongnu later period culture influenced by Han. (Ch.S.)
“XIONGNU GRAVE OF HULUSUTAI”. A brief report. Published in Wenwu, 1980, Vol. 7. The author is Ta La and Liang Zhengming. There is a Xiongnu grave on the east bank of the Hulusutai River middle flow, in 15 km in the southeast of Khangain Gobi commune (at that time), Middle banner of Urad, Bayannuur aimag, Inner Mongolia. In May, 1979, Tala, a researcher of the expedition of Inner Mongolian Institute of Archaeology and Cultural heritage explored and excavated there. They researched three graves and wrote a report on the structure of the Xiongnu graves as well as all the findings and their dates. (Ch.S.)
“XIONGNU GRAVE TYPES AND CHRONOLOGY”. An article published in the Nei Menggu wenwu yu kaogu, 1982, Vol. 2. Author Tian Guangjing. An attempt was made to classify the graves of the Xiongnu found in northern China, Mongolia, and Transbaikalia and to clarify the chronology. The grave of the Xiongnu was divided into 4 different chronologies; Spring-Autumn period, Warring States period, Western Han and the Eastern Han period. Classified graves with their shape, and organization as grave without coffin, grave with wooden coffin, wooden coffin, fenced grave, and bricked tomb grave. (Ch.S.)
XIONGNU HAIR AND BRAIDS. A few resources on the Xiongnu hair and clothing were recorded; however, those were just mentioned disorderly over records. From the historical record of the 2 Chinese generals who were arrested by some Xiongnu people and adopted the Xiongnu customs, the Xiongnu clothing and hair were absolutely different from
what Chinese had. As to see from historical fact, it was common for men to tress their hair but the way they put on them was different. Such hair styles were dependent on local environment, climate, and nature of traditional
economy. Particularly, nomads who usually herd livestock with riding horses in continental weather wore their hair braid with the aim of comfort. According to Mongolian historian G.Sukhbaatar states on the Xiongnu hairstyle, “Qian Hanshu” writing’s information “Xiongnu men tressed their hair as the hummer” and interpreted it as “the Xiongnu wore their hair in braid as tightly as possible”, which shows the Xiongnu men had braids. It reveals the different hairstyles of the Chinese and nomads. Historically the Chinese had strong fixed tradition to cut their hair in which they kept their hair by piling on the head and wore pointed hat or cap suitable with the hairstyle. However, the nomads wore their hair in a way that is different from the Chinese in which they hung their hair by wearing it in many plaits. The Chinese, named their traditional hairstyle as “biang-fa” whereas the nomads tradition to tress into two separate trait called as “pei-fa” which was offencive for them as northern barbarian habit. A Chinese historian, Liu Baonang, explained “When young boys or girls become adults, they pile their hair up on the head whereas hu’s do not follow the custom but hanging their hair down by wearing in plait. Therefore, “the term, “pei-fa” refers to hair hung
d
t
d
d
A d
Core of the braid, Noyon Uul, Tomb 6
Human braid in silk sheath, Noyon Uul, Tomb 6
Hairpins, Shombuuzyn belchir



X
206
down in plaits”. According to Mongolian scientist G.Sukhbaatar, tightly tied up hair refer to a braid hung down along one’s body, which will be the explanation of the above information about hair and braid. An evidence for this is that lots of braids were found in excavated elite tombs of the Xiongnu in Noyon Uul and even some of them were put in sheath. These braids were researched and analyzed in comparison with Khalkhas, Buriats, Koreans, Finno-Ugrians, and Slav people’s hair in terms of hair thickness, color, and style. The result of the research shows that as for the hair thickness Korean people’s hair was the thickest which was similar to Buriats’, Khalkha’s, Altainian’s and Noyon Uul, whereas Finno-Ugrians and Slavic hair was the thinnest. As for the hair structure and coloration, Khalkha’s hair was similar to Noyon Uul. An interesting finding to show the Xiongnu hairstyle is belt decoration belonging to the Xiongnu period which was brought to Mongolian National Museum in 2001, found in Khairkhandulaan soum, Uvurkhangai province. On face of the belt, a picture of two men with long garment sitting together with a box among them and some horsemen were illustrated. Horsemen wore garments with narrow sleeve, straight upper flap and long flap and suspended knife and bow case from their belts. They wore hat with wide back and without visor. Horses raised up on the hind legs are with cheek piece, circle buckle and leather bridle. The croupiers shown clearly. It is interesting that the man’s, who sit on the left edge, braids illustrated behind his ears. (U.E.)
XIONGNU HAN DYNASTY. Called as Later Han or Northern Han. State of the Southern Xiongnu. Founded by Liu Yuan in 304 CE. By the end of the 3rd century almost half million Southern Xiongnu people lived and nomadized in actual Ordos and Shanxi tribe. They were divided into 5 tribes and ruled separately. Rulers apparently changed into Chinese style but majority were still living by their traditional way of life. There were strong tendency to resist the Chinese dignitaries burden. Liu Yuan was famous by his Chinese education, military and letter skills, power and faith. In 304 he declared himself as king of the Han state and raised 50000 solders. In 309 the Xiongnu army won in major battles and conquered the former Eastern Han capital Louyang city. In 311 and 316 conquered Louyang and Chang’an cities and captured the emperor of Jin state. In 329 general Shi Le of the Jiehu tribe extinguished the Xiongnu Han dynasty. (Ts.T.)
“XIONGNU HERITAGE”. UB., 1999, Author D.Navaan. “Хүннүгийн өв соёл”. A booklet of 61 pages. It is on 2 big grave materials excavated
in the Xiongnu elite tombs located in the valley of Takhilt, Mankhan soum, Khovd province under the supervision of senior archaeologist, D.Navaan. He made excavation in accoradance with his own methodology. A funeral wooden construction with 12×16m stone cover of the grave 1 excavated in 1988 was found. The grave was looted early and placed objects were taken away. The position of such findings were not mentioned; tomb, coffin organization, and size, however, the following findings were recorded as found; a carriage hub, bronze cover of parasol, bronze platter, bronze cauldron, iron circle spike, metal bit, metal sword, knife, and two potteries. According to D.Navaan, the graves which were located in the south-east could belong to the most ancient time in terms of chronology by observing the funeral grave size, and orientation of Takhilt valley. In 1990, he excavated the grave 2 considered the most ancient of the grave in Takhilt valley with the above mentioned methodology. He started the excavation from the embankment size, and made iron fenced and wooden block to prevent the stone falling down in the depth of 5 m. A wooden chamber was found in depth of 11m excavation. Wooden chamber, coffin and human bones were not touched. He named it as “Princess grave” after considering the tomb structure, and placed objects which are referred to an aristocratic Xiongnu woman. The following artifacts and findings were found from the grave; wooden hub of a chariot, wooden cover of parasol, bronze pot with spout, bronze handle of coffin, golden earrings, metal bit and cheek piece, bone object with hole. The book also briefly introduced the results of the excavation of graves of the 13th c. made by Mongol-Soviet Joint Expedition of History and Culture in Bulgan, Khalzan sites, of Darvi soum, Khovd aimag in 1984. (Ch.Ye.)
“XIONGNU HISTORICAL CHRONOLOGY”. Author Lin Gang. Beijing, 1982. In this manual the author made chronological listing of all significant events of Xiongnu history from Warring States period (475-221 BCE) to North and South Dynasties period (420-589 CE) using different kind of historical sources, such as, state histories, personal diaries, notes, poems. The book has 3 chapters and all parts equipped with full citations and also accompanied with the Xiongnu Chanyu inheritance list. It mentioned about the Xiongnu and its related nations, Chinese policy against the Xiongnu and other historical events and wars between the Xiongnu and China. (Ch.S.)



X
207
“XIONGNU HISTORICAL SOURCES COMPILATION”. In 2 volumes. Author Lin Gang. Beijing, 1988. It was compiled as a handbook for the Xiongnu researchers, and readers by integrating all the information about the Xiongnu cited from ancient Chinese state history, and all other kind of written sources. The book is consisted of 2 chapters. Information regarding with the Xiongnu was included in the 1st chapter, whereas the 2nd chapter covered over the Xiongnu originated nations. It is significant that all the source information was cited in old Chinese characters. (Ch.S.)
“XIONGNU HISTORY”. Author, Lin Gang. Huhhot, 2007. This large work dealing about the first empire of in the history of the Central Asian nomads, covering Xiongnu politics, culture, customs, origination, social life, and relationships with other nations, especially with Han dynasty. The book was published in 2 volumes: “The Xiongnu history” (Hohhot, 1977), and “The general history of the Xiongnu” (Hohhot, 1986). At the end of the book attached some useful appendices, such as list of chanyus, and other aristocrats inheritance, historical maps of nomads during Chinese Warring State (475-221 BCE), Qin (221-206 BCE), Han (202 BCE-220 CE), Three kingdom period (220-280 CE), and some information and facts on archaeological sites such as settlements and grave of the Xiongnu in the territory of Mongolia. (Ch.S.)
“XIONGNU HISTORY AND CULTURE: The History of Great Xiongnu Empire founded by under patronage of Sky”. A monograph on Xiongnu history and Mongolian ancient history research. Author Ya.Ganbaatar. This work was published in Ulaanbaatar in 2008. It comprises of Foreward, Introduction, 7 chapters, 55 subchapters, and conclusion. In total, it consists of 363 pages. The first chapter was named “Historical studies on Nomadic Culture and Civilization”. He wrote elaborately about geographical understandings of Xiongnu studies, summary of archaeological studies, main sources of chronicle writings, Xiongnu studies in Russia, China and Mongolia. The second chapter begins with subchapter about origins and spreading of ancient nomadic tribes lived on the territory of Mongolia and its neighboring areas in the XII-VII century BCE under the name “Ancient Xiongnu”. Subchapters, of the above mentioned chapter include information about the proper name of Xiongnu. The third chapter is named “The Great Empire of Xiongnu”. In this chapter, ranging from the rise of Xiongnu to its collapse, all periods of Xiongnu
people who fled to Europe have been classified into subchapters. The fourth chapter is entitled “Xiongnu Culture and Society”. Five nomadic cultures which includes Karasuk, Pazyryk, Ordos, Yinshan and Noyon Uul cultures have been briefly described in this chapter. Moreover, religion, language, literacy and writting, society, politics, economic structure and organization, agriculture and handcrafts issues of the nomads have been covered in this chapter. The author named the last chapter as “Let study more intensively the Ancient Nomadic Culture and Civilization”. He looked for traces of nomadic culture and civilization in ancient poetry. In his chapter he claims that the history of humanities is the history of movement or nomadism. At the end of his book, author suggests to divide Mongolian history into 3 main periods, based on the recent results and achievements of studies related to the history of Central Asian ancient nomadic cultures. In addition, he mentioned that there are unclear problems in the Xiongnu history. He claims that “The strictest attitude towards some issues on Xiongnu origin will make the issue more complicated”. (Ch.A.)
“XIONGNU IN CHINA”. The author L.N.Gumilev. “Хунну в Китае”. The book entitled “The Xiongnu in China” (Three century-history of China who fought against the people in the steppe. 3-4th c. CE) is aimed to study the complicated periods in history of Chinese and Asian nomadic people. He emphasized the historic role of Nomadic people fighting Chinese expansions and same time their role in the formation of ethnic puzzle in North China. The book structurally has introduction, 11 chapters, 11 parts and comparative table. The introduction comprises 6 parts: Steppe and China; Rivers; Water; History, Historical trip and Collapse of Ancient China, whereas the first chapter, under the name “A Smoke Smoldering”, has some parts: Issues on Relationship between the Nations; The Eastern Xiongnu in Skirt of Tibet, Xianbei, Yuwen, Duan, Korea, Fuyu, Near the Amur Rivers and Siberia. The next chapter is named as “Open Fire” and tells the some historical events orderly under the headlines The Collapse of a Reign; The Collapse of Solidarity; The collapse of Democracy; Who Won; Parents and Children. The fourth chapter “Fire” is subdivided into the following parts: The Significance of Metaphor; Such Events Happened; Whirlwind; Opposite of The Wind; Typhoon; The Issues to Be Thought. The next chapters are named as Reddening (the situation became more acute); Unfrozen and open water; Three Colors of Flames; The Red Horizon;



X
208
The Extinguished Fire; The Ember Died; The Ash and tell about historical facts, his views and results on some historical events in detail. (Ch.A.)
“XIONGNU IN TRANSBAIKALIA”. Author P.B.Konovalov. “Хунну в Забайкалье” published in Ulan-Ude, 1976. This monograph comprises one introduction and five chapters in 220 pages, not including pictures in Appendix. The author wrote the monograph on the basis of the findings of the grave which he excavated by himself and other findings found from Xiongnu graves and cities in Russia and Mongolia. The result of his field research work for many years helped to synthesize history of Xiongnu and archeological research and to make a comment on other researchers’ works. He described some newly excavated graves and systematized some printed and unprinted materials in comparison with each other. And he wrote about the Xiongnu burial ceremony based on some archeological findings as a complete chapter. His research brought him a success because he made more scientific observation on the structure, organization of Xiongnu graves and funeral customs than previous researchers. He classified the findings from the graves and made clear their change and development. (Na.B.)
XIONGNU LIVESTOCK. Animal bones were found in most of the Xiongnu graves. Recently Mongolian archaeologists in cooperation with foreign experts realized several valuable researches on the study of Xiongnu domestic animals from excavation of the Xiongnu graves and settlements with the support of multidisciplinary scientific methods. Especially the projects implemented in cooperation with French archaeo-zoologists must be mentioned. French archaeo-zoologist H.Martin conducted a research for animal bones found in the Xiongnu graves of the Gol Mod and Egyin Gol sites with the latest methodology. As to see from the results of her research, the height of 16 horses from the tomb 20 at “Gol Mod” was 1.44 m on average, which was even taller than Prjewalsky’s wild horse which was 1.32 meters on average. The fact that the horses which belong to Gol Mod had “p1” or “wolf canine” teeth in its jaw, are quite archaic which are common in present day Mongolian horses. Domestic animal bones from Xiongnu common people graves of Egyin Gol site excavated by Mongolian-French joint expedition between 1995-1999, were analyzed by French palaeozoologist S.Lepets. Below the morphology of
Xiongnu cow, sheep, goat and horse features will be issued based on his analysis. The height of cow crest of Egyin Gol site was 1.14-1.37 meters (1.25 m on average). The reason for high difference of the cow height with 20% was due to sex, and the group composition consisting of domestic cows, yaks, and yak-cow hybrid. It is unique that the forehead width was very different as to see from the 47 measurements made into bones of 16 cows whose bones were possible to be measured. The cow forehead from some graves was wide enough whereas others were wide from other graves. An interesting conclusion could come regarding with a theory as the process of domesticating cows was improved, their forehead bones would have been widened. The fact that a yak was included in ox group could give an opportunity to explain the measurement difference. In addition, ox, yak, and ox-yak hybrid played major role in the group composition. Some complete sheep skeletons found from Egyin Gol site, in which comprehensive measurements were available to be made. Average height of the sheep is 66.8 cm, the shortest one’s height was 64 cm, and the tallest one was 69 cm. Although some sheep shin bone was relatively long and some sheep tiptoe bone was shorter, the difference was not more than 8 percent. In other words, the sheep of Egyin Gol site river had much homogeny morphology. Horse frame bones were found from 8 graves of Egyin Gol site. The height of these horses was 1.28-1.47 meters. The height difference was 15%, however, the height of most horses was not more than 1.35 m. (Ts.T.)
XIONGNU PALAEOANTHROPOLOGICAL RESEARCH. The beginning of palaeoanthropological research was commenced by a scientist, Yu.D.Talko-Grintsevich in 1898 with researching palaeoanthropological finding from 33 graves of the Xiongnu excavated in Ilmovaya Pad’, Transbaikalia. During 1930s, Russian anthropologist, G.F.Debets determined that the Xiongnu had later Mongolian look based on the findings of Transbaikalia. In 1965 Czech anthropologist, E.Vlchik researched and measured the anthropological findings found from the Xiongnu grave excavated in Noyon uul and referred them as northern Chinese. Hungarian anthropologist, T.Toth identified that they had similar look with Wusun, based on the findings of Naimaa Tolgoy, Arkhangai province. Since 1970s, Mongolian anthropologists, D.Tumen, L.Bilegt determined them as having



X
209
Mongolian look by conducting osteological and craniological researches based on the palaeoanthropological materials of the Xiongnu revealed by an archaeological research conducted by Mongolian-Soviet archaeologists in various regions of Mongolia. With democratic changes in Mongolia from 1990, Mongolian scholars begin to cooperate with some western countries in all disciplines so that it brought productivity to science field and created an opportunity for scientists to work on palaeoanthropological materials in high technological laboratories. For instance, between 1992-2000, Selenge expedition of the Academy of Sciences, Mongol-French joint archaeological expedition for ancient history research, Mongolian-American joint archaeological expedition “Northern Mongolia”, and MongolianFrench joint archaeological expedition “Stone age studies” worked in Egyin Gol river, KhutagUndur soum, Bulgan province. They collected and accumulated rich anthropological and livestock bones and created large collection which is valued as considerable in terms of Mongolian archaeology. Between 2003-2008, Mongolian-American joint expedition excavated and researched 32 graves of the Xiongnu in Baga Gazriin Chuluu, Delgertsogt soum, Dund-Gobi province, and conducted study of osteology, craniology and patalogy on human bones found from the above graves, which gives significant fact and material to determine origination of the Xiongnu existed in the region of Gobi. Mongol-Korean joint steppe expedition team identified people of East Asia are similar to American Indians by analyzing DNA finding found from an elite tomb of the Xiongnu located in Bayan-Adarga soum, Khentii province. B.Naran conducted paleopatological study on findings from the Xiongnu grave located in Egyin Gol river8 and and Ts.Amgalantugs conducted nonmetric study of this collection. M.Erdene, lecturer of the Mongolian National University conducted nonmetric study of 14 skulls of the Xiongnu found from eastern, western, and central Mongolia with 23 indicators and compared the result with materials from Siberia, Pacific Ocean, and the Far East. (Ts.A.)
XIONGNU ROCK ART. Researches on rock arts over Mongolia have recently been performed successfully and lots of new rock arts have been found and determined their chronological issues. Unfortunately, in spite of few rock paintings, the
Xiongnu rock arts could not have been identified thoroughly. For the sites of the Xiongnu rock arts, the following sites were identified: graved images of 2 chariots of Yamaan Us, Altai sum of Khovd province, people holding spears of Tsagaan Gol, Khaliun sum of Gobi-Altai province, people and livestock of Tsagaan Eleg of Tuvshinshiree sum of Sukhbaatar province, curlicue shape like horn in a square frame, python with checkered flake, jumping tigers with folded tails of Khakhuuliin Gol, Nomgon sum of Umnu-Gobi province, and carriage of Del Uul, Olziit sum, Dundgobi province. Also researchers suggested that rock arts at Yinshan mountain in Inner Mongolia, at Argo Mountain located along the Yenisei river, and some rock arts in Tuva, at Khakasian Minussink lowland are related to the Xiongnu period (3 c. BCE- 2 c. CE). In some tombs and graves of the Xiongnu have delivered scratched drawings.
