Few line drawing objects such as fragments of a bone utensil with animal description found in a grave at Morin tolgoi, Tuv province, a birch bark object with chariot image found in a Xiongnu grave at Burkhan tolgoi in Egyin Gol, Bulgan province, bow attachment piece with images of a man, horse and wild goat found in a grave at Baga Gazriin Chuluu, Dundugovi province are significant comparative materials to determine the chronological and cultural relevance of rock art sites. The image of an enclosed chariot on a lid of the birch-bark utensil found in grave 79 at Burkhan tolgoi demonstrated that the chariot images at Del Uul mountain and Yamaan Us are related to the Xiongnu period. Doctor Ts.Turbat made specifical article on this finding. Professor D.Tseveendorj had noticed that the image of a hunter with loaded bow in front of a wild sheep chased by a dog, which was depicted by sharp pointed object on the rock at Tsagaan Salaa valley, Ulaankhus sum, Bayan-Ulgii province, could be dated back to the Xiongnu period. After his analyses depiction method of this wild sheep is very similar with a depiction on bone object found in Xiongnu tomb 21 at Morin Tolgoi site and the hat worn in hunter of the image is similar to a fabric hat with pointed top found in elite tomb at Noyon Uul, and his bow is alike with the composite bow of the Xiongnu, which were give him the basis to determinate the chronology. Some horse depiction among rock arts can be related to the Xiongnu period by similarities with images of chariots and horses at Yamaan Us. This proved by findings of gilded bronze decoration with animal depiction found in Ilimovaya Pad’,



X
210
and in tomb 2 at Duurlig Nars in Bayan-Adarga soum of Khentiy province. Depiction of a deer running away from a beast on carpet found in tomb 2 at Noyon Uul, a bronze buckle with depiction of fighting two horses found in a grave of Darkhan, embossed silver decoration with image of a deer with horn found in elite tomb at Gol Mod of Arkhangai province, demonstrates that how the Xiongnu people used to illustrate animal movements. Therefore we consider that the special characteristics of Xiongnu rock art center around realistic depiction and the rendering of motion. For example, running animals are depicted with one front leg bent back, while the other is thrust forward. Animals’ bodies tend to be long and thin, with sagging stomachs. According to this consideration it is possible to relate the depiction of people on horses back pursuing wolves and driving herd of horses among rock arts of Songint of Bayankhongor province to the Xiongnu period. This proves by that this image illustrated on top of depiction of people on backs of a deer, horse which related to Early Iron age. A wild goat and horse image on a bow attachment found in a Xiongnu grave of Baga gazriin chuluu site and wild goat image on bow attachment found in grave at Kok Pash in Russian Altai (3-4th c. CE) display the wild goat and other animals illustrating the method of the Xiongnu rock art. It can be considered that the drawings of animals with thin and long body and with very thin stomach are common type of the Xiongnu art of Mongolia, Mountainous Altai of Tuva of Russia. Scholars correctively considered that the drawings with such type among the rock arts of Tsagaan Eleg are of the Xiongnu period as well as rock arts of animals and wild goats facing each other at Del Uul of Dundgovi province. The image of a wild goat looking back which is among the rock arts at Yamaan us, is similar by illustrated method with bronze decoration with image of a yak and wild goat found in elite tomb at Noyon uul. A big wild goat standing next to this wild goat is depicted by same method with animal depiction on a small wooden object found in a burial at Tepsey mountain of Tashtyk period (3-4th c. CE) in Tuva and this demonstrates that it is possible to relate them to Xiongnu period. Small animal’s ankle bones with signs and tamga are very interesting, depicted by sharp pointed stuff found in satellite burial 3 of tomb No.1 at Gol Mod-II cemetery of Undur-Ulaan soum in Arkhangai province. Comparing these signs with rock arts it is critical to relate all these signs and tamga to the Xiongnu but the composite
tamga on the first anklebone making a possibility to determine direct televance of these to Xiongnu period. For example, some composite tamga among rock arts of Yamaan Us and Tsagaan gol are very similar to tamga on this particular anklebone. These tamga on anklebones have been found on bottoms of potteries and scholars considered that this is a concrete example that among the Xiongnu people was a tradition to leave their family signs or tamga on their own properties. Above mentioned tamga show the probability of use of composite tamga by Xiongnu creating from retained tamga from previous generation with crotch, hook, sun and hack design. There is a composite tamga depicted near the Xiongnu chariot image at Yamaan Us and we cannot negate that this possible to be a tribal tamga of who depicted this sign. The figures on the bronze buckles of belts seem particularly similar to much of the rock art found in Mongolia. The belt buckles presenting deer, camels, cows, horses, mountain goats, wolves, dogs and cats. The animals tend to be depicted in pairs of individuals facing each other. This type of symmetrical depiction has been seen as characteristic of early Iron Age and the Xiongnu period. It is also found in early Iron Age belt buckles and other decorations that have been designated as “Scythian-Siberian Animal Style”, and have come from Xiongnu period burials in Mongolia, Ordos, Siberia and Xinjiang. In the realm of rock art, such symmetrical depictions have been found: at Baga Oigor and Tsagaan Salaa; pairs of mountain goats and horses, Bayan-Ulgii province; at Tevsh Mountain in Urvurkhangai province, and at Khakhuulyn Gol in Umnugovi province; paired mountain goats at Del Uul of Dundgovi province. Scientists suggested that depiction of two mountain sheeps looked to each other which prepared for fight are probably same with symbols that occur in ancient myths mentioned about agriculturist people who ask for rain and full harvest. This imagination is influenced to Central Asian nomads by immigration of later IndoEuropean people and probably this is the evidence to identify the meanings of the symmetrical depictions. In the period of ancient nomads or end of Bronze Age, Early Iron Age and Xiongnu period the animal figures are depicted skillfully and artistically on objects made by animal style art method but these kinds of depictions are rare in rock arts. This can be explained as painter normally depicting the image only on a flat surface but a person who is carving or casting is not limited by



X
211
flat surface and has more possibilities to express his artistic imaginations. The influences of animal style art of Bronze Age to the Xiongnu culture are clearly visible and we cannot ignore that the Xiongnu people has deeply influenced to cultures of neighboring regions. Therefore it is complicated to identify chronology of the Xiongnu rock arts differentiating from Early Iron Age rock arts. But the bronze decorations found in Xiongnu tombs have differences from previous generation by its technique. For example other depictions, besides animal style art, usually shows more realistic image of wild animals or domestic animals in background of nature. Although Xiongnu rock art has kept elements of the ancient nomads’ animal style, it is, compared to them, more close to the reality and simplistic. (Na.B.)
XIONGNU ZHAO DYNASTY (319-329 CE). South Xiongnu noble man Liu Yao established in 319 this short living state, also known as Early Zhao. As an act of treachery a rebel Xiongnu jiehu tribe general Shi Le established his own state. Then Xiongnu prince Liu Yao moved the Xiongnu-Han capital from Pingyang to Chang’an. Liu Yao felt that it was time to end the linkage with Han and explicitly restore the linkage to the great Maodun Chanyu of Xiongnu, and therefore decided to change the name of the state. However, Liu Yao is considered to be a successor of Xiongnu-Han. Thus, some historians combine the study these two states and call them Han-Zhao state. Liu Yao was defeated by Shi Le in 329 and the Xiongnu dynasty was wiped out. However, the “Liu” Xiongnu remained active in the north of China for at least an another century. (Ts.T.)
XIUBU clan. One of the noble clans of Xiongnu and appeared little bit later than others. Later, Chanyu of Xiongnu got married from this clan and major problems of the state discussed within this clan. In other word, this clan had a role of judgment and other clans had own leader, warriors and can to do anything within the state law. (S.U.)
XIUTU wang. Name of Xiongnu title. According to the sources, land of Xiutu wang was Wuwei Jung and in ancient times Yuezhi and Wusun were lived in this land. (No.B.)
XIWEI. Ancient Mongolian speaking tribe. Xiwei mentioned first time in the Chinese historical narratives of 6th c. CE. The issue of origin and language of Xiwei still in debate. But in above mentioned record, it says that Xiwei was a branch
of Kidan and they had Mongolian language. In some written sources Xiwei and Tatar tribes were confused and did not made distinctions between them. Researchers think that the tribes called Xiwei in Chinese records are noted as Tatar in Turkic runic inscriptions. But some Xiwei could have had Mongolian language and some of them had Manchu-Tungus language. They were divided into many tribes. Out of these tribes, it is presumed that Meng-wu Xiwei got name of Mongol’s. Xiwei people were warriors living on husbandry, hunting and fishing. During Turkic Khanate, sometimes Xiwei submitted to them and sometimes fight against them. Their name was not recorded after 9th c. Many tribes of Xiwei got their own name and became known by it in the historical records. (Ts.Т.)
XUNGI Mountain. (Sungi or Chung zhi) Chinese linguist Cheng Zhongmian restored the name of this lake as Songa or Soɳa. According to the sources, Han emperor was assigned to Ponu general to reach to Xungi mountain, 2000 li to the north-west with Shofan 20000 cavalries in 103BCE. Also, in March of 90 BCE, historian Shanqu Cheng moved from Xihe with 20000 cavalries, fought with Xiongnu warriors near the Xungi mountain and killed numerous Xiongnu warriors. In September of second year of Tianhan, General Li Ling moved from Juyang to Xungi mountain with 5000 warriors for 30 days, made a land map of Xungi mountain and sent it to the Han palace. The aim was to block Xiongnu to send additional forces to Yuxian wang fighting Er Xi general at Qilian-Tianshan area. In Xungi mountain the Chanyu attacked Li Ling with his 80000 cavaliers and finally Li Ling was blocked in the mountain ravine and surrendered. Chinese historian Din Qian consider that Xyungi mountain is present day Arts Bogd mountain, but by some scholars view it is the Burged mountain which situates between Tuul and Orkhon river. According to the Chinese historical atlas the Xyungi mountain is the Gurvan Bodg mountain which includes Ikh Bogd, Baga Bogd and Arts Bogd mountains. L.A.Borovka said that there are two different Xungi, one is Karliktag in China and the another one is the Tianshan of Gobi or TostNoyon Uul in Mongolia. (B.B.)
XUNYU tribe. One of the tribes of Bai Di or White Di which settled in Yellow rivers` valley during the Warring states period (476-221 BCE). They were established Zhongshan state in the present day Hebei province. (S.U.)



Y
212



Y
213



Y
214



Y
215
Y
AMAAN US site. Xiongnu period rock art site. Located between Uyench and Altai soum territory in south of Mongol Altai mountain. Khanan had is the name of 30 m high rock facing to northwest in rocky Yamaan us gorge. There are many rock carvings tamgas and ancient inscriptions from different periods including Bronze age, Xiongnu and Ancient Turkic period. Archaeologists of the team studying rock carvings and petroglyphs of Mongolian-Soviet historical joint expedition discovered this site in 1973. Well crafted rock carvings illustrating profile depicted two chariots are the most unique images of this site. Based on comparative study of paintings of Han period, researcher date this paintings back to Xiongnu period. There were several images of Xiongnu tamga around this painting. The first chariot carving illustrates the 8 nave wheel carrying a man, drawn by a horse and a rider in front of a carriage. In the second image, the carriage has wheels with 8 naves as well, but with a light vagon; there is one man in the carriage that is drawn by 3 horses. There are two horsemen carrying weapon riding in front of and behind the carriage as protection. From the image details, such as horse movement, cart, vagon, weaponry, it is clear that two paintings are skillfully executed. (No.B.)
YAN Dynasty. Early Yan state (337-370), West Yan (384-394), Late Yan (383-409), South Yan (398410) were four states founded by ancient mongol speaking nomadic Murun tribe originated from
Xianbei. Murun Huang and his son Murun Jung of Early Yan were granted king of Yan state from Qin dynasty and they called their states Yan. In 352 Murun Jung conquered the land of Late Zhao and declared himself a king. In 370 during reign of king
Murun Bei (360-370 он), he was killed by late Qin, originated from Di. Murun Hong, a brother of late king Murun Wei founded West Yan state in 384. Extinction of Later Qin state in 384 created opportunity for elits of Murun to revive their state. However, 7 kings reined the state during 10 year period; they were all assassinated during never-ending uprising. In 394, Yan was destroyed during its unification by the king Murun Chui of Later Yan state. Murun Chui, one of the sons of Murun Huang founded Later Yan state. It was an effort to revive Murun, but during 26 years 7 different kings were reign the state, which was destroyed by its internal uprising. Murun De (398-405), another son of Murun Huang founded South Yan in territory of modern Shandong, which was destroyed in 410 by the army of Qin. This way statehood of Murun, originated from ancient Mongolian nomads was destroyed.
(Ts.Т.)
YANJI Mountain. A mountain range of Nanshan Mountain, Gansu tribe, located to south east of Chilyan Mountain. It is Shandan Shan mountain of south east of Jan E city, on the effluent of the river of Ma Zhanshou lord. Xiongnu women used saffron pain to put make up and in 121when Yanchji Mountain was lost to Han, they sang the women lack powder to put make up. See the notes on Zhilyan Mountain (B.B.)
YAN ZHANG mountain of SU XIE. Most of scholars said that Yan Zhang mountain is the Khangai range. In 90 BCE, Han general Er Xi (Li Guangli) entered to Xiongnu land with his
Petroglyphs of Yamaan Us



Y
216
70 000 warriors from Wuyuan fought with Wei Lui warriors near the Gou Shan mountain of Wu Yang, and passed through Zhiju river to fight Xiongnu, but back to his land for officers` disturbance and when reached Yan Zhang mountain Xiongnu Chanyu came to fight him to this mountain with 50 000 warriors. Later, there were carved an inscription by Dou Xian and Geng Bing generals for the shattering the Northern Xiongnu. (B.B.)
YANZHI. The official title of the spouse of Xiongnu Chanyu. The word has two pronunciations: “yanchji” and “echji”. There are different opinions on the origin of this word among national and international scholars, but some researchers think the meaning and pronunciation of this word is a mongol word “esi”. It means queen and was used until Middle ages, followed by replacement by “khatan” meaning queen in the time of Rouran khanate. (No.B.)
YEROOL-ERDENE, Chimeddorj. Mongolian archaeologist and Xiongnu researcher. Born in 1975 in Ulaanbaatar. In 1998 graduated Ulaanbaatar University in major of archaeology. In 2000 defended MA degree and in 2010 defended his Ph.D. dissertation on “Xiongnu elite funeral monuments study”. In 1998-2000 worked for the Mongolian Army Museum, from 2000 working for the Institute of Archaeology as research fellow and then from 2010 as the Chef of Xiongnu Study Department of the Institute of Archaeology of the Mongolian Academy of Sciences. His main research interests focused on the Xiongnu funeral monuments, especially the elite burials of Xiongnu. He is the author and coauthor of dozen of books, including “Mongolie : Le premier empire des steppes” (2003), “Archaeology Science of Mongolia” (2007), “Xiongnu of the Arkhangai aimag” (2007), “National Atlas of Mongolia” (2009) and more than 30 scientific articles on Xiongnu study. His works were published in France, Germany, Korea, Turkey and Kazakhstan. His work on the study of Xiongnu elite burials of Mongolia is based on his research of Gol Mod elite burials and was the first significant attempt to study this distinct monument in extensive way. Main results of his study on the spatial and constructional features, funeral and ritual customs of Xiongnu elite burials and material culture of the elite burials, including profound research on some
imported objects highly contributed in the development of Xiongnu study in Mongolia. Since 2000, Ch.Yerool-Erdene participated in several projects on Xiongnu archaeology including Mongol-French joint project “Nomadic Empire” in Gol Mod, MongolAmerican joint project “Northern Mongolia” in Egyin Gol, and since 2003 directing MongolFrench joint project “Nomadic Empire” and since 2006 directing Mongol-Monako joint project in Tamir river valley. (Ts.T.)
YINSHAN Mountain. Mountain mentioned in relation with Xiongnu archaeology located in Inner Mongoilia, China. G.Sukhbaatar wrote that the Mongolian name of the Yinshan mountain is Gazal Uul. According to the Hou Han Shu, Yinshan mountain is 400 km in length, had many wild animals and forest, and it was the hunting place of Maodun Chanyu. Also the historical records deliver the information that there was a small settlement called Touman cheng at the northern slope of this mountain. There is no fixed information on since when the Xiongnu people started to inhabit the Yinshan mountain area. Certainly an ancient tribe Linhu resided in the south slope of the mountain, in the vicinity of current Hohhot city since 4th century BCE. In the 214 BCE the Xiongnu people were chased off 240 km northwest from the mountain by Meng Tian, commander of the Qin army. After the collapse of the Qin dynasty and during rise of the Han dynasty Maodun chanyu managed to take peacefully back the mountain. Later in 127 BCE the Xiongnu people again lost the mountain during the Wei Qing invasion. With the exception of 53-43 BCE the Xiongnu people had not resided until 50 CE in that mountain. (B.B.)
YONKHOR Settlement. Xiongnu period settlement in Buriatia. Located in the opposite river bank of Bayan-Undur settlement covering 350 m long bank on the 35-40 m high terrace. In 10 km to the south west from Derestyu cemetery. Thickness of the cultural level measured around 20-80 cm. heavily damaged by modern human activities. On the terrace edges and berms found Xiongnu typical potteries, stone corn pestles and iron plowshares. The site was the permanent settlement of agricultures’. (Ts.T.)
YUEBAN. Tribal state of 2-4th c. CE originated from Xiongnu. Inhabited in modern East Kazakstan and Kyrgystan. They are called also Little Xiongnu to distinguish them from



Y
217
Great Xiongnu that migrated to Europe. Some researchers call it Chuban following its original pronunciation. In Chinese records they are called Chuyue, i.e. moon worshipers. According to L.N.Gumilev they are descendants of Xiongnu after its extinction. They were mixed with Dinling and Sogdians to create new ethnic group. Record says they were fighting with Wusun to control land of Semirechiye. In 423 Yueban king encountered Rouran people whose clothes were not cleaned and women licked their utensils on his way to pay respect to Tatar khan of Rouran Empire, then he got angry to his followers for leading him to this dog country and went back. However, he could not catch up with Tatar khan, thus these two states started to be spiteful towards each other. Envoy was sent from Yueban to South Wei state to make proposal to join forces against Rouran in 429 and 448. In 448 they were defeated by Oguz and Rouran and lost its statehood. Since then the remaining of Yueban joined Oguz tribal confederation. The main triba Chuyue joined with Kok Turks and was well-known as Shato
Turks. (Ts.Т.)
YUFULUO CHANYU (150-196 CE). Chanyu of South Xiongnu. Puppet Chanyu appointed from East Han dynasty. His father Qiangqui Chanyu was also a puppet Chanyu and was killed in the hand of his own men. People of south Xiongnu did not recognize Yufuluo Chanyu, and according to the ancient tradition a noble called Huyubi from East wing was appointed to Chanyu. Thus Yufuluo hid himself in Luoyan, capital city of East Han. Yufuluo asked for military support from Han several times to fight against Xiongnu army, but did not succeed, and then he started to rob Chinese villages joining remaining men from Yellow Turban Rebellion. In 189 CE there was big uprising in Han. Many who took part in uprising joined Yufuluo Chanyu and carried out robbery. Died in 196 CE. His men from Xiongnu joined Huchuquan Chanyu. His grandson is Lui Yuan founded Han-Zhao dynasty of South Xiongnu. (Ts.T.)
YUN Hyeong-Won. South Korean archaeologist. Specialized in archaeological studies of Silla, which was one of the Three Kingdoms of Korea, and the Xiongnu Empire. Born on October 10, 1966, in the city of Daegu and in 1986-1990 he received bachelor’s degree in archaeology from the department of Archaeology and Ancient Art of Seoul National University. In 1999, he defended master’s degree at the Seoul National University. Since 1992 he has been
working as a researcher, senior researcher and head of a branch museum of National Museum of Korea. Yun Hyeong-won’s main study area is archaeological sites, especially graves and cemeteries, from the transition period into Common Era of Northeastern Asia and he published many works in this field. Since 2000 he has started his research on Xiongnu burials in Mongolia and conducted several joint studies with Mongolian archaeologist at Morin Tolgoi, Khudgiin Tolgoi and Duurlig Nars and became a co-author of excavation reports such as “Xiongnu burial at Morin Tolgoi”, “Xiongnu burial at Khudgiin Tolgoi”s and “Xiongnu burial at Duurlig Nars”. Moreover he published scientific articles on the Xiongnu study to include “Excavation of Xiongnu Tumuli at Morin Tolgoi in Mongolia” (Korean Archaeology Society, 2000), “Excavation of Xiongnu Tumuli at Hudgiin Tolgoi in Mongolia” (National Museum of Korea, 2001), “Regtangular Tomb in Mongolia” (35th Academic Symposium-Korean Ancient History Society, 2004), “Comparing research the tomb structure between Silla and Xiongnu” (East Asian Archaeology Society, 2004). (G.E.)
YUWU River (Suyu, Ungu). The name of this river mentioned for the first time in historical records in 121 BCE. “Foals were born on the Valley of Yuwu River” according to Han shu. In 97 BCE, Han great army with 240 thousand men including, 70 thousand horsemen was sent against Xiongnu in three directions. General Lu Guangli took off from Shoufang (near Xiliangqun city on the south coast of modern Yellow river) with 60 thousand horsemen and 100 thousand infantry. Aware of the strategy of Han, Chanyu moved his people and livestock to the north of Yuwu River and was waiting for Li Guangli himself with his 100 thousand horsemen on the south coast of Yuwu river. After 10 days of battle, Lu Guangli surrendered following execution of his family members. Only one or two out of a thousand soldiers of his army returned to Han safely. Also when in 90 BCE, Han great army attacked Xiongnu, the East Tuqi wang moved his people from their land to the north 600-700 li (240-280 km) crossing Yuwu River and joined a battle by the Zhiju River wait to help Chanyu. Huyangdi Chanyu of Xiongnu kept 9 thousand horsemen in 80 BCE in case of sudden Han army attack by Shouxiancheng fortress built in 104 BC (225 km to west of Huhhot, Urad banner land) and constructed



Z
218
bridge over Yuwu river in case they were to escape. In 72 BCE Han general Tian Shun with his 30 thousand horsemen were sent Wuyuan (near modern city Bugat) against Xiongnu. They made 800 li (320 km) and reached north coast of Yuwu River. From this some researchers suggests that the Yuwu River is the river that runs through modern Inner Mongolian territory. De Groot suggested the restoration of the name of this river as Urga, but some researchers claim Yuwu is the Wenjihe River (Ongi River) or some say it is the Tuul River. (B.B.)
YUWEN tribe. Nomadic tribal state originated from ancient Xianbei. In some records, it says they had mixed origin of Xiongnu and Xianbei. They were the biggest nomadic tribal state among Xianbei of modern Liaoning region. Since 260, Yuwen Mohuai was their leader, as noted in historical records. Their position was strengthened by marriage agreement with Touba during the end of the 3rd century. Yuwen fought against Murun Mogui in 302 and was defeated in 325. They were conquered by Murun Huang in 345. Descendant from Yuwen tribal state, Yuwen Tai founded North
Zhou state in 6th century. (Ts.Т.)



Z
219
Z
AMYN TOLGOI tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. Located in Battsengel soum of Arkhangai aimag, in 1 km to South-East of Tsats commune. The tombs at Zamyn Tolgoi hill is spread on the south slope of the hill, has a small stream in the south and a road to commune center in the north of it. About 60 tombs are identified here. The northernmost tomb is the biggest one in the cemetery and unique features as rectangular stone mound, existence of a south side stone path shaped entrance. The others has very common circular shape stone mound of Xiongnu common people grave. Tombs located in the east part of the cemetery are distinguish from others by relatively big size of stone mound and absence of depressions at the center of mound. The western sector smaller tombs are grouped by several tombs. Zamyn Tolgoi cemetery was found and identified by Mongol-Korean Mon-Sol project field team in 2001. No excavation held until now at this cemetery. (G.E.)
ZARAA TOLGOI tombs. Xiongnu period cemetery in Mongolia. Located in Bayan soum, Tuv aimag. Tombs of different periods are spread over rocky hill named Zaraa Tolgoi, in 12 km to south of Bayan soum center. In south and west slopes of the hill identified 8 slab graves, 2 kereksurs, 2 Xiongnu
tombs and in south-east slope are 9 Xiongnu tombs. Moreover 8 Xiongnu tombs are discovered in 250 m to the South-East of the Zaraa Tolgoi hill. This site was discovered in 1988 by Mongol-Soviet joint Xiongnu study expedition and 6 tombs were already studied by then. (G.E.)
ZHANJIANG WANG. New title appeared during split of Xiongnu into South and North Xiongnu in 48 CE. During that time there were West and East Zhanjiang wang in South Xiongnu. Close relatives of Chanyu were appointed to this position. (No.B.)
ZHAO XIN CHEN city. In the 4th year of Yuanshou, of the Wu Di of Han dynasty, great general Wei Qing departed from Dingxian (present day Huhhot) for thousand li (app. 400 km) saw army of Chanyu lined and was waiting for them. Wei Qing attacked with 5 thousand horsemen, and then Xiongnu counterattacked with several thousand horsemen. During sunset, Han took advantage of strong wind and encircled Chanyu from east and west side. Chanyu took few hundred best soldiers and run towards north west, after realizing Han outnumbering cavalry were full of energy. Two sides were close combat throughout night and Wei Qing chased after Chanyu once he learnt from a captured Xiongnu soldier. By sun rise Han cavalry made trail nearly 200 li (80 km), but did not catch Chanyu, however they captured several thousand Xiongnu soldiers and consumed from grain conserved for Xiongnu army at Zhao Xin cheng in the foot of Tianyanshan. After whole day rest Wei Qing army burnt the remaining grain and took off back to south. On the way back his Deputy Generals met Wei Qing when they were lost during crossing of Gobi. Based on the records about Zhao Xin cheng city, researchers define this city as located in the modern central Mongolian territory. But some Mongolian researchers conclude that Bayanbulag ruin in the foot of Khurkh Mountain range, Umnugobi aimag could be the ancient Zhao Xin cheng city. (B.B.)
Plan of the Zamyn tolgoi site



Z
220
ZHAO XIN (2nd c. BCE). Military general of Xiongnu and Han empire. He was a Xiongnu noble man, but submitted to Han state. His Xiongnu name is not clear, that he took name Zhao Xin when he was in Han. In 123 BCE he was one of the 6 generals of the army of Wei Qin. After his encounter with Xiongnu army, Zhao Xin immediately surrendered and went back to Xiongnu. Afterwards a frontier fortress city built in Tianyan mountain was named after him. In 199 BCE Wei Qing army destroyed and burnt this city. Mongolian researchers think that Bayanbulag ruin in Khurkh Mountain range, in Umnugobi aimag should be the ancient Zhao Xin city. (Ts.Т.)
ZHIJU River. River that run behind the city Zhao Xin according to records are thought to be modern Selenge River. In 119 BCE Han general Wei Qing took off from Dinxiang (modern day Huhhot) with 50 thousand horsemen and several hundred thousand infantry. After crossing of Gobi, making 1000 li (400 km) they reached Xiongnu fortress and faced Chanyu himself, who already prepared to battle. After sunset, Han took advantage of strong wind and encircled Chanyu from east and west side. Chanyu took few hundred best soldiers and run towards north west, after realizing Han outnumbering cavalry were full of energy. By sun rise Han cavalry made the trail nearly 200 li (80 km), but did not catch Chanyu, however they captured several thousand Xiongnu soldiers and consumed from grain conserved for Xiongnu army at Zhao Xin cheng. After whole day rest Wei Qing army burnt the remaining grain and took off back to south. Based on the records about Zhiju river run north of Gobi and could have dried out. (B.B.)
ZHIZHI CHANYU (?- 36 BCE). Xiongnu Chanyu. Personal name is Luandi Hutuwusi. After death of Wuyanqudi Chanyu in 60 BCE, Hutuwusi and his brothers Jihunshan, Huhanye Chanyu competed for the throne and in 56 BCE Hutuwusi defeated his opponent and became Zhizhi Chanyu. In 54 BCE Zhizhi Chanyu governed entire steppe land, so Huhanye run to Han state and submitted to them. With military support from Han, Huhanye defeated Zhizhi. Zhizhi took 3000 soldiers to west and joined Kangju people. Together with Kangju, he fought successfully with Wusun, the ally of Han, and founded fortress city at Talas River in modern Kyrgystan. He was defeated and killed by Han
army in 36 BC. (Ts.Т.)
ZHO-E SHAN mountain. Also called Zhotu or Tokto. Scholars note that this mountain was
mentioned in historical records of Xiongnu period, Rouran and Turkic periods. In 99 CE during Xiongnu-Han war, Han general Gongsun Ao with archers from Xihe region reached Zho-e Shan Mountain, but did not find any gain. In 90 CE general commanding soldier Shanqiu Chen reached Zhui-E Jing, but did not encounter anybody, so went back. Then Xiongnu sent great generals and Li Lin with 30 thousand soldiers to fight down Han army. They caught Han army be Xionggi mountain and fought for nine days. North Xiongnu army passed Zho-E shan mountain to counter attack Han and South Xiongnu army in 73, 89, 90 CE. Also army of Touba Wei of late Xianbei-Tabgach tribal state passed this mountain on their way to attack Rouran (Nirun) in 391, 438, 449. Chinese historian Chen Zhongmian thought Zho-E shan and Zhui-E Jing were the same mountain and also Zuncai mountain mentioned in the history records of Turkic period and Chugai mountain mentioned in Turkic-Runic inscriptions are all the same mountain. F. Hirt, B.Tomsen, Han Ruling, Chen Zhongmian and A.G. Malyavkin follow the spelling «Cughai, Cugai» of this name, Zho-E. B.Tomsen said that Zho-E is one part of Khangai mountain range, but if it says it is in Han Ruling Yingshan, then Chen Zhongmian should look for it in south of Gobi. A.G.Malyavkin thinks that Zho-E shan, i.e. Chugai is without doubt Gobi Altai range, namely Ikh Bogd, one of the main parts of Altai Mountain. But Lin Gan wrote that Zho-E shan mountain is around 1000 km to west from old Gaoche fortress, in modern Mandalgobi, Mongolia, but L.A.Borovkova disagree with this and says that it is in west end of Yingshan mountain. (B.B.)
ZHONGHANG YUE. Laoshang Chanyu state advisor of Chinese origine. In 174 Giyu, son of Modun was to take his father’s throne and became Laoshang Chanyu. Han court following “Peace and Marriage Treaty” sent to Xiongnu with a princess and large amount of present, and some service men, including a eunuch Zhonghang Yue. Zhonghang Yue submitted fully to Chanyu and became his close advisors. From Chinese records it says that after seeing that Xiongnu people were getting used to silk, textile and food products from China, he advised that if Han state send only one tenth of their domestic production to Xiongnu, everybody in Xiongnu would be dependent on Han. It is important to show once silk and textile arrive from China that they are not as durable as clothes made from leather and wool, by dragging them over bush and rough terrain. Also show preference to diary product and meat and not getting food from China. (Ch.Е.)



221



222
HISTORICAL PERSONALITIES
AFUJILO (492-496). (Ts.T.) 5 ANAHUAN (520 or 522-552. (Ts.T.) 8 ATTILA (?-453 CE). (Ts.T.) 18 BALAMIR (in 360-378). (Ts.T.) 20 BAN CHAO (32-102 CE). (Ts.T.) 20 BAN GU (32-92 CE). (Ch.Ye.) 20 BUMAN (died in 552). (Ts.T.) 41 BUDUGEN. (Ts.T.) 41 CAI WENJI. (Ch.Ye.) 49 CHEDIHOU CHANYU (101-97 BCE). (Ts.T.) 55 CHOUNU Khan (508-520 CE). (Ts.T.) 56 DATANG Khan (414-429).
